INTRODUCTION

T3 Trading Group, LLC. ("T3TG”, “T3” or the “Firm”) conducts its business consistent with the highest
standards of commercial honor and just and equitable principles of trade. Keeping our members and
customer/members' interest foremost is key to T3TG’s success. The trust of our customer/members and
T3TG’s reputation are of paramount importance. Effective supervision is an integral part of achieving our
goals in serving our customer/members.

"Compliance" is not a static event; it is a process which evolves in tandem with regulations that govern
our industry and the circumstances of each particular interaction. This manual includes T3TG’s
supervisory policies and procedures to provide guidance to designated supervisors in their oversight of
the Firm's business. It is a living document and reference for supervisors and will be updated when
necessary.

It is recognized that supervision must be a flexible tool for use by those charged with managing the Firm's
various activities. While it is generally expected these procedures will be followed, supervisors are
encouraged to adapt these procedures to the needs of T3TG, their particular department, and the
associates and customer/members of T3TG. These procedures are meant to be a basic framework upon
which supervisors oversee the Firm's activities.

This manual does not attempt to set forth all of the rules and regulations with which associates must be
familiar, nor does it attempt to deal with all situations involving unusual circumstances. When questions
arise, refer them to the Compliance Department for assistance.

Supervision may be delegated to others, where appropriate; however, designated supervisors are
responsible for ultimate supervision of assigned areas. The term "Employee/Member", “Associated
Member” or “Associates” as used in this manual includes Registered Representatives (“RRs”) and
proprietary traders (and others as identified by T3TG).

This manual is the property of T3TG and may not be provided to anyone outside the Firm without the
permission of Compliance or the Firm's counsel.

 Standards of Conduct                                                                      09062023
2 GENERAL EMPLOYEE/MEMBER POLICIES

                                 2.1 Standards of Conduct
[FINRA Rule 2010]


It is T3TG’s policy and mandate to its associates to conduct T3TG’s business under the high standards and
principles of the rules governing our industry. Associates are expected to deal with customer/members
in a fair and honest way, with the customer/member's interest of primary concern.

Compliance will distribute compliance policies and procedures to all associates and from time to time will
issue updates and other notices, as needed.

                                 2.2 Outside Business Activities
 Responsibility          •   Designated Supervisors
                         •   Requests to engage in outside business activities
                         •   Annual certifications
 Resources
                         •   Other potential indicators such as incoming or outgoing correspondence

 Frequency               •   As required
                         •   Employee/Member's supervisor:
                                 o Question Employee/Member regarding potential unapproved
                                    outside business activities referenced in correspondence or other
                                    indicators of outside business activity (i.e., Form U-4, annual
                                    certification)
                         •   Compliance Department:
                                 o Consider whether the proposed activity will:
                                         Interfere       with   or    otherwise      compromise     the
                                            Employee/Member's responsibilities to T3TG and its
                                            customer/members
                                         Be viewed by customer/members or the public as part of
                                            T3TG 's business based upon the nature of the proposed
 Action
                                            activity and the manner in which it will be offered
                                 o Determine whether the activity is an outside business activity or if it
                                    would be considered a private securities transaction subject to the
                                    requirements of the next section
                                 o Discuss all investment-related requests with CCO and non-
                                    investment-related matters, as warranted.
                                 o Determine any limitations to be imposed prior to approval
                                 o Notify Employee/Member of approval/disapproval and any
                                    limitations, in writing




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 3
 Standards of Conduct                                                                          09062023
                         •   Retention for 3 years, 2 years in a readily accessible location:
                                o Outside       Business      Activity     Request      forms including
                                    approval/disapproval and any limitations on the activity
 Record
                                o Written notification to the Employee/Member and his/her
                                    supervisor


Associates are required to disclose to T3TG, in writing, any outside business activities and obtain approval
prior to engaging in such activity. Charitable activities are not included in this requirement unless the
Employee/Member is being compensated. This policy applies to all associates (or other relationships such
as RRs acting as independent contractors registered with T3TG; it does not apply to private securities
transactions which are discussed in the next section.

Outside business activities may include a wide range of activities including but not limited to the following:

     •    Employment with an outside entity
     •    Acting as an independent contractor to an outside party
     •    Serving as an officer, director, or partner
     •    Acting as a finder
     •    Referring someone and receiving a referral fee
     •    Receiving compensation or having the reasonable expectation of compensation from any other
          person as a result of a business activity outside the scope of employment or other relationship
          with T3TG

Compensation may include salary, stock options or warrants, referral fees, or providing of services or
products as remuneration. Generally, remuneration consisting of anything of present or future value for
services rendered may be considered compensation.

Associates requesting approval to engage in outside business activities must complete the Outside
Business Activity Request form and submit it Compliance prior to engaging in the activity. Compliance will
approve or disapprove the outside business activity in writing and notify the Employee/Member.

                                 2.3 Private Securities Transactions
[FINRA Rule 3280]


                         •   Director of Compliance / Or Designee
 Responsibility

Associates are permitted to engage in private securities transactions (as defined by FINRA) with
permission from compliance. This is done on a case-by-case basis. Private Securities transactions are
prohibited if there is compensation paid for effecting the transaction. Private securities transactions are
defined by FINRA as any securities transaction outside the regular course or scope of an
Employee/Member's employment with T3TG (sometimes referred to as "selling away"). This does not
include outside securities accounts approved by T3TG, transactions with immediate family members
where the Employee/Member receives no selling compensation, and personal transactions in Investment
Company and variable annuity securities.

RRs should note that promissory notes often are securities. Even if a promissory note is not deemed a
security, the RR has the obligation to obtain T3TG’s permission before engaging in any outside business
activity involving the offer of promissory notes.

RRs are required to conduct their selling activities through T3TG.

2.4.1 Employee/Member and Employee/Member Related Accounts Defined
Employee/Member accounts include any accounts where an Employee/Member has a personal financial
interest; the Employee/Member is the named trustee or custodian; or the Employee/Member otherwise
has control over the account. "Accounts" include securities or commodities accounts at T3TG or other
financial institutions, including foreign or domestic broker-dealers, investment advisers, banks and other
financial institutions.

Employee/Member.

2.4.6 Prohibition on Purchases of Initial Public Offerings (IPOs)
[FINRA Rule 5130]


Associates and their immediate families (parents, spouse, children, in-laws, and siblings) are prohibited
from purchasing IPOs.

2.4.7 Research Restrictions
T3TG does not develop, purchase or otherwise disseminate research to its clients. However, to the extent
T3TG elects to provide research in the future, Employee/Member accounts would be restricted from
acting on a new or changed research recommendation for 24 hours from the time of announcement or
publication. Restrictions would be announced internally, and trading activity is monitored for compliance.
Failure to comply would result in cancellation of the transaction, and any resulting loss will be charged to
the Employee/Member. It could also result in the Employee/Member being fined, suspended, and/or
terminated.

2.4.8 Restrictions on Personal Accounts of Certain Firm Personnel
As T3TG does not maintain personnel in certain departments, such as research, trading, and investment
banking, additional requirements or restrictions on the trading in their personal accounts have no
application. However, if the Firm developed these functions in the future, affected personnel will be
provided with any additional policies affecting their personal transactions.

http://www.finra.org/web/groups/rules_regs/documents/rules_regs/p018025.pdf; NASDAQ Rule 3060]



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                                        Page 9
 Gifts, Gratuities and Entertainment                                                                                       09062023
                         •   Designated Supervisors

                         •   Requests to give or receive gifts
                         •   Expense reports

                         •   RR's supervisor:
                                 o Refer requests regarding gifts and gratuities to Compliance

Gifts, gratuities, and entertainment are subject to regulators' limitations. Failure to comply may result in
fines or other regulatory actions against the Employee/Member and T3TG. It is important for all
associates to know and comply with this policy. Questions regarding this policy on gifts and
entertainment should be referred to Compliance. Key requirements include:

   •   Gifts to others are limited to $100 per year, per person (other than "personal gifts" defined in
       the policy).
           o Multiple gifts to the same person are aggregated, i.e., the total of all gifts in any year, firm-
               wide, cannot exceed $100 to one person.
           o "Personal gifts" are excluded (as discussed below).
   •   Receiving gifts is limited.
   •   An Employee/Member must host entertainment to avoid entertainment being considered a
       "gift" subject to limitations.



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 10
 Gifts, Gratuities and Entertainment                                                           09062023
   •   Records of entertainment must include details of who was entertained and the nature of
       entertainment.
   •   Gifts to labor union associates require prior Compliance approval.
   •   Gifts to public officials require prior Compliance approval.
   •   Your supervisor must be notified of gifts to others.
   •   Your supervisor must be notified of gifts received (other than personal gifts).
   •   Gift requirements (whether the gift is given or received) do not apply to personal gifts to
       immediate family members (parents, children, grandparents, siblings, spouse, in-laws) who also
       happen to be customer/members and where the gift is unrelated to T3TG’s business. The policy
       also does not apply to occasional personal gifts to others (such as a wedding gift or a
       congratulatory gift for the birth of a child).
   •   If T3TG pays for the gift or reimburses the Employee/Member for the cost of the gift, the gift is
       subject to the requirements of this policy.
   •   Gifts incidental to entertainment (e.g., a golf shirt given during a golf outing) are considered gifts
       subject to reporting to Compliance and the gift limitations.

2.5.1 Gifts to Others
Associates are required to submit the Gift Approval Form to Compliance prior to giving a gift (other than
a de minimis gift described below). Gifts relating to T3TG’s business are limited to $100 per year, per
person. Gifts of tickets to sporting events, or similar gifts where the Employee/Member does not
accompany the recipient, are subject to the limitations on gifts and gratuities. Such gifts may not be so
frequent or so expensive as to raise a suggestion of unethical conduct.

Associates of regulators are also subject to rule limitations regarding gifts to them from broker-dealers
and their associates. Compliance should be contacted for guidance before giving gifts to associates of
regulators.

Gifts and gratuities are not permitted when given for the purpose of influencing or rewarding the action
of a person in connection with the publication of information which has, or is intended to have, an effect
upon the market price of any security.

2.5.2 De Minimis and Promotional Items
The policy does not apply to gifts of de minimis value (such as pens, notepads, or modest desk ornaments)
or to promotional items of nominal value with T3TG’s logo (e.g., umbrellas, tote bags or shirts).
Promotional items must be valued substantially below the $100 limit to be excluded from the gift policy.
Items of higher value (near $100 or more), even if they include T3TG’s logo, are considered "gifts" subject
to this policy.

FINRA excludes customary Lucite tombstones, plaques or other decorative items commemorating a
business transaction. This exemption is very limited; other items are considered gifts subject to the policy,
even if they commemorate a business transaction.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 11
 Gifts, Gratuities and Entertainment                                                           09062023
2.5.3 Aggregation of Gifts
The annual gift limitation is the aggregate of all gifts given to any one individual. For example, a gift of a
$75.00 ticket to a football game in November (as a gift and not as entertainment, discussed below), and
then a holiday gift of a $50.00 bottle of wine to the same person in the same year, would be in violation
of the $100 limitation.

Each recipient is limited to $100 in total gifts during any calendar year.

2.5.4 Valuation of Gifts
Gifts are valued at the higher of cost or market value, excluding tax and delivery charges. For tickets, it is
the higher of cost or face value. If gifts are given to multiple recipients, the names of all recipients are
recorded and the value of the gift is prorated among recipients. For example, a $250 fruit basket given to
an office of three individuals is permitted since the value of the gift prorated is less than the $100
limitation per person.

2.5.5 Accepting Gifts
Associates may not solicit gifts or gratuities from customer/members or other persons who have business
dealings with T3TG. Associates are not permitted to accept gifts from outside vendors currently doing
business with T3TG, or seeking future business, without a review by the Compliance Department and the
written approval of their Supervisor. This policy does not include customary business lunches or
entertainment; promotional items (e.g., caps, T-shirts, pens); or gifts of nominal (less than $100.00) value.
When accepting gifts from customer/members or other business-related persons, the Employee/Member
is required to report the gift and its value to compliance who will approve/disapprove. Once approved
compliance will add to gifts given/gifts received spreadsheet.

2.5.6 Entertainment
Entertainment of customer/members or prospective customer/members must be reasonable and not so
expensive it raises a suggestion of unethical conduct. All entertainment and related expenses must be
detailed on an expense form, with receipts attached, for expenses over $25.00. Expense forms should be
submitted to the appropriate supervisor within 30 days of incurring the expenses.

The limitation on gifts and gratuities does not apply to usual business entertainment such as dinners or
sporting events where the Employee/Member hosts the entertainment, though such expenses should be
reasonable. "Entertainment" includes a broad range of activities such as trips, parties, and other activities
where an Employee/Member hosts someone related to T3TG’s business. Questions regarding the
reasonableness of proposed entertainment and related expenses should be referred to Compliance.

2.5.7 Gifts Incidental to Business Entertainment
Items given in conjunction with entertainment (a golf shirt at a golf outing; a fruit basket delivered to a
customer/member's room during a Firm outing) are considered gifts subject to the $100 limitation.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 12
 Gifts, Gratuities and Entertainment                                                            09062023

The Department of Labor (DOL), under a federal act, requires T3TG to report any payment or loan, direct

of the dollar amount involved.

2.5.9 Gifts or Payments to Public Officials
[Various state laws]


A "public official" is anyone who is elected or appointed to an office or is an Employee/Member of a
"public entity," including any teacher or professor employed by a public entity. A "public entity" is broadly
defined, and includes political bodies, municipalities and their governing bodies (school district, school
board, etc.), public universities and colleges, as well as any other municipal entity. This policy also includes
honorarium payments (payments for any speech given, article published, or attendance at any public or
private conference, convention, meeting, social event, or similar gathering).


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                             Page 13
 Gifts, Gratuities and Entertainment                                                             09062023
Some public entities are statutorily authorized to charge the cost of inspections of regulated entities. A
public official may, therefore, receive payment for statutorily-authorized expenses. For example, if a state
securities official appears at an office to conduct an inspection, the state may, if authorized in state
statutes, charge T3TG for expenses related to conducting the inspection.

2.5.10 Compliance Department Review
Prior review by the Compliance Department and written approval from a Supervisor are required for gifts
or entertainment involving public officials.

[FINRA Notice to Members 06-21]

When an Employee/Member or an agent of a customer/member, such as a hedge fund, mutual fund,
pension plan, or investment manager, solicits a substantial charitable contribution from T3TG or an RR,
there may be a conflict of interest. For example, if an investment manager requests a $10,000
contribution to a charity, this could be construed as a condition for the investment manager to direct
business to T3TG. There can be no quid pro quo between contributions and business conducted with
T3TG.

To avoid potential conflicts of interest, T3TG has established the following guidelines for handling such
requests.

     1. Charitable giving by T3TG, or foundations created by T3TG, is subject to review by the CEO or the
        CEO's designee, in consultation with the Compliance Department, on at least an annual basis.
     2. Contributions of $5,000 or more, solicited by an Employee/Member or agent of a
        customer/member, require completion of the Charitable Contribution Approval Request form and
        the prior approval of Compliance, as well as the approval of someone representing the
        customer/member other than the person soliciting the contribution. Requests should be
        submitted by completing the Charitable Contribution Approval Request form.

    4. These requirements do not apply to a retail customer/member who solicits a charitable
       contribution when acting in his or her individual capacity.

   •    Compliance is responsible for making necessary regulatory filings, including amendments to, or

                                           2.13 Anti Money Laundering
[FINRA Rule 3310; Bank Secrecy Act]


Money laundering is a serious crime potentially related to the funding of terrorist activities. It is the
subject of extensive federal regulations that impose requirements on financial institutions, such as
broker-dealers and their associates, to detect and prevent potential money laundering activities. This is
an obligation of each Employee/Member and/or associate of T3TG.

Money laundering is the movement of criminally derived funds to conceal the true source, ownership, or
use of the funds. The funds are filtered through a maze or series of transactions, so the funds are
"cleaned" to look like proceeds from legal activities.

In general, money laundering occurs in three stages. Cash first enters the financial system at the
"placement" stage, where the cash profits from criminal activity are converted into monetary
instruments, such as money orders or traveler's checks, or deposited into accounts at financial
institutions. At the "layering" stage, the funds are transferred or moved into other accounts or other
financial institutions to separate further the proceeds from their criminal origin. At the "integration"
stage, the funds are reintroduced into the economy and used to purchase legitimate assets or to fund
further criminal or legitimate activities.

Engaging in money laundering is a federal crime with severe penalties for those engaged in criminal
activities and those who facilitate, intentionally or inadvertently, money laundering. It is important that
T3TG, as well as all associates, remain diligent and active participants in T3TG’s Anti-money Laundering
(AML) program, which may be revised from time to time.

The firm does not have any foreign clients at this time.

2.13.1 Background
The Currency and Foreign Transactions Reporting Act, also known as the Bank Secrecy Act (BSA), and its
accompanying regulation, is a tool the U.S. government uses to fight drug trafficking, money laundering,
and other crimes. Congress enacted the BSA to prevent financial service providers (such as banks and
broker-dealers) from being used as intermediaries for, or to hide the transfer or deposit of, money derived
from criminal activity. Money laundering schemes may include the use of wire transfers, cash, bearer
instruments, travelers' checks, money orders, cashiers' checks, and other negotiable instruments.

T3TG is required to comply with the reporting, recordkeeping, and record retention requirements of the
BSA. The requirements govern the payment, receipt, or transfer of currency within, and into and out of,
the U.S. and foreign financial transactions and accounts.

2.13.2 Shell Companies
[FINCen advisory on shell companies: http://www.fincen.gov/AdvisoryOnShells_FINAL.pdf]




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 18
 Anti Money Laundering                                                                       09062023
Shell companies may represent potential money laundering risks. "Shell Company" refers to non-publicly
traded corporations, limited liability companies (LLCs), and trusts that typically have no physical presence
(other than a mailing address) and generate little or no independent economic value. It is important for
associates to be aware of the risks involved in dealing with shell companies.

Most shell companies are formed for legitimate business purposes, such as to hold stock or intangible
assets of another business entity, or to facilitate domestic and cross-border currency and asset transfers
and corporate mergers. Unfortunately, shell companies have become common tools for money
laundering and other financial crimes, primarily because they are easy and inexpensive to form and
operate, and ownership and transactional information can be concealed from regulatory and law
enforcement authorities. Most states do not collect or require disclosure of ownership information at the
formation stage or after.

Agents, also known as intermediaries or nominee incorporation services (NIS), can play a central role in
the formation and maintenance of shell companies. Agents and NIS firms offer a wide range of services
that may include offering an office address, mail-forwarding services, local telephone listings, and other
services that may give the appearance of a locally-established business. Some agents and NIS firms also
provide nominee services which can preserve a client's anonymity. Some risk indicators of shell
companies potentially engaged in money laundering are:

   •   An inability to obtain (through Internet searches, commercial database searches, or direct
       inquiries to the company's foreign correspondent bank) information necessary to identify
       originators or beneficiaries of wire transfers.
   •   A foreign correspondent bank exceeds the anticipated volume projected in its client profile for
       wire transfers in a given period or an individual company exhibits a high amount of sporadic
       activity that is inconsistent with normal business patterns.
   •   Payments have no stated purpose, do not reference goods or services, or identify only a contract
       or service number.
   •   Goods or services of the company do not match the company's profile based on information
       previously provided.
   •   Transacting businesses share the same address, provide only a registered agent's address, or raise
       other address-related inconsistencies.
   •   An unusually large number and variety of beneficiaries receive wire transfers from one company.
   •   Frequent involvement of beneficiaries located in high-risk, offshore financial centers.
   •   Multiple high-value payments or transfers between shell companies with no apparent legitimate
       business purpose.

2.13.3 Penalties
Participation in a money laundering scheme or the knowing receipt of proceeds from criminal activities is
a crime. T3TG and its associates are subject to severe criminal, civil, and regulatory penalties if they
facilitate or participate in money laundering activities. Violations by associates may result in internal
disciplinary action, including termination.



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 19
 Anti Money Laundering                                                                        09062023
An Employee/Member may be deemed to be facilitating or participating in money laundering by engaging
in a transaction with a customer/member (accept a deposit, arrange a withdrawal, effect a trade, etc.)
when he or she is aware of, or willfully ignores, the fact that the customer/member is engaged in illegal
activities.

2.13.4 Treasury Dept. OFAC List / FINCEN Review
The U.S. Treasury Department's Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC) is responsible for publishing
sanctions against persons, corporations, and other entities, including foreign governments that have been
identified by the U.S. Government as engaging in criminal activities, including drug trafficking and terrorist
activities. T3TG is obligated to check its accounts against the lists of blockings to ensure it does not engage
in prohibited transactions, which include securities transactions and transfer of assets out of a blocked
account or to a blocked person or entity. T3TG checks customer/members and the agents who have been
granted authority to trade or otherwise conduct business activities through T3TG on their behalf.

T3TG has procedures to monitor the OFAC lists and comply with requirements to block property and
notify OFAC when required. Questions regarding T3TG’s program should be referred to the AML
Compliance Officer. More information is also available at the OFAC website at www.treas.gov/ofac.

FINCEN reviews are conducted whenever they are posted to the secure information website. Any match
in the system will be immediately disclosed through the website and the account in question will be
closed. Further information or SARS reporting will be examined at that time.

2.13.5 Preventing Money Laundering
There are a number of ways T3TG and its associates can avoid money laundering schemes.

2.13.6 Knowing the Customer/member
Being familiar with the customer/member's financial resources, business activities, and sources of funds
are avenues for knowing the customer/member. Knowing the customer/member occurs at the time an
account is opened, as well as during the operation of a customer/member's account.

The identity of customer/members and their agents who utilize the Firm’s trading systems must be
verified when a new account is opened. Procedures for verifying customer/member ID are explained in
the chapter ACCOUNTS in the section New Accounts.

2.13.7 Risk Indicators
The following are examples of risk indicators (red flags) that may suggest potential money laundering.

 Red Flags indicating potential Money Laundering
 The customer/member exhibits unusual concern regarding T3TG’s compliance with government
 reporting requirements and T3TG’s AML policies, particularly with respect to his or her identity, type
 of business and assets, or is reluctant or refuses to reveal any information concerning business
 activities, or furnishes unusual or suspect identification or business documents.



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 20
 Anti Money Laundering                                                                          09062023
The customer/member wishes to engage in transactions that lack business sense or apparent
investment strategy, or are inconsistent with the customer/member's stated business strategy.
The information provided by the customer/member that identifies a legitimate source for funds is
false, misleading, or substantially incorrect.
Upon request, the customer/member refuses to identify or fails to indicate any legitimate source for
his or her funds and other assets.
The customer/member (or a person publicly associated with the customer/member) has a
questionable background or is the subject of news reports indicating possible criminal, civil, or
regulatory violations.
The customer/member exhibits a lack of concern regarding risks, commissions, or other transaction
costs.
The customer/member appears to be acting as an agent for an undisclosed principal, but declines or
is reluctant, without legitimate commercial reasons, to provide information or is otherwise evasive
regarding that person or entity.
The customer/member has difficulty describing the nature of his or her business or lacks general
knowledge of his or her industry.
The customer/member engages in suspicious activity involving the practice of depositing penny
stocks, liquidates them, and wires proceeds. A request to liquidate shares may also represent engaging
in an unregistered distribution of penny stocks which may also be a red flag. [FINRA Regulatory Notice
09-05]
The customer/member attempts to make frequent or large deposits of currency, insists on dealing
only in cash equivalents, or asks for exemptions from T3TG’s policies relating to the deposit of cash
and cash equivalents.
The customer/member engages in transactions involving cash or cash equivalents or other monetary
instruments that appear to be structured to avoid the $10,000 government reporting requirements,
especially if the cash or monetary instruments are in an amount just below reporting or recording
thresholds.
For no apparent reason, the customer/member has multiple accounts under a single name or multiple
names, with a large number of inter-account or third-party transfers.
The customer/member is from, or has accounts in, a country identified as a non-cooperative country
or territory by the Financial Action Task Force (FATF).
The customer/member's account has unexplained or sudden extensive wire activity, especially in
accounts that had little or no previous activity.
The customer/member's account shows numerous currency or cashier’s check transactions
aggregating to significant sums.
The customer/member's account has a large number of wire transfers to unrelated third parties
inconsistent with the customer/member's legitimate business purpose.
The customer/member's account has wire transfers that have no apparent business purpose to or
from a country identified as a money laundering risk or a bank secrecy haven
The customer/member's account indicates large or frequent wire transfers, immediately withdrawn
by check or debit card without any apparent business purpose.
The customer/member makes a funds deposit followed by an immediate request that the money be
wired out or transferred to a third party, or to another firm, without any apparent business purpose.

Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 21
Anti Money Laundering                                                                     09062023
 The customer/member makes a funds deposit for the purpose of purchasing a long-term investment
 followed shortly thereafter by a request to liquidate the position and transfer of the proceeds out of
 the account.
 The customer/member engages in excessive journal entries between unrelated accounts without any
 apparent business purpose.
 The customer/member requests that a transaction be processed in such a manner to avoid T3TG’s
 normal documentation requirements.
 The customer/member, for no apparent reason or in conjunction with other "red flags," engages in
 transactions involving certain types of securities, such as penny stocks, Regulation "S" (Reg S) stocks,
 and bearer bonds, which although legitimate, have been used in connection with fraudulent schemes
 and money laundering activity. (Such transactions may warrant further due diligence to ensure the
 legitimacy of the customer/member's activity.)
 The customer/member's account shows an unexplained high level of account activity with very low
 levels of securities transactions.
 The customer/member maintains multiple accounts, or maintains accounts in the names of family
 members or corporate entities, for no apparent business purpose or other purpose.
 The customer/member's account has inflows of funds or other assets well beyond the known income
 or resources of the customer/member.

2.13.8 Cash Deposits Not Accepted
T3TG does not accept cash deposits or cash equivalents (money orders, traveler’s checks).
Customer/members who attempt to deposit cash should be advised to submit a personal check or wire
transfer to the Firm’s Clearing Agent for benefit of his or her account. T3TG will accept cash equivalents
from its Members to pay for registration fees only. All capital contributions must be a personal check or
wired funds.

2.13.9 Reports of Crimes and Suspected Crimes
All associates are obligated to report to the AML Compliance Officer any known or suspected crimes. If
the violation implicates the AML Officer, it should be reported to a senior officer of T3TG. T3TG is
obligated, under federal regulations, to report crimes or suspected crimes potentially relating to money
laundering activity.

2.13.10 What to report
Crimes or suspected crimes by individuals (whether associated with T3TG, a customer/member, or
prospective customer/member) are required to be reported. This includes suspicion that T3TG is being
used as a conduit for criminal activity, such as money laundering or structuring transactions (discussed
below), to evade the Bank Secrecy Act reporting requirements or a trading violation. There is no clear
definition of what constitutes a "crime." If you believe some improper or illegal activity is occurring, it is
your obligation to report it.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 22
 Anti Money Laundering                                                                          09062023
2.13.11 SAR reports
Broker-dealers are required to file Suspicious Activity Reports (SARs) for transactions that may be
indicative of money laundering activity. A SAR is filed for a suspicious transaction involving $5,000 or more
in funds or other assets. By law, T3TG and its associates cannot disclose to the customer/member or
anyone other than authorized regulators that it has filed a SAR. Questions regarding SAR filings should be
referred to Compliance.

2.13.12 Currency Transaction Reporting
The Firm has a strict policy not to accept cash currency deposits. As a reminder, the Bank Secrecy Act
requires broker-dealers to report certain transactions relating to currency transactions, as follows:

   •   Report cash or currency deposits of more than $10,000, including multiple deposits on the same
       day that would total more than $10,000. A Currency Transaction Report (CTR) is filed with the
       Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN), a bureau of the Treasury Department. Some
       state regulators also require reporting of currency transactions.

   •   Report currency or bearer instruments over $10,000 transferred into or out of the U.S. The
       Currency and Monetary Instrument Transportation Report (CMIR) is filed with the U.S. Customs
       Service.

2.13.13 Prohibition against Structuring Deposits to Avoid Reporting
Cash or currency deposits or attempted deposits which appear to be part of a deposit structure to avoid
IRS or Customs currency reporting requirements or Firm limitations, or are otherwise suspicious, may not
be accepted and must be reported to Compliance. Associates are prohibited from:

   •   aiding or advising a customer/member in structuring a transaction to avoid reporting
       requirements
   •   holding instruments for deposit on succeeding days
   •   transporting cash or cash equivalents or bearer instruments to a bank on behalf of a
       customer/member

2.13.14 Recordkeeping Requirements
In addition to reporting requirements, broker-dealers are subject to requirements to maintain records of
transfers of funds (including wire fund transfers) of $3,000 or more. This includes transfers between
accounts that are not for the same owner and transfers to third parties, including banks and other
financial institutions. Records of transfers are available for inspection by regulators and other appropriate
authorities, when requested.

2.13.15 AML Compliance Officer
T3TG has designated an AML Compliance Officer who is responsible for overseeing T3TG’s Anti-money
Laundering Program. The AML Officer may be contacted whenever an Employee/Member has questions
about T3TG’s program, a current or prospective account, or activities or transactions that raise questions
about potential money laundering activities. An Employee/Member may also provide information

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 23
 Anti Money Laundering                                                                         09062023
anonymously to the AML Officer. The AML Officer is responsible for investigating suspected money
laundering activities and taking corrective action when necessary.

2.13.16 Identity Theft
Identity thieves use someone's personal identifying information to open new accounts and misuse
existing accounts. T3TG has established an Identity Theft Prevention Program (ITPP) to help detect and
prevent identity theft. Many elements of detecting or preventing identity theft are similar to anti-money
laundering (AML) requirements that are included in these policies.

The ITPP is based on identifying "red flags" that indicate identity theft may have occurred.
It is the responsibility of all associates to be alert and report to the AML Compliance Officer any new or
existing customer/members who may be engaged in violations of anti-money laundering regulations
or identity theft or who have reported identity theft.

Following is a list of potential identity theft red flags.
 Red Flag
 Category: Alerts, Notifications or Warnings from a Consumer Credit Reporting Agency
 1. A fraud or active duty alert is included on a consumer credit report. An "active duty" alert is an alert
 a military person may add to his/her credit report to identify potential identity theft.
 2. A notice of credit freeze is given in response to a request for a consumer credit report.
 3. A notice of address or other discrepancy is provided by a consumer credit reporting agency.
 4. A consumer credit report shows a pattern inconsistent with the person's history, such as a big
 increase in the volume of inquiries or use of credit, especially on new accounts; an unusual number of
 recently established credit relationships; or an account closed because of an abuse of account
 privileges.
 Category: Suspicious Documents
 5. Identification presented looks altered or forged.
 6. The identification presenter does not look like the identification's photograph or physical
 description.
 7. Information on the identification differs from what the identification presenter is saying.
 8. Information on the identification does not match other information our firm has on file for the
 presenter, like the original account application, signature card or a recent check.
 9. The application looks like it has been altered, forged or torn up and reassembled.
 Category: Suspicious Personal Identifying Information
 10. Inconsistencies exist between the information presented and other things we know about the
 presenter or can find out by checking readily available external sources, such as an address that does
 not match a consumer credit report, or the Social Security Number (SSN) has not been issued or is
 listed on the Social Security Administration's (SSA's) Death Master File.
 11. Inconsistencies exist in the information that the customer/member gives us, such as a date of birth
 that does not fall within the number range on the SSA's issuance tables.
 12. Personal identifying information presented has been used on an account our firm knows was
 fraudulent.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 24
 Anti Money Laundering                                                                          09062023
 13. Personal identifying information presented suggests fraud, such as an address that is fictitious, a
 mail drop, or a prison; or a phone number is invalid, or is for a pager or answering service.
 14. The SSN presented was used by someone else opening an account or other customer/members.
 15. The address or telephone number presented has been used by many other people opening
 accounts or other customer/members.
 16. A person who omits required information on an application or other form does not provide it when
 told it is incomplete.
 17. Inconsistencies exist between what is presented and what our firm has on file.
 18. A person making an account application or seeking access cannot provide authenticating
 information beyond what would be found in a wallet or consumer credit report, or cannot answer a
 challenge question.
 Category: Suspicious Account Activity
 19. Soon after T3TG gets a change of address request for an account, we are asked to add additional
 access means (such as debit cards or checks, if applicable) or authorized users for the account.
 20. A new account exhibits fraud patterns, such as where a first payment is not made or only the first
 payment is made (or, if applicable, the use of credit for cash advances and securities easily converted
 into cash).
 21. An account develops new patterns of activity, such as nonpayment inconsistent with prior history,
 a material increase in credit use, or a material change in spending or electronic fund transfers.
 22. An account that is inactive for a long time is suddenly used again.
 23. Mail T3TG sends to a customer/member is returned repeatedly as undeliverable even though the
 account remains active.
 24. We learn that a customer/member is not getting his or her paper account or electronic statements.
 25. We are notified that there are unauthorized charges or transactions to the account.
 Category: Notice From Other Sources
 26. An outside agency, law enforcement, a clearing firms, or other source notifies T3TG that an
 account has been opened or used fraudulently.
 27. T3TG is notified of potential unauthorized access to customer/member personal information due
 to data loss from an outside provider or a breach of an outside provider's data.
 28. Notice from a customer/member of the loss of information (e.g., loss of wallet, birth certificate,
 etc.).

                                 2.14 Business Continuity/Disaster Recovery
[FINRA Rule 4370]


T3TG has a Business Continuity Plan that assigns responsibilities and outlines procedures in the event of
a disaster or emergency which impacts T3TG’s ability to continue conducting business (also termed a
"significant business disruption"). Examples of a major disruption include a regional power outage;
disruption at another company that provides services critical to T3TG’s business; and destruction of an
office or other facilities by natural causes or by other means. The Plan designates associates who are
responsible for Employee/Member safety and protection of firm property, records, and
customer/member assets.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 25
 Business Continuity/Disaster Recovery                                                      09062023
In the event of a disruption, associates will be given instructions by authorized personnel. Depending on
the nature of the emergency, it may be necessary to use alternative communication systems; transfer
personnel and/or business activities to alternative office space; or transfer T3TG’s business to other
brokerage firms or financial institutions until normal operations can be resumed.

T3TG has established procedures for contacting associates in the event of an emergency. If T3TG conducts
a test of its emergency procedures, all associates are required to participate as if the emergency were
real. Past emergencies affecting the securities industry have shown that preparedness and cooperation
are key to maximizing the safety of associates and minimizing business interruptions. It is important for
all associates to follow instructions from senior management and other authorized key personnel during
any drill or when an emergency occurs.

Questions regarding T3TG’s Business Continuity Plan may be referred to Compliance.

2.15.1 Use of Firm Name
No Employee/Member may use T3TG’s name in any manner which could be reasonably misinterpreted
to indicate a tie-in between T3TG and any outside activity of the Employee/Member.

2.15.2 High Pressure Sales Tactics
[FINRA Rule 2111]
As the Firm does not engage in making securities recommendations, T3TG and its RRs will not engage in
high pressure sales tactics which may include excessive telephone calls, implying that a price may change

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 26
 Prohibited Activities                                                                        09062023
on a security if the customer/member doesn't act immediately, or falsely representing that there is a
limited supply of a security at a particular price.

2.15.3 Providing Tax Advice Not Permitted
Associates may not give tax advice to customer/members since T3TG and its associates are not engaged
in the practice of providing tax advice. Customer/members requiring specific tax guidance should be
referred to their personal tax advisers.

2.15.4 Rebates of Commission
[FINRA Rule 2420]


Associates are prohibited from rebating to anyone, directly or indirectly, any commission or
compensation received unless authorized and approved by Senior Management.

2.15.5 Sharing Commissions or Fees with Non-Registered Persons

With few exceptions, regulations generally prohibit the sharing of commissions or compensation with
non-registered persons. Any payment or sharing arrangement to a non-registered person must be
referred to Compliance for review.

2.15.6 Settling Complaints or Errors Directly With Customer/members
Associates may not make payments to customer/members of any kind to resolve an error or
customer/member complaint. Errors and complaints must be brought to the attention of the
Employee/Member's designated supervisor. All material complaints of any nature, whether in writing or
oral, must be brought to the immediate attention of the Compliance Department.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                      Page 27
 Prohibited Activities                                                                    09062023
2.15.7 Borrowing From and Lending To Customer/members
Registered associates are generally not permitted to borrow from or lend to their own
customer/members. "RR" as used in this policy refers to any registered person of T3TG.

This restriction does NOT apply when an Employee/Member enters into a loan arrangement with a
customer/member who is:

     1. an immediate family member (defined as parents; grandparents; in-laws; spouse; siblings;
        children; grandchildren; cousins; aunts or uncles; nieces or nephews; and any other person whom
        the RR supports, directly or indirectly, to a material extent);
     2. a financial institution in the business of providing credit, financing, or loans AND where the terms
        of the lending arrangement are those that would also be available to the general public doing
        business with those institutions;
     3. another registered Employee/Member of T3TG;
     4. someone (or an entity) who has a personal relationship with the RR and the lending arrangement
        arises from the personal relationship rather than an RR/customer/member relationship; or,
     5. Someone (or an entity) that has a business relationship outside the RR/customer/member
        relationship.

Any proposed loan with the RR's customer/member (other than a loan with a family member or financial
institution in item numbers 1 and 2 above) requires the PRIOR review and approval by Compliance. RRs
requesting exceptions must complete the RR/Customer/member Lending Arrangement Request form and
submit it to Compliance prior to effecting the loan arrangement. Compliance will retain written approvals
for at least 3 years after the date that the borrowing or lending arrangement has terminated or for at
least 3 years after the RR has terminated employment with the Firm.

2.15.8 Personal Funds Deposited In Customer/member Accounts
In general, associates are not permitted to deposit personal funds or securities in customer/members'
accounts or deposit customer/members' personal funds or securities in Employee/Member accounts. The
same prohibitions apply to withdrawals. Any potential exception must be forwarded for review to
Compliance.

2.15.9 Prohibition against Guarantees
[FINRA Rule 2150]


T3TG and its associates are prohibited from guaranteeing a customer/member against loss in any
securities transaction. Designated supervisors are responsible for identifying prohibited guarantees in
correspondence or other written communications with public customer/members. Options or written
agreements that establish the future price of a transaction, such as repurchase agreements, are not
included in this prohibition.

2.15.10 Fees and Other Charges
Associates are not permitted to charge fees or assess other charges to customer/members or
customer/members' accounts unless they are expressly permitted by T3TG.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 28
 Prohibited Activities                                                                        09062023
2.15.11 Customer/member Signatures
Associates are not permitted to sign documents on behalf of customer/members, even when doing so is
meant to accommodate a customer/member's request. Customer/member signatures must be original
by the customer/member on all documents.

Associates are prohibited from spreading any rumors or misinformation that the Employee/Member

character that might reasonably be expected to affect market conditions. Discussion of unsubstantiated
information published by a widely circulated public media is not prohibited, providing the source and
unsubstantiated nature are also disclosed.

2.15.13 Misrepresentations
Associates may not disseminate any information that falsely states or implies guarantees or approval of
securities by the government or other institution, such as government guarantee of securities that carry
no such guarantee. SIPC may not be misrepresented as a guarantor of a customer/member's account
against losses from transactions.

institutions or foreign governmental or political officials to obtain business. Entertainment and gifts in

 Computer Records, Equipment and Software                                                    09062023
T3TG considers its computer records, systems, and software to be corporate assets. Associates are
responsible for protecting these assets from unauthorized use, destruction, or unauthorized modification.
This includes a prohibition against violating copyright laws or licensing agreements applicable to
computer software.

Physical equipment (PCs, printers, software, diskettes, etc.) must be placed in a secure location to avoid
theft, tampering, unauthorized use, and environmental hazards (water, smoke, magnets, etc.). The use of
personal computers for T3TG business is subject to the same guidelines and restrictions as T3TG
computers.

When an Employee/Member terminates, any disks or other storage medium that includes proprietary
information, including customer/member information, are considered property of T3TG and must be left
with T3TG.

2.16.1 Laptop Computers
Associates who use laptops are responsible for the security of the laptop and the information contained
on the laptop. Serious security breaches can occur if a laptop containing confidential information is lost
or stolen.

Associates who use laptops for company business are required to comply with the following
requirements:

   •   Laptops containing confidential company records, such as payroll records and sensitive
       customer/member account records (including social security numbers), cannot be removed from
       company premises unless: (1) The Employee/Member is authorized to have such information on
       a laptop and remove it from company premises; and (2) confidential information is encrypted.
   •   All confidential data must be accessed only on the Firm's servers and may not be stored on laptops.
   •   Associates must use passwords that are changed periodically and include random letters and
       numbers. Passwords may not be readily-identifiable words or sequences, such as the user's city
       name, home address, etc.
   •   Laptops must be physically locked with a cable to a stationary object while in use at the office or
       at home.
   •   When traveling, laptops must be locked up unless tracking software has been installed.
   •   Attend training on security breaches and handling of computer equipment and confidential
       information.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 31
 Computer Records, Equipment and Software                                                   09062023
2.16.2 Prohibited Downloading
Associates are prohibited from:

   •   Downloading customer/member and other confidential Firm information from T3TG’s mainframe
       or other central records, unless specifically authorized
   •   Using portable devices such as USB key drives, MP3 players, mobile phones, and other devices for
       downloading information
   •   Downloading programs from the Web to T3TG computers unless specifically authorized or is
       associated with maintenance conducted by authorized computer technicians

                                 2.17 Electronic Communications Policy
This policy governs the use of electronic communications by associates. It applies during business hours
and after-business hours. This is an important policy; associates will be required to certify annually that
they are familiar with and will comply with the policy.

2.17.1 Electronic Communications
1. T3TG electronic systems or communications devices are for T3TG business purposes and business
communications must conform to accepted business standards and regulatory requirements. Only
Firm-approved and monitored electronic communications may be used by the Firm’s associates to
conduct the Firm’s business.
    • Inappropriate communications (profanity, obscenity, threats, and otherwise offensive content)
       are prohibited. Report threatening or harassing communications to Compliance.
    • Communications must include current and valid information.
    • Copyrighted material cannot be sent unless authorized; contact Compliance for assistance.
    • Copyrighted software cannot be copied or transmitted to others unless authorized.
    • References and/or links to websites may be a form of sales literature or advertising requiring
       Compliance review and approval prior to use.
    • Communications that must be accompanied by a prospectus may not be sent electronically unless
       the prospectus is available as an electronic attachment.

2. Electronic business communications must be accessed and transmitted only through T3TG -
sponsored systems.
   • Regulators require retention of business communications and T3TG systems are designed to
       comply with retention requirements.
   • Approved T3TG -sponsored systems include:
           o Email and e-faxes, if any, through desk-top computers in Firm offices (the use of personal
              email accounts for business communications is prohibited)
           o PDAs (personal digital assistants) issued by the Firm
           o Instant messaging limited to Compliance-approved departments or associates
           o Compliance-approved external systems/computers (requires specific approval for
              departments and/or associates)
           o Internal systems for internal-use only (associates are notified of appropriate use of
              internal-use systems)

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 32
 Electronic Communications Policy                                                            09062023
   •   Encryption of information may be required by T3TG; associates may not independently encrypt
       communications on T3TG systems.
   •   Home computers or other personal devices and external systems may not be used for business
       purposes (unless specifically approved by Compliance).

3. Consider electronic communications as public communications; protect confidential information.
    • Do not confuse phone conversations or face-to-face communications with electronic
       communications. Your electronic conversation is subject to review and retention and may be the
       subject of subpoena in a civil or regulatory action.
    • Confidential communications must not be sent on portable devices in public places unless
       encrypted.
    • Do not view confidential information where unauthorized persons may have access (elevators,
       other public places).
    • Safeguard portable devices to avoid unauthorized access to T3TG business.
    • Safeguard passwords.
    • Close open pages and sign out when the device is not in use.

4. There are restrictions on unsolicited emails under the CAN-SPAM Act of 2003.
    • Unsolicited "mass" commercial emails are prohibited.
    • "Commercial" email includes any electronic messages that send a commercial advertisement or
       promote a commercial product or service. It does not include email where there is an existing
       business relationship.
    • Recipients may "opt-out" of receiving future emails. Forward such requests to Compliance.
    • "Address harvesting" or "dictionary attacks" may not be used to obtain email addresses off the
       Internet.
    • Emails sent from T3TG systems will include required identification of T3TG and disclosures or
       disclaimers.

5. Electronic communications will be reviewed, monitored and audited by T3TG.
    • All electronic communications are subject to review and retention.
    • Communications that require pre-use approval may not be transmitted prior to review by the
        designated supervisor. This includes:
           o Communications to be sent to 25 or more investors within any 30 calendar-day period and
               that make any financial or investment recommendation or otherwise promote a product
               or service.
           o Communications that must be accompanied by a prospectus (Compliance approval
               required).
           o Advertising, sales literature, and market letters (Compliance approval required).

6. Use of the Internet related to T3TG’s business is subject to restrictions.
    • Associates are prohibited from posting information to the Internet without prior T3TG approval.
    • Associates may not participate in social networking sites (Facebook, LinkedIn, Twitter, etc.), chat
       rooms, message boards, web logs, blogging, or other external communications systems for
       business purposes or using T3TG systems.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 33
 Electronic Communications Policy                                                          09062023
   •   Accessing offensive sites is prohibited. T3TG may block sites that are offensive or contrary to the
       conduct of business in its sole opinion.
2.17.2 Instant Messaging (“IM”)
        No RRs shall be able to use IM without approval by their supervisor and the Compliance
        Department via the T3TG IM Agreement (See APPENDIX OF FORMS). T3TG blocks all known IM
        systems on its network except for AOL IM and Bloomberg. All approved AOL and Bloomberg IMs
        will be subject to daily review. To assist in compliance with IM review and retention rules and
        requirements, T3TG has incorporated the use of Smarsh for managing IM surveillance. The system
        allows T3TG to screen all IM against a list of words programmed into the software. When a word
        is detected within the body of a message it is flagged and a designee in T3’s Compliance
        Department will have access to the Smarsh system for review. Pursuant to SEC Rule 17a-3, SEC
        Rule 17a-4 all conversations on any approved IM account shall be stored and archived for a
        minimum of three (3) years from the date of the conversation. Once the conversation is
        completed, the data will be converted on a monthly basis into a .pst file and stored on T3TG’s
        server for future retrieval and/or review. Any IM messages containing a “hot word” programmed
        into the system will generate an email alert to a designee in T3TG’s Compliance Department for
        further investigation and this is done on a daily basis. Due to the large volume of alerts generated
        by the system based on false positive matches (i.e. hot word is “sue”, a message containing
        “issue” will create an alert), a random sampling of alerts will be reviewed for each day. If a
        message warrants further investigation, T3TG will contact the Supervisor, RR and maintain all
        pertinent documentation related to such investigation.

2.17.3 Social Media
       RRs will be required to affirm all social media (i.e. Facebook, blogs, Twitter, etc.) websites that
       they use for business and trading related purposes through the Outside Social Media Disclosure
       form in the Associated Person Pack. All RRs will be required to sign annual affirmations disclosing
       all business and trading related social media and also affirming that they will abide by T3TG’s
       WSP. All approved social media usage will be subject to review on a random sampling basis. Any
       review shall be documented, stored and archived for a minimum of three (3) years from the date
       of the review.

2.17.4 Failure to Comply
Failure to comply with this policy may lead to disciplinary action. Non-compliance may generate one or
more of the following:
    • Oral and/or written warning or notification
    • Education/training
    • Suspension of electronic communications privileges permanently or for a set period of time
    • Fine
    • Regulatory discipline
    • Suspension or termination of employment




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 34
 Electronic Communications Policy                                                             09062023
2.17.5 Consent to Policy
Use of T3TG’s electronic communications systems represents the Employee/Member's
acknowledgement and consent to the terms outlined in this Policy, including consent for T3TG to monitor
and audit content and/or usage.
                                 2.18 Advertising and Publishing Activities
Prior to issuing any advertising or writing any books, articles, newsletters, or other materials to be
published in public media (magazines, newspaper, computer bulletin boards, Internet, etc.) for public
access, associates must contact Compliance for review and approval.
                                 2.19 Associates Acting As Trustees, Executors, or Other
                                      Fiduciary Capacities
Associates usually will not act in a fiduciary capacity (e.g., trustee, executor) for a customer/member's
account unless the account is for a relative of the Employee/Member. Exceptions require the approval of
Compliance, who should be notified by written memo requesting the exception and the reasons for the
exception.
                                 2.20 Use of Titles
Associates may not use titles unrelated to their activities with T3TG. The use of any other title requires
the prior approval of Compliance. Examples of the types of titles not specifically related to T3TG’s
activities include (but are not limited to) C.P.A., J.D., M.B.A., or Attorney at Law.

                                  3.2 Continuing Education
[FINRA Rule 1240]

Registered associates are subject to SRO continuing education requirements composed of two distinct
elements. Registered associates are required to complete both elements at specified time intervals. The
two elements are:

3.2.1 Firm Element
All RRs are required to complete continuing education administered by T3TG.

3.2.2 Regulatory Element
This element is a computer-based training program that focuses on compliance, regulatory, ethical and
sales practice standards. Its content is derived from rules and regulations, as well as standards and
practices, widely accepted within the industry. This element is administered at designated testing centers
or through the CE Online Program

 Responsibility            •   Compliance / Or Designee
                           •   CRD Firm Queues and related reports
 Resources
                           •   CRD email notifications of inactive registrations
 Frequency                 •   Ongoing
                           •   Review CRD Firm queue
                           •   Request queue reports
                           •   Notify affected persons of requirements
 Action
                           •   Schedule computer based training, as necessary
                           •   Review CRD email notifications of inactive registrations
                           •   Notify designated supervisors of restricted persons
                           •   Queue reports, email notices, and records of notifying associates are
 Record
                               maintained in registration files

3.2.3 Who Is Subject to the Requirements
All registered persons are subject to the regulatory element.

3.2.4 When Requirements Must Be Completed
The regulatory element is to be completed within 120 days of the 2nd anniversary of the individual's
original registration date and every three years thereafter. For registered persons who become subject
to statutory disqualification or disciplinary action as defined under the rules, the regulatory element must
be completed within 120 days of the posting date of the disciplinary action and every three years after
that date.

3.2.5 Regulatory Element Contact Person
[FINRA Rule 1240(a) (7)]
Compliance or a designated person responsible for registration will notify FINRA of the name and email
address of the contact person to receive CRD notices. Changes will be reported within 30 days of the

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 37
 Continuing Education                                                                         09062023
change. Annually, by the 17th business day following the end of the calendar year, the contact person
information will be reviewed and updated, if necessary.

3.2.6 CRD Notices and Appointments for Training Sessions
The Director of Compliance is responsible for: (1) notifying associates of pending Regulatory Element
requirements; and, (2) scheduling training sessions at designated testing/training centers or the CE Online
Program. CRD notices are retained in the registered person's registration file.

3.2.8 Who Is Subject to the Requirements
All registered persons who do business with the public, and their supervisors, are subject to the firm
element. Firm element continuing education is required regardless of the length of registration or
employment in the securities industry.

3.2.9 Firm Requirements
T3TG is required to:

   •   identify job functions and persons subject to the requirement.
   •   prepare an annual needs analysis including gathering information about products and services and
       training topics.
   •   determine training objectives.
   •   develop a written training plan.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 38
 Continuing Education                                                                        09062023
   •   implement the training plan.
   •   retain a record of participation.
   •   consider developing a method of evaluating the effectiveness of the training.
   •   if available, consider the evaluations in developing the next year's needs analysis.
   •   restrict covered persons who do not complete the requirement.

T3TG’s internal program may include videos, computer training, in-person presentations, and other
methods of conveying training material, including a combination of methods. T3TG may not solely rely on
teleconferences or other remote training methods.

3.2.10 Annual Needs Analysis
An annual needs analysis is prepared for covered persons.

In developing the needs analysis, the following methods may be used:


   •   Questionnaires will be provided to key managers in business and other areas, including Legal and
       Compliance, and responses will be considered in topics to be included.
   •   Feedback from regulators, including recent audits, regulatory alerts, and CE feedback will be
       evaluated.
   •   Customer/member complaints, arbitrations, and other litigation involving the firm will be
       evaluated.
   •   New business lines or marketing strategies will be evaluated.

Needs analyses will be completed each year for the calendar year.

3.2.11 Evaluating the Firm Element Program
Participants may be asked to complete an evaluation form or otherwise provide feedback to evaluate the
effectiveness of the firm element continuing education program. These evaluations are considered when
designing the next year's continuing education program.

3.2.12 Registered Persons Who Fail To Complete Requirements
Registered persons who fail to complete the requirements of continuing education cannot conduct any
duties that require registration or earn commissions or other compensation related to such activities.
Registrations will be considered "inactive" until continuing education requirements are completed.

The designated supervisor will notify affected persons and their supervisors by phone and/or written
memorandum when their registration becomes inactive and when the requirement is satisfied and
inactive status is lifted. Accounting/Commissions also will be notified by memorandum. Copies of memos
will be retained in the individual's registration file.

                                                 3.3 Employment, Registration and Licensing
3.3.1 Hiring Procedures
[FINRA Regulatory Notice 07-55]

This section outlines hiring requirements.

3.3.2 RR Interview Guidelines
[FINRA Notice to Members 07-06]


At the time an RR is being considered for hire, the following are areas the hiring manager should consider:

     1. Discuss with the applicant the nature of the applicant's educational background, licenses, industry
        experience, including the securities firms where they worked and their functions, and other work
        relevant educational, trading and work experience. Explain, to the extent necessary, the Firm’s
        business model and the products and services it offers. Provide other relevant information
        regarding the firm that relates to candidate’s due diligence.
     2. Obtain the applicant's explanations regarding any customer/member complaints and regulatory
        actions to determine the merit, to the extent practicable, of each before hiring.
     3. Ask the applicant about the existence of and nature of any pending proceedings,
        customer/member complaints, regulatory investigations, or arbitrations not listed in the CRD.
     4. Discuss the reasons for the applicant's frequent change of employers, if applicable.
     5. Ask the RR whether he or she signed an employment contract with the present employer and if
        so, obtain a copy from the RR.

3.3.3 Certain Prospective RRs Require Pre-Clearance by Compliance
Information regarding RRs who are being considered for hire should be referred to Compliance for review
of the individual's CRD record and clearance if the individual has answered “yes” to any disclosure
question or has a FBI fingerprint status of other than “clear.” No individual who is subject to pre-clearance
by the Compliance Department may perform activities requiring registration unless the Compliance
Department has cleared the individual.

If T3TG designates multiple chief compliance officers (CCOs), it will meet the following requirements:

     •     CCOs will be designated on Schedule A of Form BD.
     •     Each designated CCO is a principal.
     •     Each CCO's areas of compliance responsibility are defined and documented with identification of

     •     Each CCO meets the requirements of Rule 3130 regarding the defined area of primary compliance
           responsibility.
     •     The designated CCOs have the responsibilities and expertise enabling them to consult with the
           CEO on the totality of subject matters included in certification requirements.

3.3.6 Background Investigation
[FINRA Rule 3110(a) (6) and 3110(e)]


T3TG will conduct a background investigation for all new associates including the following:

     •     Contact with at least the last employer
     •     For registered persons, obtain a copy of and review the RR's Form U5 from the prior firm or inquire
           for the U5 information through the Web CRD system. As per FINRA Rule 3110 (e) this must be
           done within 60 days of the persons initial registration date.
     •     Run the prospective registrant’s name through MIS or BIG database to affirm identification and
           access due diligence information. As per FINRA Rule 3110 (e) this must be done within 30 days of
           the persons initial registration date,
     •     Any "Yes" answers or termination for cause on Form U5 or similar information reported on CFTC
           Form 8-T that were not previously known will be reviewed by Compliance

A record of contacting prior employers will be included on the RR's Form U4 or in the RR's personnel or
registration file, or employment verification letter(s) will be maintained. A copy of Form U5; the Web CRD
inquiry; employment verification letter(s); or CFTC Form 8-T will be maintained in the RR's file unless
electronically maintained.

3.3.8 Policies and Procedures
At the time of hire, Branch Manager will provide the RR with a current copy of T3TG’s policies and
procedures, either in hard copy or by notifying the RR of the location of policies and procedures in
electronic form or physical location. The RR may be asked at the time of registration, and/or annually, to
sign a written acknowledgement that the policies were received and they are responsible for complying.

3.3.9 New Employee/Member Questionnaire
New associates may be asked to complete T3TG’s New Employee/Member Questionnaire or other
disclosure documents relative to the registration process. Any material disclosures relating to the
associates ability to be registered, or otherwise of material concern, will be referred to Compliance for
review.
