3.3.11 Notification to Compliance
Whenever an associate terminates employment from T3TG, other than on a voluntary basis, the
designated supervisor is responsible for immediately notifying Compliance. Notification to Compliance
regarding registered associates should include:

   •   Name of terminated person and RR number(s)
   •   Type of other than voluntary termination (e.g., permitted to resign, discharged, etc.)
   •   An explanation of the reason for termination
   •   Date of termination
   •   Any known compliance problems at the time of termination

3.3.12 Securing and Retrieving Firm Property
As applicable, designated supervisors are responsible for retrieving T3TG property from terminated
associates, including office keys, company credit cards, computer files, customer/member files, and any
other items which are the property of T3TG.

3.3.13 Reassignment of Accounts
A Managing Member is responsible for reassigning the accounts of terminated RRs, as appropriate.

3.3.14 Responding To Customer/member Inquiries
Designated supervisors should instruct branch or department associates, including RRs receiving
reassigned accounts, to only indicate the Employee/Member is no longer with T3TG. No details or
speculation regarding the departure should be given to customer/members, or anyone else outside T3TG,
unless authorized by Compliance to do so.

3.3.15 Form U5
Compliance is responsible for filing Form U5 for any terminated registered Employee/Member.
Compliance will also send, within 30 days of termination, a copy of Form U5 to the former
Employee/Member.

3.4.1 CRD Electronic Filings
[FINRA Rule 1010]

T3TG has designated one or more principals with authority over registration functions, as named in T3TG’s
Designation of Supervisors chart. Any supervisor of electronic filings is a registered principal or a corporate
officer, and is responsible for review and approval of electronic filings and acknowledging electronically
that the forms are filed on behalf of T3TG and its associated persons.

3.4.2 Dual Registration
RRs may be registered with another broker-dealer resulting in dual registration. For those with dual
registration, the following procedures will apply: At this time, T3TG does not allow for dual registration
unless it is with another T3 entity.

   •   Dual registration must be reviewed by the Compliance Department and approved by a Managing
       Member.
   •   Activities covered by registration at each firm will be outlined in a memo provided to the RR by
       the RR's supervisor, and retained in the RR's file (unless dual registration is with another T3 entity).
   •   Supervision of activities that will occur under registration with the other firm will be outlined in
       the memo.

3.4.3 Registration Requirement
All individuals engaged in activities (including soliciting customer/members, selling registered products,
or trading on a proprietary basis) are subject to registration requirements of SROs or other regulators and
must complete the necessary registration and licensing prior to engaging in such activities (“registered

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 45
 Registration and Licensing                                                                     09062023
representative activities”). Associates may not conduct business on behalf of the Firm with public
customer/members until required registrations or licenses are effective.

RRs who assume duties that require registration with FINRA as a principal have 90 calendar days to pass
the appropriate principal's examination.

3.4.4 Requests for Waivers
[FINRA Rule 9600]


Regulators seldom grant waivers of training period requirements or examinations. In those very few
instances where waivers are granted, the candidate must be able to demonstrate comparable work
experience or other successfully completed examinations that could, in the view of the regulator,
constitute satisfying their requirements.

Any requests for waivers will be submitted by Compliance.

3.4.5 State Registrations
RRs must be registered in the state from which they conduct business and may be required to be
registered in other states where customer/members are domiciled. Most states require successful
completion of the Series 63 Uniform State Agent Securities Law Examination. Successful completion of
the exam does not automatically confer registered status on the examinee. Application must be made to
the CRD to obtain each state registration.

The designated supervisor is responsible for identifying transactions in states where registration may be
required.

3.4.6 Parking Registrations
[FINRA Rule 1031(a)]


T3TG does not permit individuals to "park" licenses. Parking occurs when a securities firm maintains a
registration on behalf of an individual that does not work for the firm or who does not need that
registration for their job function. Registration status is retained only for those persons where it is
required. Broker-dealers are permitted, however, to maintain registration for legal, compliance, or other
non-sales associates as permitted under applicable FINRA rules.

3.4.7 Form U4
[FINRA Rules 1010 and 2263]


All applicants for registration are required to complete Form U4. It is the RR's responsibility to include
accurate and complete information and promptly notify T3TG of any updates that may require
amendment to Form U4.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 46
 Registration and Licensing                                                                 09062023
At the time a new or amended U4 is signed, the applicant will be provided the Form U4 Disclosure to
Associated Persons, which discloses information about the pre-dispute arbitration clause included in Form
U4.

3.4.8 Amendments To Form U4 or Form U5
The Firm will electronically submit amendments to Form U4 when an RR advises of updates that require
amendment. Upon request, the Compliance Department will advise whether reportable events or other
matters require the filing of an amendment to an RR's Form U4. Compliance will also determine, to the
extent it is unclear, whether a disciplinary or complaint matter needs to be reported on Form U5
termination notice, including amendments required after termination. Required reportable events
include the receipt of an SEC Wells notice.

['34 Act Sec. 3 (a) (39) and Rule 19h-1; FINRA By-Laws Art. III, Sections 3 and 4, Regulatory Notice 09-19]

3.5.1 Introduction
Individuals may become subject to statutory disqualifications as a result of a felony conviction or
regulatory suspension, revocation of registrations or injunctions (including actions by domestic regulators
including the CFTC and actions by foreign regulators). The definition of statutory disqualification is
included in Section 3(a) (39) of the Securities Exchange Act of 1934. FINRA Regulatory Notice 09-19
includes a chart, in Attachment B, that outlines statutory disqualifications under the Rule. Generally,
however, the Firm will not sponsor an individual subject to a statutory disqualification unless special
approval is provided in writing by a Managing Member.

3.5.2 Hiring a Statutorily Disqualified Person
All prospective associates (including those engaged solely in clerical and/or ministerial activities) are
subject to background investigations that include identification of potential statutory disqualification.
Prior to hiring an individual subject to a statutory disqualification, Compliance must be consulted to


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 47
 Statutorily Disqualified Persons                                                            09062023
review the nature of the statutory disqualification and potential special supervision that may be required
upon hiring.

3.5.3 Regulatory Filings
If Management approves of the sponsorship of an individual subject to a statutory disqualification,
Compliance is responsible for completion and filing of the appropriate regulatory form or application,
which will be signed by a Managing Member of T3TG. A hearing may be required prior to approval of the
individual's association with T3TG. The individual may not conduct any activities requiring registration
until approval is received from the appropriate regulatory authorities.

3.5.4 Supervision
If the Firm elects to sponsor an individual who is statutorily disqualified, Compliance will establish
procedures to carry out the supervision required under agreement with the SRO reviewing the
disqualified person, including records of supervision to be conducted by the designated supervisor. The
supervisor assigned to supervise the statutorily disqualified person will be provided with a copy of the
procedures and will be responsible for carrying them out.

3.5.5 Reporting Statutory Disqualifications
When an Employee/Member becomes subject to a statutory disqualification, if approved by the Firm,
Compliance will file the necessary registration updates and, in addition, the required notification on the
quarterly complaint report will be made to regulators consistent with those SRO's reporting
requirements.

                                   3.6 Continuing Commissions Policy (Registration Not Retained)
[FINRA IM-2420-2; SEC no-action letter to SIFMA, November 20, 2008]

 Responsibility           •   Compliance / Designated Supervisors
 Resources                •   Requests from retirees to continue commissions.
                          •   As required - consider requests; obtain agreements.
                          •   Annually
 Frequency
                                  o Obtain annual compliance certification from retired RR.
                                  o Contact customer/members subject to the program.
                          •   Determine RR's state of domicile permits paying continuing commissions
                              without retaining registrations.
                          •   Determine eligibility of RR
                          •   Obtain signed continuing commissions agreement from retiree.

 Action
                          •   Obtain annual compliance certification from retired RR.
                          •   Contact customer/members subject to the program to verify the retired RR

                              percentage of high-net-worth customer/members)
                          •   Terminate arrangement no later than 5 years after retirement.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 48
 Continuing Commissions Policy (Registration Not Retained)                                  09062023
                          •   Determination of RR's eligibility to participate.
                          •   Signed agreement with RR.
                          •   Annual compliance certification.
 Record
                          •   Annual contact with customer/members to confirm the RR has not
                              contacted them to discuss securities.
                          •   Termination of RR's participation

RRs who retire may be eligible to participate in T3TG’s a continuing commission policy. This policy
provides for the transfer of accounts from the retiring RR to another RR and gives the retiring RR the
opportunity to provide support to the receiving RR as well as deal with customer/member transition
issues. Retirees under the program sign a continuing commission agreement and are eligible to earn
commissions and other income through the term of the agreement. Implementation of the policy requires
written approval by senior management.

The following conditions apply:

     •    Retirees must have been continuously employed with T3TG for at least 3 years. (There are
          exceptions when an RR dies suddenly and his or her heirs participate in the program.)
     •    Retirees must have demonstrated professional conduct including a low incidence of
          customer/member complaints/arbitrations settlement or decided for more than $25,000 in the 3
          years prior to retirement or pending at retirement. If the retiree has been the subject of such
          complaints, T3TG must determine that the complaints did not require disciplinary action or
          heightened supervision and the retiree was not at fault for improper sales practices. The retiree
          also must not have been subject to statutory disqualification during the 3 years prior to
          retirement.
     •    Retirees must contractually agree to cease all securities-related activities (cannot contact
          customer/members; cannot remain associated with any broker-dealer or investment adviser
          except for state registrations required to receive commissions; cannot take a job at a bank or
          insurance company that involves securities transactions).
     •    Retirees must obey all securities laws and regulations to the extent they remain applicable and
          must certify compliance annually.
     •    Receiving RRs (the RR to whom accounts are transferred) must be approved by the designated
          supervisor, have been in the industry for 3 years and at T3TG for 1 year (with some exceptions)
          and must not have been subject to statutory disqualification during the prior 3 years.
     •    Continuing commissions are limited to 5 years after retirement.
     •    T3TG will annually contact a sample of customer/members whose accounts are subject to the
          program (including a large percentage of high-net-worth customer/members) to ensure the
          retired RR has not contacted them to discuss securities.

RRs who retire may be eligible to participate in T3TG’s continuing commissions’ policy. This program

3.8.2 Change in Ownership, Control, or Business Operations
[FINRA Rules 1011(i) and 1017, IM-1011-1, Notice to Members 06-56, FINRA Regulatory Notice 09-21; FINRA website re: mergers,
acquisitions, and business transfers: http://www.finra.org/Industry/Issues/Mergers/index.htm, FINRA checklist for organizational change:
http://www.finra.org/RegistrationQualifications/MemberFirms/p014283; NASDAQ Rule 1017]


When T3TG anticipates a material change in its business, Compliance will file requests for approval by the
appropriate SROs. Events that require approval include a merger with or acquisition of another broker-
dealer or acquisition of 25% or more of the assets of another dealer; a change in ownership or control;
and a material change in business operations. In addition, material changes include removing or
modifying a membership agreement restriction; market making for the first time; adding business
activities that require a higher level of minimum net capital; and engaging in activities beyond proprietary
trading as defined in NASDAQ rules.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                                    Page 50
 Broker-Dealer Registration                                                                                             09062023
Certain types of expansions are presumed to not be a "material change in business operations" and do
not require FINRA approval. However, this safe harbor is not available to firms that, among other things,
have a "disciplinary history" as defined in IM-1011-1. The interpretation must be consulted to determine
what changes are not material and what constitutes disciplinary history precluding use of the safe harbor.

If T3TG operates under a Restriction Letter, it will conduct business consistent with the Letter, and
Compliance will contact FINRA, if a change is necessary.

3.8.3 Regulatory Contact Information
T3TG is obligated to maintain current contact information with regulators.

3.8.4 FINRA and NASDAQ Contact Information
Updates to contact information will be made within 30 days following any change. In addition, by the 17th
business day after the end of each calendar year, T3TG is required to verify contact information through
the FINRA Contact System.

Compliance will update the following information through FINRA's Contact System when necessary and
will conduct the mandatory annual verification:

     •    Executive Representative [FINRA By-Laws Article IV, Section 3 and Rule 4517; NASDAQ Rule 1150]
     •    Regulatory Element Continuing Education Contact Person [ FINRA Rule 1240]
     •    Emergency contact persons [FINRA Rule 4370]
     •    AML contact person(s) [FINRA Rule 3310.02]
     •    Other contacts mandated by FINRA rules

Contact information will be provided promptly to FINRA upon request, but no later than 15 days after the
request.

3.8.5 Regulatory Filings
[FINRA Rule 4517, Notice to Members 06-61]


T3TG will submit regulatory filings electronically where required. Filing procedures are included in
appropriate sections of this manual.

3.9.1 Introduction
T3TG will institute heightened supervision for RRs or others when appropriate. The following sections
describe T3TG’s procedures for identifying RRs subject to heightened supervision, and the types of
supervision that may be conducted.

3.9.2 Identifying RRs for Heightened Supervision
It is the responsibility of the BOM to identify and refer to Compliance RRs for potential heightened
supervision. RRs will be identified at the time of hire or when an RR becomes subject to regulatory action
and/or a pattern of customer/member complaints. Unregistered individuals who were previously
registered and the subject of customer/member or regulatory complaints are also subject to
consideration for heightened supervision.

3.9.3 Criteria for Identifying Candidates for Heightened Supervision
The following are some criteria that may trigger a review by the BOM to determine whether an RR should
be subject to heightened supervision. Pending as well as resolved matters will be considered, as well as
the materiality and age. The criteria are subjective and the details of the complaints and/or regulatory
actions must be considered in determining whether heightened supervision is necessary.

   •   Three or more customer/member complaints alleging sales practice abuse within the past two
       years (complaints include written complaints, arbitrations, other civil actions)
   •   Complaint filed by a regulator
   •   Injunction in connection with an investment-related activity
   •   Termination for cause or permitted to resign from a former employer where the termination
       appears to involve a significant sales practice or regulatory violation
   •   Employment with three or more broker-dealers in the past five years




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 52
 Heightened Supervision                                                                      09062023
3.9.4 Heightened Supervision Memorandum
When a candidate is identified for possible heightened supervision, Compliance, in consultation with the
RR's supervisor, will consider whether heightened supervision will be established. After the determination
is made, Compliance will prepare a memorandum outlining action taken (or not taken).

Where it is decided heightened supervision will be conducted, Compliance will outline the supervision to
be conducted (including type, frequency, time period of heightened supervision, and how supervision
should be documented) and provide copies of the memorandum to the subject RR and the RR's supervisor
outlining the terms of the heightened supervision. The RR and the supervisor will sign and return copies
of the memorandum to Compliance.

3.9.5 Scope of Potential Heightened Supervision
Heightened supervision will be established after considering the specifics that apply to the RR. Heightened
supervision may take many forms and may include some of the following, to be determined by
Compliance. This list does not limit or prescribe how heightened supervision should be structured for any
one RR, since each case must be reviewed individually.

   •   Limits on type of business (options, retail accounts, etc.)
   •   Limits on types of accounts (discretionary, certain age groups or other demographics, etc.)
   •   Verification with customer/members of new account information when accounts are opened
   •   Pre-approval of some or all trades entered
   •   Pre-approval of certain types of accounts
   •   Contact with customer/members by the RR's designated supervisor
   •   Pre-approval of all written public communications originated by the RR
   •   Extra training or continuing education in areas subject to heightened supervision
   •   Assignment of the RR to a "mentor" or partner
   •   Telephone monitoring
   •   Office geography

3.9.6 Certification by RR's Supervisor
During the term of heightened supervision, the RR's supervisor will certify to Compliance, in writing, that
the heightened supervision has been conducted. The form and frequency of certification will be
determined by Compliance and will be explained in the Heightened Supervision Memorandum provided
to the supervisor.

 Heightened Supervision                                                                      09062023

                                         4.1 Advertising and Sales Literature
[FINRA Rule 2210]
 Responsibility                   •   Compliance / Designated Supervisors

 Record                               electronic initials, are retained in an Advertising file in

4.1.1 Advertising and Sales Literature Defined
Advertising generally includes material published or designed for use in newspapers, magazines or other
periodicals, radio, television, telephone or tape recordings, telephone directory advertising (other than
routine listings), computer bulletin boards or other electronic messages, videotape displays, signs or
billboards, movies, or other material published in public media.

Sales literature generally includes circulars, research reports, form letters sent to more than one person,
newsletters, seminar texts and reprints, or other material originated by T3TG or its associates and to be
reproduced and provided to multiple customer/members or prospective customer/members. Sales
literature also includes telemarketing scripts. The terms "advertising" and "sales literature" are
interchangeable in this chapter.

4.1.2 General Guidelines
All advertising and sales literature must meet the general standards of good taste and accuracy and
should fairly represent the products or services included in the advertisement or sales literature.
Promissory, exaggerated, or false statements, as well as language inferring guarantees, are not permitted.
Projections and predictions are not permitted. Past performance is not a guarantee of future performance
and should be identified as such, if included in advertising or sales literature. Portraying the performance
of past recommendations or actual transactions must include an acceptable universe over a reasonable
period of time.

4.1.3 Required Information
All advertisements and sales literature will contain the name of the Firm. "Blind" ads are not permitted
except for recruiting personnel. Exceptions to use of the Firm's name must comply with rules that deal
with generic, derivative, and other potential variations on the Firm's name (i.e., DBAs).




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                  Page 54
 Advertising and Sales Literature                                                                     09062023
4.1.4 Approval Prior To Publication
All advertising or sales literature must be submitted to Compliance for approval prior to publication or
use.

4.1.5 SIPC Membership
[Securities Investor Protection Act of 1970, U.S. Code Title 15, Chapter 2B-1 and SIPC website at www.sipc.org/how/sipclogo.cfm]


Advertising must include a notation that T3TG is a member of SIPC, e.g., "Member, SIPC." If an explanatory
statement will be included in advertising explaining what SIPC is, one of the following two standardized
phrases must be included:

     •    Member of SIPC, which protects securities of its customer up to $500,000 (including $250,000 for
          claims for cash). An explanatory brochure is available upon request or at www.sipc.org.
     •    Member of SIPC. Securities in your account protected up to $500,000. For details, please see
          www.sipc.org.

The words "Member, SIPC" may be omitted if the official explanatory statement is used in conjunction
with the official SIPC symbol.

When SIPC is referenced in T3TG’s website, if applicable, the site will include a hyperlink to the SIPC
website.

"Advertising" is defined under SIPC rules as any promotional material used in or on any newspaper,
magazine, or other periodical, radio, television, telephone or tape recording, videotape display, motion
picture, slide presentation, telephone directory, sign or billboard, electronic or other public media.

4.1.6 FINRA Membership
[FINRA Information Notice "Use of FINRA Logo," April 29, 2009; FINRA email address for questions: trademarks@finra.org]


Member firms are prohibited from including FINRA's logo on websites, business cards, stationery, or other
marketing materials. The FINRA logo may not be used in any manner by member firms. T3TG may,
however, include "FINRA Member Firm" or "Member of FINRA" on such materials.

4.1.7 Special Filing or Approval Requirements
[FINRA Rule 2210(c), Notice to Members 89-11]


There are special filing or approval requirements for certain products and broker-dealers, as outlined
below.

     •    Filings must be accompanied by FINRA's Advertising and Sales Literature Filing Cover Sheet.
     •    All advertising or sales literature to be submitted to FINRA must be approved by the designated
          supervisor prior to submission to FINRA.
     •    The actual or expected date of first use or publication must be included with FINRA filing.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                               Page 56
 Advertising and Sales Literature                                                                  09062023
4.1.8 Institutional Sales Material
[FINRA Rule 2210]


Sales material prepared for institutional investors is not subject to the same requirements as material
prepared for non-institutional investors. This does not include material that T3TG has reason to believe
will be distributed to anyone who is not an institutional investor. For example, if sales material is provided
to an institution for distribution to associates participating in a 401K plan, the sales material would be
treated as distributed to retail investors. "Institutional investor" includes a:

     •    bank, savings and loan association, insurance company, or registered investment company
     •    registered investment adviser
     •    entity (natural person, corporation partnership, trust, or other) with total assets of at least $50
          million
     •    government entity
     •    Employee/Member benefit plan under Internal Revenue Code Section 403(b) or 457 with at least
          100 participants
     •    qualified plan under Section 3(a)(12)(C) of the '34 act with at least 100 participants
     •    FINRA member or someone registered with a FINRA member firm
     •    someone acting solely on behalf of an institutional investor

Institutional sales material is subject to T3TG’s correspondence review procedures and does not require
pre-use approval and filing with FINRA.

4.1.9 Records of Advertising and Sales Literature
Compliance maintains a central file of all advertising and sales literature approved by T3TG. The file
includes, for each item reviewed:

     •    the approval by the Advertisement Principal
     •    any revisions, if necessary
     •    a record that the item was filed with an SRO and when (if required)
     •    approval received from the SRO (if required)
     •    in what media the item appeared (if applicable)
     •    date of approval

4.1.10 Options
Refer to the chapter OPTIONS regarding requirements for options advertising.

4.1.11 Advertisements Involving Non-Branch Locations
FINRA rules specify that any non-branch location referenced in an advertisement or sales literature by its
local telephone number and/or local post office box is permitted if the advertisement does NOT include
the street address of the non-branch location and INCLUDES the address and telephone number of the
branch office or OSJ directly supervising the non-branch location.



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 57
 Advertising and Sales Literature                                                               09062023
T3TG’s central office address and telephone number may be substituted for the supervisory branch or
OSJ only with the approval of Compliance. Refer to the chapter OFFICES for further information.

4.1.12 Testimonials
[FINRA Rule 2210(d) (1) (E) and 2210(d) (2); FTC Guides Concerning the Use of Endorsements and Testimonials: http://www.ftc.gov/bcp/guides/endorse.htm]

There are specific requirements when using testimonials in communications with the public. Compliance
should be contacted before preparing any communications that include testimonials.

                                              4.2 Outgoing Correspondence
[FINRA Rules 2211, 3110(d) and 3110(c) (4)]

4.2.1 Correspondence Defined
Correspondence includes any written letter or electronic mail message and any market letter distributed
to one or more existing retail customer/members and to fewer than 25 prospective retail
customer/members within any 30-day calendar period. Such correspondence is subject to review and
approval after sending. Correspondence that falls outside this definition, including correspondence that
makes a financial or investment recommendation distributed to 25 or more existing retail
customer/members within a 30-day calendar period, is considered sales literature and requires prior
approval (see the section Advertising And Sales Literature in this chapter).

"Existing retail customer/member" includes any person (except an institutional investor) for whom T3TG
or a clearing broker used by T3TG carries an account; the definition also includes a non-institutional
investor who has an account with any registered investment company for which T3TG serves as principal
underwriter. "Institutional investor" means a bank, savings and loan association, insurance company,
registered investment company, registered investment adviser, any entity (natural person or otherwise)
with total assets of at least $50 million; a governmental entity or subdivision; an Employee/Member
benefit plan meeting requirements of IRS Sec. 403(b) or 457 with at least 100 participants; qualified plan;
FINRA member or associated person of a FINRA member firm; and a person acting solely on behalf of an
institutional investor.

Correspondence also includes portfolio summaries and other types of information originated by the RR
and provided to customer/members or prospective customer/members. Interactive electronic
conversations (direct links or "chat rooms") generally are not regarded as correspondence, though they
are subject to restrictions discussed in the policies on electronic communications included in this chapter
and the chapter GENERAL EMPLOYEE/MEMBER POLICIES. Advertising, sales literature, and market letters
are not included in this definition of correspondence and are subject to separate requirements described
in other sections of this chapter.

A "market letter" is defined as a communication not considered a "research report" (described in the next
section).

Note: Communications Defined As "Research” [Regulation AC; FINRA Rule 2711(a) (9) (A)]
RRs are not permitted to send communications that may be deemed "research" since there are complex
requirements that apply to the issuance of research reports. Federal and SRO rule interpretations define

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                                               Page 58
 Outgoing Correspondence                                                                                                           09062023
"research" as any written communications (including electronic) that includes an analysis of equity
securities of individual companies or industries, and that provides information reasonably sufficient upon
which to make an investment decision and that is distributed to at least 15 persons. This applies even if
the author does not hold the title of "research analyst" and does not work in a research department.

There are specific exceptions under SRO rules. Questions regarding whether a communication constitutes
"research" should be referred to Compliance.

4.2.2 Correspondence Requiring Approval Prior To Sending

Correspondence (including emails) to be sent to 25 or more existing retail customer/members within
any 30 calendar-day period and that makes any financial or investment recommendation or otherwise
promotes a product or service must be approved prior to sending. Such correspondence must be
submitted to the appropriate supervisor and may not be sent until approval has been received.

These communications may also be subject to FINRA rules regarding pre-use approval and filing with
FINRA, depending on content.

Pre-approved form letters and group emails used without change (other than customer/member name,
address) may be sent to customer/members or prospective customer/members without additional
approval. Records of to whom form letters and group emails are sent must be retained in T3TG’s records.

4.2.3 Independently Prepared Reprints
Articles and other reprints are subject to outgoing correspondence reviews and copies should accompany
correspondence submitted for review and approval. If the article or reprint was provided by T3TG to RRs,
the letter or other correspondence may reference what was enclosed without including a copy of the
reprint.

Articles and reprints that are issued by an independent publisher and not materially altered are not
subject to FINRA filing requirements and most content standards. This exception does not apply to
reprints published by an affiliate of T3TG.

Distribution of copyrighted material is subject to the copyright holder's approval. T3TG will obtain
approval for independent reprints it distributes to RRs for sending to customer/members. For other
reprints, Compliance should be consulted for assistance in obtaining the publisher's approval.

4.2.4 Content Guidelines
Items to consider when preparing and reviewing outgoing correspondence (and other forms of written
or electronic communications) include:

   •   Truthfulness and good taste are required.
   •   Exaggerated, unwarranted, or misleading statements or claims are prohibited.
   •   Promises or guarantees: past performance may not be used to promise, guarantee, or imply future
       profits or income from securities.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 59
 Outgoing Correspondence                                                                    09062023
   •   Projections and predictions are not permitted.
   •   Comparisons of personnel, facilities, or charges with those of other broker-dealers should not be
       made unless supported by the facts, and other firms' names should not be included.
   •   Correspondence or other written communications regarding securities subject to pending
       distributions (underwritings) are generally not permitted.
   •   Correspondence regarding securities sold by prospectus (mutual funds, limited partnerships, etc.)
       must be approved by Compliance prior to sending (except for pre-approved correspondence
       where no changes are made).
   •   Only T3TG approved hedge clauses may be used.
   •   Tax advice must not be provided; the customer/member should be referred to his or her tax
       adviser for such issues.
   •   Photocopying and distributing copyrighted material may violate copyright laws.
   •   Profit and loss or other portfolio analyses should include a disclaimer that the customer/member
       should rely on customer/member statements provided by T3TG and any analysis or calculation is
       provided for information purposes only.
   •   The use of T3TG letterhead should be restricted to T3TG-related matters.
   •   Correspondence regarding options is subject to specific requirements which are discussed in the
       chapter OPTIONS.

4.2.5 Letters and Notes
Copies of letters, notes, and similar correspondence must be provided to the designated supervisor on
the day sent.

titled GENERAL EMPLOYEE/MEMBER POLICIES.

The following guidelines for review apply:
   • Correspondence identified as "Confidential" will be opened and reviewed.

   • Complaints will be immediately forwarded to the RR's supervisor and to Compliance.
   • Checks or securities intended for deposit with clearing agent will be immediately forwarded to
        clearing agent.
   • Original customer/member correspondence will be retained for T3TG’s files; the addressee will
        receive a copy.
   • Original customer/member correspondence will be forwarded to the designated supervisor for
        review, initialing, and filing.

4.3.2 Personal Mail
Associates should direct all personal mail to their home address. Personal mail is subject to incoming
correspondence and electronic mail review policies.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 61
 Incoming Correspondence                                                                     09062023
                                      4.4 Legends and Footnotes
[FINRA Rule 2210(d) (1) (C)]

When legends or footnotes are included in public communications, they cannot be placed or sized in a
way that limits the investor's ability to read or understand the information. Small fonts may inhibit reading
the information or may inappropriately diminish the importance of the information. Bold claims balanced
by a footnote may also mislead the reader.

                                      4.5 Internal Communications

4.5.1 Inter-Office Communications
['34 Act Rule 17a-4(b) (4)]


Inter-office memos and other communications are subject to retention requirements. Copies of written
inter-office communications must be retained for 3 years with the 2 most recent years in an accessible
location. Electronic communications are retained for 3 years in an accessible and retrievable format.

4.5.2 Internal Use Only
Printed or electronic information marked "internal use only" may not be sent or otherwise provided to
individuals outside T3TG.

4.6.1 Complaint Defined
"Complaint" is defined as any written statement by a customer/member or a person with authority and
capacity to act on behalf of a customer/member alleging a grievance involving the activities of a person
under the broker-dealer's control in connection with the solicitation or execution of any transaction or
the disposition of securities or funds of that customer/member.

4.6.2 Handling of Customer/member Complaints
When a written complaint is received, a copy should be forwarded immediately to Compliance for follow-
up.

Oral complaints may be resolved by the designated supervisor, if the nature of the complaint is
operational or administrative in nature or another type of nominal problem. Oral complaints alleging
mishandling of the customer/member's account or other material issue should be brought to the
attention of Compliance for review and resolution.

4.6.3 Oral Complaints
Oral complaints should be reported immediately to the respective Branch Manager for sales practice
issues, or to Operations for operational issues. Examples of sales practice issues include complaints
regarding losses, improper trades, and other complaints involving wrongdoing by the RR or T3TG. RRs
should not make independent decisions regarding whether to report complaints; all oral complaints
should be reported either to the Branch Manager or directly to compliance.

4.6.4 Complaints Received By Clearing firms
As required under SRO rules, whenever T3TG’s clearing firms receives a customer/member complaint,
the clearing firms will:
    • Provide a copy of the complaint to T3TG’s compliance officer.
    • Provide a copy to T3TG’s designated examining authority (DEA).
    • Notify the customer/member directly that their complaint has been forwarded to T3TG for
        response and to the DEA.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 63
 Complaints                                                                                 09062023
When received by T3TG, the complaint will be handled in the same manner as other complaints received
directly by T3TG.

4.6.5 Records of Complaints
Compliance will maintain a central record of all customer/member complaints, including the following:

     •    Complainant's name and address
     •    Account number
     •    Date the complaint was received
     •    Name(s) of Employee/Member(s) identified in the complaint
     •    Description of the nature of the complaint
     •    Disposition of the complaint

4.6.6 Notice to Customers / Members
Each customer/member is provided with notification of the address and telephone number of the
department to which complaints may be directed. The CCO is responsible for establishing procedures to
provide this information to customers / members.

4.7.1 Introduction
T3TG has adopted a Privacy Policy which is provided to customer/members at the time a new account is
opened. Notice is also sent to all customer/members on an annual basis. The Privacy Policy explains the
Firm's policies regarding safeguarding of customer/member information and records and whether T3TG
shares information with outside parties. T3TG also publishes its Privacy Policy on its website.

SEC Regulation S-P ("Privacy of Consumer Financial Information") applies only to accounts for individuals
(i.e., institutional accounts are not affected) and differentiates between "customer/members," where
T3TG has an established relationship with the individual, and "consumers," where there is no pre-
established relationship. For purposes of this section, any individual from whom information is obtained
(and their legal representative acting on their behalf) to open an account or to obtain services or products
from T3TG is considered a "customer/member." The term "consumer" will be considered synonymous
with "customer/member" for purposes of this section.

The Privacy Policy applies to all individual customer/members of T3TG whether U.S. residents or foreign
residents.

4.7.2 "Public" vs. "Nonpublic" Personal Information about Customer/members
Generally, information provided to T3TG by a customer/member or potential customer/member in the
normal course of T3TG offering a product or service is considered "nonpublic personal information."
Identifying whether information is "public" or "nonpublic" is important as to T3TG’s obligations, if T3TG
shares information with nonaffiliated third parties. Public information is information that T3TG
reasonably believes may be obtained from three sources:

   •   federal state or local government records;
   •   widely distributed media; or,
   •   Disclosures to the general public that are required to be made by federal, state, or local law.

Nonpublic personal information also includes any list, description, or other grouping of
customer/members (and publicly available information about them) that is derived from financial
information that is not publicly available.

4.7.3 Sharing Nonpublic Financial Information
In the normal course of business, T3TG may share customer/member nonpublic financial information
with service providers, such as clearing firms or service bureaus. Agreements with such third parties
include assurances regarding the protection of customer/member records and information. Information
sharing with affiliated companies may also occur, and if applicable, is disclosed in T3TG's Privacy Policy.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 66
 Customer/member Privacy Policies and Procedures                                              09062023
Outside possible integral processes for the Firm’s day-to-day business operations, T3TG generally does
not share customer/member nonpublic financial information with non-affiliated companies or non-
exempt service providers.

4.7.4 Annual Notification
On an annual basis, T3TG will provide all customer/members with notice regarding T3TG’s Privacy Policy.

4.7.5 Affiliate Marketing
[SEC Regulation S-AM; Section 624 of the Fair Credit Reporting Act; SEC Small Entity Compliance Guide
regarding Regulation S-AM: http://www.sec.gov/divisions/marketreg/tmcompliance/34-60423-
secg.htm]

Regulation S-AM limits use of certain information received from T3TG’s affiliates to solicit a consumer for
marketing purposes. Consumers may block the use of certain financial information by affiliates of the
person the consumer does business with. T3TG may use "eligibility information" (i.e., certain financial
information, such as information regarding the consumer's transactions or experience with the affiliate)
if:
    • the potential market use of the information has been clearly, conspicuously, and concisely
       disclosed to the consumer;
    • the consumer has been provided with reasonable opportunity to opt out; and
    • the consumer has not opted out.

T3TG will only use information from affiliates if the above requirements are satisfied. Opt-out notices
under Regulation S-AM may be included with Regulation S-P opt-out notices described above.

4.7.6 Protection of Customer/member Information and Records
[SEC Regulation S-P, Rule 30(a) and SEC Release No. 34-50781; Fair and Accurate Credit Transactions Act of 2003, Section 216; FINRA Notice to Members 05-
49; FINRA web page "Customer/member Information Protection":
http://www.finra.org/Industry/Issues/Customer/memberInformationProtection/index.htm]


T3TG has adopted procedures to protect customer/member information, including the following:

     •    Customer/members will be provided the Firm's Privacy Policy at the time an account is opened.
     •    The Firm's website is protected by firewalls, a secure server, and anti-virus programs.
     •    Associates are prohibited from downloading sensitive information.
     •    Associates' ability to download sensitive information is restricted by software or devices that block
          downloading.
     •    Requests for customer/member information from outside parties such as regulators, the IRS, and
          other government or civil agencies, are referred to Compliance for review and response.
     •    All agreements with clearing firms and other service providers include the third party's privacy
          policies or confidentiality requirements.
     •    The integrity of the Firm's internal computer systems, including privacy protection, is subject to
          regular review.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                                                  Page 67
 Customer/member Privacy Policies and Procedures                                                                                      09062023
     •    If required by state law, states will be notified if customer/member information is stolen making
          it subject to potential identity theft.

4.7.7 Social Security Numbers (SSNs)
SSNs are part of the information subject to protection of customer/member records. SSNs are obtained
when accounts are opened, as required by federal law. SSNs are retained with account records and used
for federally-required year-end reporting of transactions and/or income. Physical and electronic access is
limited to authorized associates (operations personnel, RRs, managers, etc.) and are provided to outsiders
only when required by law or court/arbitration action or other authorized authority.

4.7.8 Access to Customer/member Information via Wi-Fi
Because of risk of unauthorized access by outside parties and the difficulty of ensuring the security of
wireless connections to the Internet, associates are not permitted to use wireless fidelity (Wi-Fi) to access
customer/member account information, unless:

     •    the Employee/Member is working on T3TG premises; or
     •    the Employee/Member has installed Firm-required firewalls or other protections and has prior
          approval from T3TG’s designated information officer to use Wi-Fi for T3TG business.

                                           4.8 Prohibition against Payments Involving Publications to
                                                Influence Market Prices
[FINRA Rule 5230]

Payments of anything of value, directly or indirectly, are prohibited for the purpose of influencing or
rewarding someone in connection with the publication or circulation of information in any electronic or
other public media for the purpose of influencing the market price of the subject security. This includes
any investment service or similar publication; websites; newspapers, magazines, or other periodicals;
radio; or television program. The prohibition does not apply to clearly-identified paid advertising; a
communication that discloses the amount and receipt of compensation; or a research report as defined
under FINRA rules.

                                           4.9 Pre-recorded Voice Messages and Automatic Telephone
                                                Dialing Systems
[Telephone Consumer Protection Act of 1991]


T3TG does not permit the use of pre-recorded telephone solicitations and autodialers.

4.10.1 General Guidelines
The general concepts of truthfulness, good taste, and a fair presentation apply to associates engaging in
public speaking. Public speaking includes participation in a seminar, forum (including an interactive
electronic forum), radio or television interview, or other public appearance or public speaking activity.

4.10.2 Seminars
RRs should use pre-approved materials and advertisements for sales seminars. Materials and advertising
must be submitted to Compliance for approval prior to use if it has not been pre-approved.

4.10.3 Approval
Prior to engaging in public speaking, the RR should prepare an outline and submit a Public
Speaking/Seminar/Luncheon Request for approval by Compliance at least 3 weeks, if practical, prior to
the event to ensure timely review. The Request should also include a copy of any proposed advertising
about the event, if using materials that are not pre-approved by T3TG.

4.10.4 Radio, TV, and Other Extemporaneous Presentations
The standards of communication with the public apply to all public appearances whether scripted or not.
The following should be considered when participating in radio, TV, or other non-scripted public
appearances. Also refer to the chapter GENERAL EMPLOYEE/MEMBER POLICIES and the section Media


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 69
 Public Speaking                                                                            09062023
Contact Is Limited to Certain Authorized Persons that explains restrictions on dealing with the media. Most
associates are restricted from such contact, except with the specific approval of Compliance.
   • Specific recommendations of securities must be avoided unless approved by Compliance or the
       speaker is authorized under T3TG’s “Media Contact" policy. If a recommendation is made, the
       speaker is required to disclose material information, such as when T3TG is a market maker in the
       stock. The current price and any special risks associated with the security also must be disclosed.
   • The Firm's name must be clearly disclosed in conjunction with any securities or services offered.
   • The speaker cannot assume a specific level of audience knowledge, experience or suitability. High
       risk securities may not be appropriate for discussion in a broadcast format available to any
       listener.
   • Media presentations should be clear and understandable. Avoid overly complex messages and
       technical terminology which may not be understood by the general audience.
   • Include products only where the speaker is licensed to sell the product.

For RRs who are approved to engage in radio, TV, or other extemporaneous public speaking, Compliance
is responsible for periodically reviewing the presentations, either on tape or concurrent with broadcast,
contacting the RR if unacceptable material is included, and making a record of the review and any action
taken.

4.10.5 Options
Any presentation that includes a discussion of options must be approved by Compliance prior to the
presentation. Compliance will maintain a written record of any such approved presentations, including
an outline or description of the presentation.

4.11.3 Review of Electronic Communications
This section outlines procedures for reviewing electronic communications.

4.11.4 Methods of Review

                         •   Compliance / Or Designee
 Responsibility
                         •   Electronic communications from approved systems
 Resources               •   Reviewers including qualified supervisors and designees

                         •   As required
 Frequency
                         •   Determine programs/methods of review, (Minimum 3% random sampling)

 Action

                             as required, prior to assuming review responsibilities

                         •   Records of training, including subject matter, names of those trained, when,
                             and by whom or what method
 Record
                         •   Records of flagged communications that require corrective action, including
                             action taken


All electronic communications are subject to review. Types of review include:

   •   Review of all or sampled communications, depending on business area and/or criteria determined
       by Compliance for selective review

4.11.5 Delegation of Review
The designated supervisor is responsible for reviewing electronic communications and taking necessary
actions. The review may be delegated to a qualified reviewer; “qualified” means the individual has been
trained in what to review and what items to refer to the supervisor and/or Compliance. Though reviews
are delegated, the supervisor is ultimately responsible for compliance with review requirements. The
delegated reviewer will refer the following communications:

   •   Questionable language to the designated supervisor or Compliance

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 72
 Electronic Communications                                                                   09062023
   •   Objectionable language or content (profanity, etc.) to the supervisor or Compliance
   •   Complaints to the designated supervisor and Compliance
   •   Advertising/sales literature/advertisements not previously approved to Compliance
   •   Communications regarding complaints, errors or account designation changes in orders to the
       supervisor or Compliance
   •   Suspicious emails suggesting possible illegal activity or violations of Firm policy or a securities law
       or regulation to Compliance.

4.11.6 Communications Review Before or After Sending
The following communications require approval by a qualified supervisor prior to sending. Other types of
communication are subject to post-sending review.

   •   Advertising, sales literature, or market letters (Compliance approval required)
   •   Form letters (approval of initial form required; subsequent communications may be sent without
       prior approval if there is no change in previously-approved content)
   •   A communication to be sent to 25 or more investors (prospective or current customer/members)
       (Compliance approval required)
   •   Customer/member Letters (BOM Approval)
   •   Communications to retail investors about options (Compliance approval)

4.11.8 Special Reviews
Compliance may impose different standards of review that may include review of all incoming and
outgoing communications regardless of form; pre-approval; or other special reviews. See the section
Heightened Supervision in the chapter EMPLOYMENT, REGISTRATION AND LICENSING for further
information.

4.11.9 Use of Non-Firm Systems or Devices
T3TG may approve the use of non-Firm systems or devices by certain associates. When exceptions are
approved, the Employee/Member will be required to submit a separate certification regarding the use of
the external system/device and re-certify annually; usage will be monitored and periodically audited by
Compliance. Generally, the only approved form of electronic communications is T3TG email. No associate
of the firm may conduct the business of the firm through any electronic media except T3TG email, which
is subject to surveillance. Instant messaging is not a Firm-approved method of communication and RRs
may not conduct business through it.

4.11.10 Advertising, Sales Literature, And Market Letters
Electronic advertising, sales literature, and market letters are subject to pre-use review by Compliance.
Refer to the section Advertising and Sales Literature in this chapter.

4.11.11 Internet Use
[FINRA Guide to the Internet for Registered Representatives: http://www.finra.org/Industry/Issues/Advertising/p006118]

This section outlines procedures for accessing communications systems on the web and posting
information to the web, including a firm website.

The use of social networking sites (Facebook, LinkedIn, Twitter, etc.), bulletin boards, websites, message

soliciting business, or in any way communicating about Firm business is subject to the following
requirements. These requirements do not apply to associates' personal use of systems where no
reference to the Employee/Member's association with T3TG or any business-related communication is
included.

4.11.13 Permitted sites

The use of specific sites requires the approval of Compliance prior to access for business purposes.

4.11.14 Permitted participation

Compliance must approve RRs prior to their participating in social media websites. Compliance will
provide guidelines for permitted activities.

4.11.15 Training

RRs are not permitted to participate in social media websites until they complete the required training to
ensure they understand the requirements and allowable use of sites.

4.11.16 Interactive sites

Where RRs are permitted to participate in interactive sites, a record must be retained of all interactive
"conversations" as part of T3TG's records. These records will be reviewed by the designated supervisor in
accordance with firm policy on review of electronic communications (either on a sampling basis or review
of all communications).

4.11.17 Static postings

Some sites allow static postings where information is posted by the RR or T3TG and remains unchanged
unless revised by the originator. Such postings are considered "advertisements" which require the prior
approval of Compliance. These postings are considered advertisements even if they are updated
frequently.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 75
 Electronic Communications                                                                  09062023
4.11.18 Third party postings
RRs are prohibited from participating in or endorsing third-party postings. The Firm's site includes a
disclaimer that third party posts do not reflect the views of T3TG and have not been reviewed by T3TG
for completeness or accuracy.

4.11.19 Prohibition against recommendations

RRs are prohibited from making specific securities recommendations in postings. Postings must be only
general in nature regarding the RR and T3TG's products and services.

4.11.24 Hyperlinks
[FINRA Interpretation Letter from Thomas M. Selman to the Investment Company Institute, November 11, 1997]

 Frequency                      •    As required

 Record                         •    Website or other material maintained in review file

When hyperlinks to third-party sites are included in T3TG communications or on a T3TG website, it is
important the hyperlink meets the following conditions to avoid liability for the content or regulatory
filing of information included in the third-party site:
     • A hyperlink may not be established to a site known (or there is reason to know) to contain false
         or misleading information.
     • The hyperlink must be continuously available to customer/members who visit the site.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                         Page 78
 Electronic Communications                                                                                   09062023
     •    T3TG or its associates cannot have discretion to alter information on the third-party site.
     •    Customer/members have access to the hyperlinked site whether or not it contains material
          favorable to T3TG.
     •    The linked site can be updated or changed by the third party, following which customer/members
          would still be able to use the hyperlink.

                                           4.12 Identification of Sources
When using communications not prepared under the direct supervision of T3TG, it is necessary to identify,
on the communication, the person or entity that prepared the material. This includes research reports
obtained from outside sources.

                                           4.13 FINRA Taping Rule
[FINRA Rule 31103170(b) (2), Notice to Members 05-46]

T3TG is not subject to this rule. If the Firm is notified by FINRA that it will become subject to the rule, the
Firm is able to submit an exemption request within 30 days of receiving notice from FINRA (either the
one-time "opt-out" provision or other exemption) or implement and document procedures to comply.

5 FINANCIAL AND OPERATIONS PROCEDURES

This chapter provides key policies and procedures affecting the financial and operations areas of T3TG.
Detailed operations procedures are included in the Firm's (or a clearing firm's) operations procedures
manuals.

T3TG has designated a Financial and Operations Principal who is responsible for general oversight of
financial and operations areas of the Firm. Supervisors of specific financial or operations areas are
responsible for day-to-day procedures.

                                         5.1 Books and Records
['34 Act Rules 17a-3 and 17a-4; FINRA Rule 3110, FINRA New and Amended Recordkeeping Checklist:
http://www.finra.org/web/groups/rules_regs/documents/rules_regs/p006378.pdf; MSRB Rules G-8 and G-9]


5.1.1 Introduction
SEC Rule 17a-3 identifies the types of books and records to be retained by T3TG and 17a-4 identifies the
period these records are to be retained. SROs also specify certain record requirements. Designated
supervisors are responsible for retaining required records for areas under their supervision. The FINOP is
responsible for establishing and maintaining T3TG's record retention schedule.

For accounts and other business introduced to a clearing firms, the clearing firms is responsible for
retaining certain records as outlined in the clearing agreement.

5.1.2 Electronic Storage of Records
['34 Act Rule 17a-4(f)]

T3TG utilizes electronic storage media and/or micrographic media for certain records.

5.1.3 Notification to Examining Authority
['34 Act Rules 17(d) and 17a-4(f) (2) (i)]


T3TG has provided the required notification to its designated examining authority (DEA) prior to
employing electronic storage media. The independent third party with download access is also required
to submit an undertaking indicating it agrees to promptly furnish information to regulators. The CCO shall
make all such notifications.

5.1.4 Conditions
['34 Act Rule 17a-4(f) (2) (ii)]


As required by rule, T3TG’s electronic storage media meets the following conditions:

      •     non-rewritable, non-erasable format
      •     automatic verification of the quality and accuracy of the process
      •     serialize original (and duplicate units) and time-date for required retention period
      •     capacity to readily download indexes and records

5.1.5 Ability to Retrieve and Reproduce
['34 Act Rule 17a-4(f) (3)]


T3TG’s electronic or micrographic storage will also meet the following requirements:

      •     facilities for immediate, easily readable production of records
      •     ability to provide facsimile enlargements
      •     duplicate copies stored separately from the originals
      •     organized and indexed accurately
      •     current information necessary to access records and indexes
      •     independent third party who has access and the ability to download records



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                          Page 81
 Books and Records                                                                                            09062023
5.1.6 Audit System
The third party that provides electronic storage services has an audit system to test, on an ongoing basis,
the integrity of the Firm's records retained in electronic storage media. Anomalies reported by the third
party will be reviewed by the FINOP, or another designated person on the FINOP's behalf, to ensure
corrective action has been taken and records are retained as required.

5.1.7 Confidentiality of Electronic Records
T3TG (in conjunction with any vendor) protects against unauthorized access to or use of
customer/member records by use of a password system. Passwords will be changed periodically and
disabled for terminated associates or associates no longer requiring access.

                                    5.2 Calculation and Reporting of Net Capital
['34 Act Rules 15c3-1, 17a-5 and 17a-11; FINRA Rules 4110, 4120, 4130, 4140, and 4521]

 Responsibility            •   FINOP
 Resources                 •   Financial and transaction records
 Frequency                 •   Ongoing

                           •   Report net capital deficiencies as required, when necessary
                           •   Records retained by the FINOP include:
 Record                            o FOCUS reports
                                   o Other net capital calculations

The calculation and monitoring of net capital is the responsibility of the FINOP, who also is responsible
for ensuring the accurate and timely reporting of periodic net capital reports. Some of the FINOP's specific
responsibilities include:
    • Review and filing of T3TG 's financial reports and periodic review of accounting records
    • Periodic consideration of whether T3TG 's minimum net capital requirements have changed
       because of changes in T3TG 's business
    • Supervising additions to, and withdrawals from, the equity capital of T3TG
    • Reporting borrowings and subordinated loans for capital purposes
    • Establishing procedures for retention of required financial books and records

If T3TG becomes deficient in its net capital position, the FINOP is responsible for alerting the CCO and
making the necessary reports to regulators and communicating any restrictions in business that may
result.

                                          5.3 Reconciliations and Bank Records
 Responsibility                    •   FINOP
 Resources                         •   Bank records
 Frequency                         •   Monthly
 Action                            •   Reconcile bank accounts against T3TG ’s records

The FINOP is responsible for establishing procedures for the periodic reconciliation of bank statements,
clearing and depository accounts, and other accounting and business records. Records of bank accounts
and other reconciled accounts will be maintained in accordance with regulatory requirements. The FINOP
may utilize internal or external accounting services to assist with the reconciliation process.

                                          5.4 Designation of Accountant
[SEC Rule 17a-5(f)

The FINOP is responsible for filing notice of the designation of its accountant by December 10 of each
year with FINRA, the SEC's principal office, and T3TG’s SEC regional office. If the agreement with the
accountant is continuous providing for successive annual audits, notice is not required annually. The
following additional requirements apply:
    • An agreement must exist with an independent public accountant by December 1 covering a
       contractual commitment to conduct T3TG’s annual audit during the following calendar year.
    • If the agreement is for a single audit or if the continuing agreement is terminated or amended, a
       filing is required by December 10.
    • If T3TG is exempt from filing an annual audited financial statement, notice must still be filed
       indicating the date as of which an unaudited report will be prepared.
    • Notice must be filed with FINRA and the two SEC offices within 15 days after:
            o T3TG notifies the accountant that its services will be terminated;
            o the accountant notifies T3TG it will not continue its engagement; or
            o a new accountant has been engaged without notice to or by T3TG’s current accountant.

The FINOP is responsible for maintaining a record of filing the required notices.

                                             5.7 Fidelity Bonding
[FINRA Rule 4360]

The FINOP or designee is responsible for obtaining and maintaining fidelity bonding as required by rule
and verifying the adequacy of coverage on at least an annual basis when the bond is subject to renewal.

                                             5.8 Cash Deposits Not Accepted
T3TG’s clearing firms and T3TG policy prohibits the acceptance of cash or currency from
customer/members. Circumstances surrounding any attempt by a customer/member to deposit cash or
currency shall be referred to, and reviewed by, T3TG’s AML Compliance Officer. The Operation’s Manager
is responsible for advising the customer/member that T3TG will only accept checks or wire transfers. The
FINOP and Operation Manager are responsible for referring instances of attempted or actual cash
deposits to the AML Compliance Officer.

                                             5.9 Cash Equivalents
T3TG will only accept deposits of cash equivalents, such as traveler’s checks, money orders, or cashier’s
checks to pay for registration fees for Prop Traders. Any retail Customer/members attempting to deposit
cash equivalents should be advised that the firm’s Clearing Firm does not accept these types of deposits
and will be instructed to instead provide a wire transfer for deposit to their account.

5.9.1 Risk Assessment, Recordkeeping and Reporting
A broker-dealer that is owned by a holding company is subject to SEC requirements governing
recordkeeping and reporting.

5.9.2 Recordkeeping Requirements
['34 Act Rules 17h-1T and 17h-2T; SEC no-action letter to Douglas G. Preston, Securities Industry Association, dated September 30, 1993]


The FINOP is responsible for establishing procedures and determining that T3TG maintains records in
accordance with SEC requirements for risk assessment recordkeeping.

5.9.3 Reporting Requirements
The FINOP is responsible for determining whether T3TG is required to file Form 17-H Risk Assessment
Report For Brokers And Dealers and, if applicable, filing the form with the SEC within 60 calendar days of
the end of T3TG ’s fiscal year.

5.9.4 Risk Practices Regarding Employment and Associates
T3TG has established procedures regarding the hiring of personnel; conduct and review of
Employee/Member accounts; granting of authority to act in various capacities on behalf of T3TG; and the
integrity of T3TG’s systems and financial reporting.



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                                                       Page 85
 Fidelity Bonding                                                                                                                          09062023
5.9.5 Background Checks
One of T3TG’s first lines of defense is the hiring of qualified people who do not bring high-risk behavior
to their positions. T3TG conducts background checks on all applicants for employment with T3TG. All
offers of employment are considered conditional pending the outcome of the background checks. These
background checks include utilizing FINRA’s fingerprint system, McDonald’s Information Services (MIS)
and Business Information Group (BIG) and confirming prior employment when applicable. Any adverse
information is referred to the hiring manager for consideration prior to finalization of employment.

Also refer to the section Hiring Procedures in the chapter EMPLOYMENT, REGISTRATION AND LICENSING
for more information about hiring procedures.

5.9.6 Employee/Member Accounts
Associates are subject to policies governing the conduct of their personal securities accounts. Refer to the
section Employee/Member and Employee/Member Related Accounts in the chapter GENERAL
EMPLOYEE/MEMBER POLICIES, as well as the chapter on INSIDER TRADING.

5.9.7 Authority
Associates may only act on behalf of T3TG within the boundaries of authority granted them by T3TG. Only
a Manager Member has the legal authority to bind the Firm.

                                  5.10 Business Continuity Plan
[FINRA Rule 4370, Notices to Members 06-74 and 04-37]

T3TG has developed a Business Continuity Plan to provide procedures for response and recovery in the
event of a significant business disruption. The purpose of the Plan is to identify responsible personnel in
the event of a disaster; safeguard associates' lives and firm property; evaluate the situation and initiate
appropriate action; recover and resume operations to allow continuation of business; provide
customer/members with access to their funds and securities; and protect books and records. The Plan
was developed considering the types of business conducted, systems critical to support business, and
geographic dispersion of offices and personnel.

5.10.1 Designation of Responsibilities
The following is a list of those responsible for T3TG’s Business Continuity Plan.

5.10.2 Retention and Location of the Plan
Copies of the current and prior versions of the Business Continuity Plan are retained as follows. Copies
are dated as of the effective date of the version of the Plan.

   •   A current hard copy is retained by Compliance with a record of the senior manager's approval.
   •   An electronic copy is retained by Compliance on the U Drive.
   •   Back-up copies are retained electronically via optical disk technology.

5.10.3 Implementation of the Plan
The Plan has been designed to be implemented in the event of a disaster that results in a significant
business disruption. Whether all or only parts of the Plan are implemented depends on the nature of the
disruption. Generally, a significant business disruption would include:
    • Destruction of one of T3TG ’s offices or facilities, whether by natural causes or by other means
    • Loss of life or major injuries to personnel in an office location that disables that office's ability to
        conduct business
    • Disruption of service from a critical service provider
    • Disruption of service due to wide-ranging regional outages such as a power outage

5.10.4 Emergency Response Team
T3TG has designated an Emergency Response Team that is responsible for implementing necessary
procedures included in this Plan. The Response Team's action will depend on the nature and scope of the
disruption. The "first responder" has the primary responsibility for taking action, and the "secondary
responder" acts as a back-up in the event the first responder is unable to act. Where feasible, the two
responders are located in different office locations.
