 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 158
 New Accounts                                                                                    09062023
Identity thieves use someone's personal identifying information to open new accounts and misuse
existing accounts. T3TG has established an Identity Theft Prevention Program (ITPP) to help detect and
prevent identity theft. Many elements of detecting or preventing identity theft are similar to anti-money
laundering (AML) requirements that are included in these policies. A more detailed explanation of the
Program is included in the section Identity Theft Prevention Program (FACT Act Red Flags Rule) in the
chapter ANTI-MONEY LAUNDERING (AML) PROGRAM.

The ITPP is based on identifying "red flags" that indicate identity theft may have occurred. It is the
responsibility of all associates to be alert and report to the AML Compliance Officer any new or existing
customer/members who may be engaged in violations of anti-money laundering regulations or identity
theft or who have reported identity theft.

8.2.16 Identity Theft and Money laundering Red Flags
The following two tables include "red flags" that are possible indicators of identity theft or money
laundering.
  Red Flags Indicating Potential Identity Theft
  Category: Alerts, Notifications or Warnings from a Consumer Credit Reporting Agency
  1. A fraud or active duty alert is included on a consumer credit report. An "active duty" alert is an alert
  a military person may add to his/her credit report to identify potential identity theft.
  2. A notice of credit freeze is given in response to a request for a consumer credit report.
  3. A notice of address or other discrepancy is provided by a consumer credit reporting agency.
  4. A consumer credit report shows a pattern inconsistent with the person's history, such as a big
  increase in the volume of inquiries or use of credit, especially on new accounts; an unusual number of
  recently established credit relationships; or an account closed because of an abuse of account
  privileges.
  Category: Suspicious Documents
  5. Identification presented looks altered or forged.
  6. The identification presenter does not look like the identification's photograph or physical
  description.
  7. Information on the identification differs from what the identification presenter is saying.
  8. Information on the identification does not match other information our firm has on file for the
  presenter, like the original account application, signature card or a recent check.
  9. The application looks like it has been altered, forged or torn up and reassembled.
  Category: Suspicious Personal Identifying Information
  10. Inconsistencies exist between the information presented and other things we know about the
  presenter or can find out by checking readily available external sources, such as an address that does
  not match a consumer credit report, or the Social Security Number (SSN) has not been issued or is
  listed on the Social Security Administration's (SSA's) Death Master File.
  11. Inconsistencies exist in the information that the customer/member gives us, such as a date of birth
  that does not fall within the number range on the SSA's issuance tables.
  12. Personal identifying information presented has been used on an account our firm knows was
  fraudulent.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 159
 New Accounts                                                                                  09062023
13. Personal identifying information presented suggests fraud, such as an address that is fictitious, a
mail drop, or a prison; or a phone number is invalid, or is for a pager or answering service.
14. The SSN presented was used by someone else opening an account or other customer/members.
15. The address or telephone number presented has been used by many other people opening
accounts or other customer/members.
16. A person who omits required information on an application or other form does not provide it when
told it is incomplete.
17. Inconsistencies exist between what is presented and what our firm has on file.
18. A person making an account application or seeking access cannot provide authenticating
information beyond what would be found in a wallet or consumer credit report, or cannot answer a
challenge question.
Category: Suspicious Account Activity
19. Soon after T3TG gets a change of address request for an account, we are asked to add additional
access means (such as debit cards or checks) or authorized users for the account.
20. A new account exhibits fraud patterns, such as where a first payment is not made or only the first
payment is made, or the use of credit for cash advances and securities easily converted into cash.
21. An account develops new patterns of activity, such as nonpayment inconsistent with prior history,
a material increase in credit use, or a material change in spending or electronic fund transfers.
22. An account that has been inactive for a long time is suddenly used again.
23. Mail T3TG sends to a customer/member is returned repeatedly as undeliverable even though the
account remains active.
24. We learn that a customer/member is not getting his or her paper account statements.
25. We are notified that there are unauthorized charges or transactions to the account.
Category: Notice From Other Sources
26. An outside agency, law enforcement, a clearing firms, or other source notifies T3TG that an
account has been opened or used fraudulently.
27. T3TG is notified of potential unauthorized access to customer/member personal information due
to data loss from an outside provider or a breach of an outside provider's data.
28. Notice from a customer/member of the loss of information (e.g., loss of wallet, birth certificate,
etc.).
Red Flags Indicating Potential Money Laundering
The customer/member exhibits unusual concern regarding T3TG’s compliance with government
reporting requirements and T3TG’s AML policies, particularly with respect to his or her identity, type
of business and assets, or is reluctant or refuses to reveal any information concerning business
activities, or furnishes unusual or suspect identification or business documents.
The customer/member wishes to engage in transactions that lack business sense or apparent
investment strategy, or are inconsistent with the customer/member's stated business strategy.
The information provided by the customer/member that identifies a legitimate source for funds is
false, misleading, or substantially incorrect.
Upon request, the customer/member refuses to identify or fails to indicate any legitimate source for
his or her funds and other assets.



Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 160
New Accounts                                                                               09062023
The customer/member (or a person publicly associated with the customer/member) has a
questionable background or is the subject of news reports indicating possible criminal, civil, or
regulatory violations.
The customer/member exhibits a lack of concern regarding risks, commissions, or other transaction
costs.
The customer/member appears to be acting as an agent for an undisclosed principal, but declines or
is reluctant, without legitimate commercial reasons, to provide information or is otherwise evasive
regarding that person or entity.
The customer/member has difficulty describing the nature of his or her business or lacks general
knowledge of his or her industry.
The customer/member attempts to make frequent or large deposits of currency, insists on dealing
only in cash equivalents, or asks for exemptions from T3TG’s policies relating to the deposit of cash
and cash equivalents.
The customer/member engages in transactions involving cash or cash equivalents or other monetary
instruments that appear to be structured to avoid the $10,000 government reporting requirements,
especially if the cash or monetary instruments are in an amount just below reporting or recording
thresholds.
For no apparent reason, the customer/member has multiple accounts under a single name or multiple
names, with a large number of inter-account or third-party transfers.
The customer/member is from, or has accounts in, a country identified as a non-cooperative country
or territory by the Financial Action Task Force (FATF).
The customer/member's account has unexplained or sudden extensive wire activity, especially in
accounts that had little or no previous activity.
The customer/member's account shows numerous currency or cashier’s check transactions
aggregating to significant sums.
The customer/member's account has a large number of wire transfers to unrelated third parties
inconsistent with the customer/member's legitimate business purpose.
The customer/member's account has wire transfers that have no apparent business purpose to or
from a country identified as a money laundering risk or a bank secrecy haven
The customer/member's account indicates large or frequent wire transfers, immediately withdrawn
by check or debit card without any apparent business purpose.
The customer/member makes a funds deposit followed by an immediate request that the money be
wired out or transferred to a third party, or to another firm, without any apparent business purpose.
The customer/member makes a funds deposit for the purpose of purchasing a long-term investment
followed shortly thereafter by a request to liquidate the position and transfer of the proceeds out of
the account.
The customer/member engages in excessive journal entries between unrelated accounts without any
apparent business purpose.
The customer/member requests that a transaction be processed in such a manner to avoid T3TG’s
normal documentation requirements.
The customer/member, for no apparent reason or in conjunction with other "red flags," engages in
transactions involving certain types of securities, such as penny stocks, Regulation "S" (Reg S) stocks,
and bearer bonds, which although legitimate, have been used in connection with fraudulent schemes

Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 161
New Accounts                                                                                09062023
 and money laundering activity. (Such transactions may warrant further due diligence to ensure the
 legitimacy of the customer/member's activity.)
 The customer/member's account shows an unexplained high level of account activity with very low
 levels of securities transactions.
 The customer/member maintains multiple accounts, or maintains accounts in the names of family
 members or corporate entities, for no apparent business purpose or other purpose.
 The customer/member's account has inflows of funds or other assets well beyond the known income
 or resources of the customer/member.

8.2.17 Notifying the AML Compliance Officer
If an Employee/Member identifies suspicious activity or red flags involving new or existing accounts, it is
the Employee/Member's obligation to immediately notify his or her supervisor and/or the AML
Compliance Officer. Supervisors are responsible for contacting the AML Compliance Officer if notified by
an Employee/Member of suspicious activity or red flags or when he or she personally identifies them.

8.2.18 SIPC Disclosure
[FINRA Rule 2266]

When new accounts are opened, new customer/members will be provided information about the
Securities Investor Protection Corporation (SIPC), including SIPC's website address and phone number.
This information is also provided annually in writing to all customer/members.

change.

8.2.21 Addresses on Customer/member Accounts
['34 Act Rule 17a-3(17) (i) (B) (2); FINRA Rule 3120(a) (2) (B)]

Acceptable addresses include:
   • for an individual: residential or business street address. If no street address exists or is available,
      an APO or FPO box number or the residential or business street address of a next of kin or another
      contact individual
   • for a non-individual (corporation, trust, etc.): a principal place of business, local office, or other
      physical location.
   • for a P.O. Box address: a legal address for the customer/member must also be provided. Please
      note, however, that T3TG does not generally allow P.O. Boxes to be used as a mailing address for
      customer/member accounts. An acceptable P.O. box may occur when the USPS does not deliver
      to the remote location. Compliance will need to verify this USPS information.

Accounts may not be addressed to T3TG, an RR or other Employee/Member of T3TG, with the exception
of accounts for the beneficial ownership of the RR or Employee/Member. Accounts may not be addressed
care/of (c/o) someone else, unless the customer/member provides written authorization requesting such
an address. As noted earlier, the Firm does not generally permit the use of a P.O. Box as a mailing address.

Address changes require written instructions from the customer/member. Upon notification of a change
of address, a notice will be sent to the customer/member's old address confirming that an address change

When the account is opened by a fiduciary, such as an investment adviser on behalf of the fiduciary's
customer/member, T3TG will provide either confirmations or periodic account statements to the
underlying beneficial account holder. T3TG will make a good faith effort to obtain the information
necessary to send confirmations directly to the beneficial owner; however, if this information is not
provided by the fiduciary, T3TG will forward confirmations to the owner's custodian or, if there is no
custodian or T3TG is the custodian, T3TG will send the confirmation directly to the fiduciary.

8.2.26 Associates of Other Broker-Dealers
When opening an account for a person employed by another broker-dealer (including accounts where
the Employee/Member has control or a personal financial interest), the other broker-dealer must be
notified. T3TG will provide duplicate confirmations, statements, or other information requested by the
employing broker-dealer.

8.2.27 Transactions Involving FINRA Associates
[FINRA Rule 2070]

When T3TG has notice that a FINRA Employee/Member has a financial interest in, or controls trading in,
an account, T3TG will obtain and implement instructions from the Employee/Member to provide
duplicate account statements to FINRA.

T3TG or its associates are prohibited from making a loan of money or securities to any FINRA
Employee/Member other than disclosed, routine banking and brokerage agreements (or loans originating
from a personal or family relationship).

T3TG or its associates cannot directly or indirectly give anything (other than something of nominal value
such as a logo hat, pen, etc.) to a FINRA Employee/Member who has responsibility for a regulatory matter
involving T3TG. "Regulatory matter" includes examinations, disciplinary proceedings, membership
applications and dispute-resolution proceedings.

8.2.28 Post Office Addresses
Generally. T3TG does not permit the use of P.O. Boxes. However, there may be instances where a P.O.
Box Is warranted, such as customer/members who reside in rural areas where mail is not delivered to the
home address and the post office address is the only address for mail delivery. In such instances, it will
be the responsibility of Compliance to confirm this mailing arrangement with the Post Office and record
the information with the new account documents.

8.2.29 Initial Deposits
If the initial transaction in a new account is a purchase order, a minimum deposit of $25,000.00 of the
value of the transaction is required for T3TG Retail accounts. For Prop Traders the initial minimum is
$5,000.00 and $2,500 thereafter. Management may allow for lesser initial amounts at its discretion. Prop



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 165
 New Accounts                                                                               09062023
Traders are permitted to make deposits of lesser amounts to cover debits once they leave the firm. The
Firm generally does not permit the use of foreign banks.

If the initial transaction is a sell order, receipt of the securities prior to entering the order is recommended,
particularly where the RR is unfamiliar with the new account. Potential items of concern include
certificates not registered in the seller's name, restricted or legend stock, expired warrants or rights, and
other securities that would not constitute "good delivery."

8.2.30 Unacceptable Accounts
The following are examples of accounts that are unacceptable. Questions regarding whether new
accounts may be opened should be referred to Compliance.

Unacceptable accounts include:

     •     Fictitious accounts in a name other than the name of the legal owner
     •     Accounts in the name of a minor
     •     Margin accounts for minors

                                       8.3 Transferring Accounts
[FINRA Rules 2140 and 11870]

8.3.1 Accounts Transferring In
When new accounts are transferred from another broker-dealer, a transfer form must be completed by
the customer/member authorizing the transfer and provided to the receiving firm. Most accounts transfer
via ACATS which expedites validation and transfer from the other BD.

Orders to sell securities to be transferred from the other firm may not be entered until validation is
received. RRs should contact Compliance to confirm whether the transfer has been validated.

8.3.2 Accounts Transferring Out
When validated instruction has been received to transfer a customer/member's securities account assets
to another firm, the account will be "frozen," i.e., all open orders (with the exception of option positions
that expire within 7 business days) must be canceled and no new orders taken.

T3TG and its associates may not interfere with a customer/member's request to transfer his or her
account unless there is a bona fide reason for doing so, such as a lien for money owed.


                                       8.4 Accounts and Securities Subject To Blocking
[Various Treasury Dept. regulations]

T3TG is prohibited from doing business in specific countries or with organizations or individuals the
subject of Government sanctions. The U. S. Treasury Department's Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC)
is responsible for administering and enforcing economic and trade sanctions. Sanctions target foreign

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                             Page 166
 Transferring Accounts                                                                            09062023
countries, terrorism sponsoring organizations, narcotics traffickers, money launderers, and other entities
and individuals. Sanctions include the freezing of assets and blocking securities issued by embargoed
foreign issuers.

As part of its anti-money laundering program, T3TG monitors accounts and securities included on OFAC's
lists, available at OFAC's website at www.treas.gov/ofac. OFAC information is updated whenever the
Government issues new sanctions that result in blocking requirements.

Blocking will occur under the following circumstances:
   • An account is opened for someone included on an OFAC list.
   • The owner of an existing account is added to an OFAC list.
   • A security is identified in a customer/member account where the issuer is the subject of blocking
       requirements.
   • A request is made by a customer/member to pay or transfer funds or securities to a blocked
       person or entity.

RRs will be notified if one of their customer/member accounts; a security held in one of their accounts;
or a requested transfer of funds or securities is blocked. In addition to the above, open orders will be
cancelled for any account that is blocked.

Compliance with blocking requirements is very important. Violations can result in substantial fines against
T3TG or persons engaging in prohibited transactions. Questions regarding blocked accounts, transfers, or
securities should be referred to Compliance.

                                            8.6 Margin Accounts
[Federal Reserve Regulation T; FINRA Rule 4210]

8.6.1 Opening Margin Accounts
Prior to engaging in margin transactions, the customer/member's account must have on deposit at least
$2,000 in cash or securities. Customer/members must sign T3TG’s margin agreement, which must be
received within five days from the first margin trade.
The clearing firms, acting as T3TG’s agent, shall carry customer/members’ margin accounts introduced by
T3TG and accepted by the clearing firm on a fully disclosed basis, Certain services will be performed by
T3’s clearing firms. They include:
a. Execute transactions in the customer/members’ accounts and release or deposit money or securities
   to or for the accounts, only upon T3TG’s instructions.
   Provide, electronically, confirmations and summary monthly statements to T3TG’s
   customer/members disclosing that the account is carried on a fully disclosed basis for T3TG.
b. Settle contracts and transactions in securities and securities futures contracts and options thereon
   (collectively referred to as “securities”) (i) between T3TG and other brokers and dealers, (ii) between
   T3TG and its customer/members and (iii) between T3TG and third persons.
c. Perform cashiering functions for such customer/members’ accounts, including receipt and delivery of
   securities purchased, sold, borrowed and loaned; remittance and receipt of payments therefor,
   provision of custody and safekeeping of securities and cash; and handling of margin accounts,
   dividends and exchanges, rights, warrants, redemptions, and tender offers with respect to such
   securities.
d. Mail to each customer/member a copy of the Notice to Customer/members notifying said
   customer/member in writing upon the opening of his account of the existence of the agreement and
   of the relationship between the introducing and carrying organization.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 168
 Margin Accounts                                                                            09062023
e. Complete the transfer of securities and accounts on behalf of customer/members.
f. Assist T3TG in seeking to ensure compliance with restricted and control securities under the Securities
   Act of 1933.
g. The clearing firm will furnish any written customer/member complaint it receives regarding T3TG, or
   T3TG’s associated persons, and relating to the responsibilities allocated to T3TG

T3 will utilize its back-office systems, Prop Reports and our proprietary risk monitor for monitoring margin
activity. These system will be monitored throughout the day by supervisory personnel at our main office.
Margin or day trade call will be sent by the clearing firms to T3 via email. The trade desk communicates
with the customer/member and acts accordingly. If necessary, the trade desk will cover or sell out
positions to meet a margin call.

8.6.2 Employee/Member Accounts
Extensions and prepayments are not permitted in Employee/Member accounts, except under
extraordinary circumstances and with approval of the designated supervisor.

8.6.3 Margin Disclosure Statement
[FINRA Rule 2264]

Under FINRA rules, all non-institutional customer/members who open a margin account must be provided
with a margin disclosure statement when the account is opened. T3TG's disclosure statement must be
included when the margin agreement is sent to the customer/member for signing. In addition, all margin
customer/members will be provided annually with a copy of the disclosure statement or an abbreviated
version of the statement. RRs are responsible for providing the initial disclosure statement; T3TG (or its
clearing firms, if applicable) will send annual disclosures. The margin disclosure statement also appears
on T3TG’s website.

Under this requirement, the term "non-institutional customer/member" includes all customer/members
except:

     •     A bank, savings and loan association, insurance company, or registered investment company.
     •     An investment adviser registered either with the SEC or with a state securities commission
     •     Any other entity (whether a natural person, corporation, partnership, trust, or otherwise) with
           total assets of at least $50 million

New Accounts is responsible for providing the initial disclosure document to new non-institutional margin
account customer/members.

T3TG’s clearing brokers, Velocity and ICBC Financial Services, LLC (“ICBC”) is responsible for providing the
annual disclosure documents.

8.6.4 Disclosure of Credit Terms
['34 Act Rule 10b-16; The Fair Credit Reporting Act]



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 169
 Margin Accounts                                                                              09062023
Upon opening a margin account, the customer/member will be provided a written statement explaining
the operation of a margin account and the calculation of interest charges on debit balances. It is the CCO's
responsibility to establish procedures, as needed, for providing the required disclosure.

The Compliance Department is responsible for providing credit disclosures to margin customer/members.

8.6.5 Equal Credit Opportunity Act Requirements
T3TG will not discriminate in the extension of credit to customer/members. Where credit is denied, T3TG
will provide information to the credit applicant in accordance with the provisions of the Equal Credit
Opportunity Act.

8.6.6 Arranging Credit
RRs are not permitted to assist a customer/member in making credit arrangements to purchase securities
outside T3TG, other than on terms consistent with those permitted by Regulation T and other rule
requirements.

8.6.7 Suitability
Margin accounts may involve more risk than cash accounts, depending on a number of factors, including
leverage used and types of transactions. As T3TG provides margin and day-trading related services, to the
extent applicable, as the Firm’s representatives make no recommendations, the RR is responsible for
determining the suitability of margin trading in a customer/member's account, including understanding
that the customer/member's investment objectives and financial profile are aggressive and consistent
with such activity.

8.6.8 Margin Requirements
Initial and maintenance requirements are available by contacting the Operations Manager.

8.6.9 Fiduciary Accounts
Margin transactions are permitted for accounts controlled by an administrator, conservator, custodian
(not including UTMA/UGMA accounts), executor, guardian or trustee as follows:

     •    when such person holds explicit power to engage in margin transactions
     •    after review of the appointment and applicable document (trust agreement, trust certification,
          will, etc.) explaining investment powers and approval by Compliance

RRs must submit the appropriate enabling document (trust agreement, etc.) to Compliance for review
and approval prior to engaging in margin transactions.
8.6.10 Portfolio Margin Accounts (PMA)
[FINRA Rule 4210, Notices 08-41, 07-14 and 07-11]


Portfolio margin is offered by T3’s clearing firm. The clearing firm will determine if the account qualifies
for portfolio margin. The clearing firm supplies to T3 the necessary documents for the client.



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 170
 Margin Accounts                                                                              09062023
Customer/members with portfolio margin accounts must be provided with a special written disclosure
describing the nature of, and risks associated with, portfolio margining. Customer/members must sign
and acknowledge that they have read and understand the disclosure statement and agree to the terms
of maintaining a portfolio margin account. Compliance is responsible for providing the disclosure and
obtaining signed acknowledgment, which will be retained in the customer/member's account file. There
documents are provided by the respective clearing agents and signed by the appropriate
customer/members.

Portfolio margining is a margin methodology that sets margin requirements for an account, based on the
greatest projected net loss of all positions in a product class or group, using computer modeling to
perform risk analysis using multiple pricing scenarios. It is applicable to all margin equity securities, listed
options, security futures products, unlisted derivatives, warrants, index warrants and related instruments.

There are eligibility requirements for customer/members (other than broker-dealers) which can vary by
clearing firm, but generally:
   •   Meet basic standards for having an account approved for uncovered writing
   •   Have and maintain at all times account net equity of at least $500,000 (waived for accounts solely
       limited to listed security futures contracts and listed single stock options) aggregated across all
       accounts under identical ownership at the clearing broker or other minimums established by the
       clearing firms providing such services.

Portfolio margining generally permits greater leverage resulting in greater risk for loss. The time limit for
meeting margin calls is shorter than for a standard margin account. T3 will monitor and review all trading
both real time and T+1. Margin calculations and calls are calculated by the clearing firm. Any issues are
communicated to T3 who contacts the client for funds or sell out. Compliance should be consulted for
more detailed information regarding portfolio margining requirements. All PMA applications are
submitted to T3TG’s clearing agent(s) for review and approval, if appropriate.

Customer/members who do not meet T3’s requirement of $500,000 in Equity will not be granted portfolio
margin

                                 8.7 Third Party Accounts
When a third party who is not the principal or named person on the account gives instructions regarding
orders, disposition of funds, or other actions involving an account, T3TG must have a signed third-party
trading authorization. The authorization is signed by the principal of the account and the third party,
giving the third party authority to act on behalf of the principal. An example of a third party account is an
account for a wife whose husband will give instructions regarding his wife's account. The signed trading
authorization must be received BEFORE accepting instructions from the third party.

There are generally two types of trading authorizations:
   • Limited trading authorizations limit the third party to giving instructions regarding the purchase
       and sale of securities and does NOT give authority regarding the disposition of funds or securities.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 171
 Third Party Accounts                                                                            09062023
     •    Full trading authorizations give the third party authority to give instructions regarding purchases
          and sales as well as the disposition of funds or securities in the account.

T3TG generally only permits limited trading authorizations for its customer/member accounts.

                                             8.8 Discretion for Orders and Accounts
[SEC no-action letters regarding cash management accounts (http://sec.gov/divisions/investment/noaction/ubs092905.htm) and family/related accounts
(http://sec.gov/divisions/investment/noaction/morganlewis111705.htm)]


T3TG does not permit discretionary accounts where the customer/member signs a discretionary trading
authorization and permits the RR to make decisions for the account without consulting with the
customer/member first. However, the Firm permits unsolicited orders to be taken on a “not held’ basis.

                                             8.9 Accounts for Minors
[Uniform Gifts to Minors Act; Uniform Transfers to Minors Act]

T3TG generally does not accept accounts for minors. Any exceptions must be approved by the Chief
Compliance Officer.

8.10.1 General Requirements
When opening and handling accounts for senior investors, there are certain considerations in addition to
usual account handling procedures. There is no benchmark for what constitutes a "senior" or "older"
investor, but generally these are individuals who are approaching or have achieved retirement.

8.10.2 Opening Accounts for Senior Investors
When opening accounts, the following should be considered when serving senior investors:

     •    encourage customer/members to identify a 3rd-party emergency contact and obtain permission
          to contact that person in the event there is an issue or event that requires clarification (such as
          the customer/member suffers diminished mental capacity in the future)
     •    indicate "retired" on the new account form to assist in evaluating the investor's status as someone
          potentially withdrawing from investments vs. accumulating assets
     •    obtain "lifestyle" information such as when the investor plans to retire, if not already retired; how
          much money will be needed after retirement; whether a dependent is supported by the investor;
          other expenses anticipated by the investor; the existence of a will and financial power of attorney

Accounts must NOT be opened for a senior investor if there is evidence of financial abuse or diminished
capacity; orders should not be accepted under such circumstances, either.

8.10.3 Recommendations to Senior Investors
[FINRA Regulatory Notice 07-43]


It is against T3TG’s policy to recommend or solicit any securities transaction to any investor, particularly
senior investors who are retired.

                                         8.11 Incompetent Persons
T3TG does not open accounts for incompetent individuals, even though the individuals may have proper
representation and others who are duly appointed to act on their behalf.

                                         8.12 Trust Accounts
New accounts for trusts require a copy of the trust agreement or a trust certification signed by the
authorized trustee. The following activities in trust accounts require prior approval as follows:
    • Margin trading requires approval by Compliance
    • Option trading requires approval by Compliance
    • Discretionary accounts require approval by Compliance (if T3TG permits discretionary accounts)
Fiduciaries (executors, trustees, guardians, administrators, conservators, etc.) may not be able to
delegate their duties to a third party (whether the RR or an outside person) to manage the account unless

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                               Page 173
 Incompetent Persons                                                                                09062023
the trust or other authorizing instrument specifically permits delegation. Some states require the fiduciary
to obtain a court order to delegate authority to a third party. The Trust Indenture or Agreement must be
reviewed by Compliance in advance of any transaction.

                                          8.13 High Risk Banking Accounts and Senior Foreign Political
                                               Figures
[USA PATRIOT Act Secs. 312 and 313; FinCEN Fact Sheet (http://www.fincen.gov/312factsheet.pdf); Dept. of Treasury 31 CFR Part 103, RIN 11506-AA29,
January 4, 2006 Federal Register]


Under anti-money laundering (AML) rules, there are special requirements that apply to "correspondent"
and "private banking" accounts as well as to accounts for "senior foreign political figures." Definitions of
these and other terms follow the summary of requirements. Questions should be referred to the AML
Compliance Officer.

8.13.1 Summary of Requirements
     •    Correspondent accounts require due diligence to determine ownership of the account.
     •    Accounts cannot be opened for foreign shell banks.
     •    When opening an account for a foreign bank, the Foreign Bank Certification form must be
          submitted to the AML Compliance Officer with the New Account Form for review and approval.
     •    Every three years foreign banks will be required to re-certify the information in the Foreign Bank
          Certification.
     •    When opening an account for a senior foreign political figure, the new account application must
          be submitted to the AML Compliance Officer for review and approval.

8.13.2 Definitions
Correspondent account: Includes any account established for a foreign financial institution to receive
deposits from, or to make payments or other disbursements on behalf of, the foreign institution, or to
handle other financial transactions related to such foreign financial institution. This type of account
presumes a formal relationship through which the financial institution provides regular services. Due
diligence requirements apply to correspondent accounts maintained for the following foreign financial
institutions:
     • Foreign bank
     • Foreign branch of a U.S. bank
     • Business organized under a foreign law that, if located in the U.S., would be a securities broker-
        dealer, futures commission merchant, introducing broker in commodities, or a mutual fund
     • Money transmitter or currency exchanger organized under a foreign law

Private banking account: A private banking account is an account that is established or maintained for
the benefit of one or more non-U.S. persons, requires minimum aggregate deposit of funds or other assets
of not less than $1,000,000, and is assigned to a bank Employee/Member who is a liaison between the
financial institution and the non-U.S. person. If the account otherwise satisfies the definition but the
institution does not require a minimum balance of $1,000,000, the account does not qualify as a private
banking account.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                                          Page 174
 High Risk Banking Accounts and Senior Foreign Political Figures                                                               09062023
Senior foreign political figure includes:

    •   a current or former senior official in the executive, legislative, administrative, military, or judicial
        branches of a foreign government, whether or not they are or were elected officials
    •   a senior official of a major foreign political party
    •   a senior executive of a foreign government-owned commercial enterprise (Senior executives are
        individuals with substantial authority over policy, operations, or the use of government-owned
        resources.)
    •   immediate family members of the above, and those who are widely and publicly known (or
        actually known) close associates of a senior foreign political figure
    •   a corporation, business, or other entity formed by or for the benefit of one of the above individuals

Proceeds of foreign corruption: any asset acquired by, though, or on behalf of a senior foreign political
figure through misappropriation, theft, or embezzlement of public funds, the unlawful conversion of
property of a foreign government, or through acts of bribery or extortion, and include any other property
into which any such assets have been transformed or converted.

Foreign bank: a bank organized under foreign law, or an agency, branch, or bank office located outside
the United States. The term does not include an agent, agency, branch or office within the U.S. of a bank
organized under foreign law.

Foreign shell bank: is defined as a foreign bank without a physical presence in any country.

Regulated affiliate: is a foreign shell bank that (1) is an affiliate of a depository institution, credit union,
or foreign bank that maintains a physical presence in the United States or a foreign country, as applicable;
and (2) is subject to supervision by a banking authority in the foreign country regulating such affiliated
depository institution, credit union, or foreign bank.

8.13.3 Prohibition against Correspondent Accounts for Foreign Shell Banks
T3TG is prohibited from establishing, maintaining, administering, or managing a correspondent account
in the United States for an unregulated foreign shell bank. The prohibition does not apply to a foreign
shell bank that is a regulated affiliate. If an account is inadvertently opened for an unregulated foreign
shell bank, the AML Compliance Officer should be notified and the account will be immediately closed.

8.13.4 Foreign Bank Certification
When opening an account for a foreign bank, T3TG is obligated to ensure the bank is not a foreign shell
bank and must obtain information about the foreign bank's owners and an agent for service of process.
The bank must complete the Foreign Bank Certification which must be submitted to the AML Compliance
Officer with a copy of the New Account Form for review. Every three years the bank is also required to
re-certify the information filed with T3TG.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 175
 High Risk Banking Accounts and Senior Foreign Political Figures                                 09062023
8.13.5 Accounts for Foreign Political Figures / Politically Exposed persons (“PEPs”)
Accounts for foreign political figures (as defined above) are subject to special reviews to comply with Bank
Secrecy Act requirements. If aware, RRs are required to identify, on the new account application, whether
an account for a foreigner falls under the definition. In addition, T3TG will submit the customer/member
name to BIG and/or comparable due diligence firm to determine if the customer/member is a foreign
political figure. If a customer/member is a foreign political figure, the application must be submitted to
the AML Compliance Officer for review and approval.

                                            8.14 Collateral/Escrow Accounts
T3TG will not maintain accounts for customer/members where the assets in the account are pledged to
a third party and T3TG is asked to acknowledge it is responsible for holding the assets pending instructions
from the third party.

                                            8.15 Pension and Retirement Accounts
[U.S. Dept. of Labor Retirement Plans website: http://www.dol.gov/dol/topic/retirement/index.htm]

T3TG does not permit the opening of pension or retirement accounts.

                                            8.16 Foreign Accounts
Accounts for residents of foreign countries may be subject to special requirements under securities laws
of the foreign country. Before opening an account for a person or entity residing in a foreign country,
contact Compliance for further information regarding any special restrictions or requirements. At this
time we do not accept Foreign accounts.

Referrals are an important part of business. Satisfied customer/members may refer others to T3TG and
may also seek referrals from associates when they need services not provided by T3TG. It is important
that referrals to others are based on sound knowledge about the other person or company. In addition,
FINRA rules restrict compensation to unregistered persons or entities.

Key requirements regarding referrals include the following:
   • Associates are expected to make referrals involving investments or investment advisory services
       only to persons or companies included in a Firm-sponsored program or on a list of Firm-approved
       providers.
   • Associates are prohibited from receiving compensation for referrals, except through Firm-
       sponsored programs.
   • Any proposed compensation, whether for referring or receiving referrals, must be approved in
       advance by Compliance.
   • Referrals involving compensation may require disclosure to the customer/member of potential
       conflicts of interest.
   • Non-cash compensation is subject to the policy described in the chapter ORDERS.

8.17.1 Referrals to Others
Associates may not accept compensation from someone outside T3TG for providing referrals.
Compensation may be paid by T3TG under Firm-sponsored programs. Referrals to Investment Advisers.

Referring customer/members to investment advisers for compensation may require T3TG (and possibly
the RR) to be registered as an investment adviser and investment adviser agent, respectively. If T3TG
has a program for adviser referrals, required registrations will be obtained and a list of approved
advisers will be provided to RRs. All advertising, sales material, marketing materials, scripts, and
proposed presentations related to investment adviser referrals must be approved by Compliance prior
to use.

Other than through Firm-approved referrals, RRs should avoid referring customer/members to
investment advisers unless Compliance has been contacted to make a determination regarding the



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 177
 Referrals                                                                                  09062023
registration status of the IA and to conduct other necessary reviews of the IA before making a referral.
Such activity would also require approval as an outside business activity.

8.17.2 Referrals to Hedge Funds or Other Outside Investments
RRs are expected to limit their investment recommendations to products or services offered by T3TG.
Referrals to outside investments not part of a Firm program are prohibited.

8.17.3 Referrals to Other Departments and Affiliates
Compensation paid by other Firm departments because of business referrals must be reviewed and
approved by Compliance in advance.

8.17.4 Referrals to the Firm
When others outside T3TG refer potential customer/members, referral fees or other compensation may
not be paid to the outside person or firm without prior approval by the CCO.

8.17.5 Referrals from Nonregistered Foreign Persons
FINRA rules provide exceptions (with specific requirements) for referrals from foreign persons who are
not registered. Such arrangements require prior approval by Compliance.

                                       8.18 Day Trading Accounts
[FINRA Rule 2130, 2360, 2361]

T3TG provides services to customer/members who trade their own accounts using T3TG’s services and
order entry facilities. The term "day trading account" as used in this section is limited to these types of
accounts. This section outlines requirements and procedures specific to day trading accounts. For
procedures regarding providing customer/member direct electronic access to NASDAQ, refer to the
chapter ORDERS and the section Customer/member Access to NASDAQ Order Entry Systems.

A day trading strategy is defined as an overall trading strategy characterized by the regular transmission
by the customer/member of intra-day orders to effect both purchases and sales in the same security or
securities.

8.18.1 Opening Day Trading Accounts
 Responsibility                 •   Compliance / Or Designee

8.18.2 Minimum Account Requirements
Prospective day trading accounts must meet the following minimum requirements:

For retail accounts:

     •    $25,000 equity
     •    2 or more years' experience in stock investments
     •    High risk tolerance
     •    Primary investment objective of speculation

8.18.3 Investment Objectives
Only customer/members with high risk tolerance and investment objectives consistent with high-risk
investing are permitted to open day trading accounts. In addition, day trading customer/members should
not have short-term needs for funds invested in day trading accounts and should be willing to risk loss of
their entire investment.

8.18.4 New Account Application
A new account application must be completed for each customer/member who will open a day trading
account. All information must be completed and the customer/member must sign the application.

8.18.5 Risk Disclosure Statement
[FINRA Rule 2361]

                 Prior to opening an account, T3TG will provide a risk disclosure statement to all non-
institutional customer/members. The disclosure is the "Day-Trading Risk Disclosure Statement" included
in FINRA Rule 2270 or may be a substantially similar alternative. "Institutional" customer/members
include: (1) a bank, savings and loan association, insurance company, or registered investment company;
(2) a registered investment adviser; or (3) any other customer/member (including natural persons) with
total assets of at least $50 million.

8.18.6 Approval Procedures
All day trading accounts must be approved by Compliance prior to commencement of trading. The
supervisor is responsible for the following:
    • Ensure the customer/member's new account application is signed and complete
    • Review the customer/member's account prior to approving for day trading, taking into
       consideration the following:
           o investment objectives
           o investment and trading experience and knowledge


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 179
 Day Trading Accounts                                                                       09062023
            o financial situation (including estimated annual income from all sources, estimated net
                worth exclusive of family residence, and estimated liquid net worth)
            o tax status
            o employment status (name of employer, self-employed or retired)
            o marital status
            o number of dependents
            o age
   •   Approve or reject the account for day trading
   •   If the account will not engage in day trading, ensure the customer/member has provided a written
       agreement ("Other-Use Agreement") that the customer/member does not intend to engage in
       day trading in the account

For accounts that will NOT engage in day trading, the customer/member may be approved without
qualifying for day trading if the customer/member signs T3TG’s Other-Use Agreement.

8.18.7 Accounts That Will Not Engage In Day Trading
Non-institutional accounts that do not meet the standards for day trading may be opened if the
customer/member does NOT intend to engage in day trading. For non-day trading accounts, the
customer/member must sign an Other-Use Agreement. To subsequently engage in day trading, the
customer/member's account must be re-approved and must meet the minimum account requirements
for day trading accounts. Typically, however, T3TG services accounts that qualify as “pattern day-trading”
accounts only.

8.18.8 Trading Controls
T3TG’s order-entry systems include controls to prevent rule violations. These controls are described in
this section. Associates are responsible for escalating any material systems issues to the firm’s
management to assure that system controls continue to operate properly.

8.18.9 "Other-Use" Accounts
T3TG monitors non-institutional accounts of customer/members who have signed agreements stating
they will not engage in day trading, to identify any such accounts that commence day trading. If an other-
use account becomes a day trading account, the account must be approved for day trading as outlined in
the section Approval Procedures under Opening Day Trading Accounts.

8.18.10 Long Sales
At the time of entry, sales marked long are compared to the customer/member's account to assure the
security is long in the account on the various order management systems that may be used.

8.18.11 Short Sales
               Upon entry of a short sale, the order entry system will: (1) determine if the security is
included on the Easy-to-Borrow List (“ETBL”) before permitting entry of the short sale order; and, (2) if
not on the ETBL, assure that a locate has been conducted in accordance with short sale rule requirements.



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 180
 Day Trading Accounts                                                                       09062023
8.18.12 Trading Limits
Each account is assigned dollar trading limitations. The order entry system will reject any order that
exceeds the assigned limit or a host of other risk-limiting metrics. The customer/member must request
an increase in limits from the designated supervisor. Any change will be documented in the system to
reflect approval of increased limits. Prior to increasing limits, the supervisor should consider the
customer/member's financial situation and trading history with T3TG.

8.18.13 Loss Levels
Each account may be assigned a loss level which triggers notice and possible expanded review of the
account by the designated surveillance specialist. Whenever an account exceeds the loss level, the
supervisor may wish to, consult with the customer/member (if appropriate), and determine whether to
raise the loss level or restrict future trading.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 181
 Day Trading Accounts                                                                       09062023
8.18.15 Advertising Day Trading Services
Advertising of day trading services is subject to the review process described in the chapter titled
COMMUNICATIONS WITH THE PUBLIC. Because of the special nature of day trading and regulators'
concerns regarding potential improper advertising, this section outlines areas of particular concern when
advertising day trading services.

8.18.16 Access to markets
Customer/members' access must not be overstated or imply access to "all" markets.

8.18.17 Disclosures regarding executions
Advertising cannot ensure "immediate" executions without disclosing that market volatility and volume
may delay system access and trade execution.

8.18.18 Disclosures regarding risks
References to the success of day trading should be balanced with discussion of the risks and costs of active
trading. There should be no implication that day trading is a simple strategy or that some level of return
is expected.

8.18.19 Comparisons of costs
Any comparison of the costs of day trading as compared to other forms of trading at other firms must be
reasonable and balanced.

8.18.20 Customer/member status
Statements must not imply that the customer/member is an Employee/Member of T3TG because they
are a "trader" or because they are using T3TG’s facilities to effect trades.

                                 8.19 Death of a Customer/member
When a customer/member dies, the account assets owned by the deceased person may become subject
to a will, estate laws, and other governing laws or documents. The assets are, therefore, frozen in the
account until necessary documents are received and legal distribution has been determined. Joint
accounts and other accounts where the deceased person is a joint owner with others may be subject to
certain distribution requirements depending on the styling of the account.

When a customer/member dies, the RR should:
  • immediately notify the Operations Manager or Compliance
  • consider assets in the deceased person's account as "frozen" until distribution of assets has been
      determined, i.e., accept no orders and do not authorize sending of securities or funds from the
      account
  • cancel all open orders

                                 8.20 Large Trades

 Death of a Customer/member                                                                   09062023
8.20.1 Introduction
T3TG has established procedures to detect, monitor and evaluate the risks of large trades and the
potential accumulation of large positions in accounts.

8.20.2 Extension of Credit
The Operations Manager or Compliance may establish higher margin requirements for accounts with
concentrated positions.

 Large Trades                                                                       09062023
9 ORDERS

This chapter provides policies and guidelines for the entry and handling of customer/member orders.
References to "order records" in this chapter includes manual order tickets and electronic order records,
where appropriate.

                                             9.1 Acceptance and Prompt Entry of Orders
Orders should be accepted only from the beneficial owner of an account or their authorized agent(s).
Authorized agents would include anyone holding third-party power to act on the customer/member's
behalf, such as a trustee, court-appointed guardian, authorized investment adviser, or other agent.
Orders accepted from an unauthorized third party may result in rescission of the transaction and assigning
the loss to the RR. For example, orders should not be accepted from a husband, on behalf of his wife's
account, unless the wife has signed a trading authorization giving her husband authority to act on her
behalf.
                                             9.2 Solicited and Unsolicited Orders

9.2.1 Definition of Solicited Order
When a transaction is recommended to a customer/member and the customer/member enters an order
as a result of that recommendation, the resulting order is considered to be solicited. Other actions that
may result in an order being deemed solicited include the mailing of a research report or other written
communication for the purpose of encouraging the customer/member to act on the information provided
or sending a prospectus on a new issue.

9.2.2 Solicited Orders Prohibited
T3TG strictly prohibits its associates from soliciting orders from its customer/members. T3TG
customer/members submit their orders on a wholly independent, unsolicited and self-directed basis. RRs
may not solicit or recommend any transaction.

                                             9.3 Fair Prices
[FINRA Rule 2121, Regulatory Notice 08-36]

T3TG is required to make a reasonable effort to obtain a price for the customer/member that is fair and
reasonable in relation to prevailing market conditions. In transactions (whether listed or unlisted)
between T3TG and a customer/member where T3TG sells from, or buys into, its own account, there is an
obligation to effect the transaction at a price that is fair considering all relevant circumstances, including
market conditions; expenses involved and reasonable profit for T3TG; and the expense of executing the
order and value of service provided.

9.3.1 Commissions
The designated supervisor is responsible for reviewing the reasonableness of commissions on agency
transactions. Relevant factors in determining the reasonableness of commissions may include:
    • the expense of executing and filling the customer/member's order
    • the value of the services rendered by T3TG

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 184
 Acceptance and Prompt Entry of Orders                                                          09062023
     •    the amount of any other compensation received by T3TG in connection with the transaction
     •    factors considered in principal transactions
     •    any other relevant factors at the time of execution

                                 9.4 Regulation NMS
Regulation NMS was adopted to strengthen the national market system for equity securities. It requires
markets to interact in a way that permits orders to seek the best available market. Rule 611, the Order
Protection Rule, is the primary rule that affects broker-dealers and requires broker-dealers to prevent
"trade-throughs."

9.4.1 Key Definitions
[Regulation NMS Rule 600]

Automated trading center: a trading center that:
   • has implemented systems, procedures and rules to display automated quotations in compliance
     with Regulation NMS;
   • identifies all quotations, other than automated quotations, as manual quotations;
   • immediately identifies its quotations as manual quotations whenever it has reason to believe it is
     not capable of displaying automated quotations; and
   • has adopted reasonable standards limiting when its quotations change from automated to manual
     quotations, and vice versa, to defined circumstances that promote fair and efficient access to its
     automated quotations.

Directed order: a customer/member order that the customer/member specifically instructs the broker
or dealer to route to a particular venue for execution.

Intermarket sweep order (ISO): a limit order for an NMS stock that meets the following requirements:
    • When routed to a trading center, the limit order is identified as an intermarket sweep order; and
    • simultaneously with the routing of the ISO limit order, one or more additional limit orders, as
      necessary, are routed to execute against the full displayed size of any protected bid, in the case of
      a limit order to sell, or the full displayed size of any protected offer, in the case of limit order to
      buy, for the NMS stock with a price that is superior to the limit price of the ISO limit order. These
      additional routed orders also must be marked "ISO."

An ISO is premised on the condition that the trading center or broker-dealer responsible for routing the
ISO will have attempted to access all better-priced protected quotations. This exception facilitates the
execution of certain types of orders, such as large block orders.

Market center: any exchange market maker, OTC market maker, alternative trading system, national
securities exchange, or national securities association.

NMS security: any security or class of securities for which transaction reports are collected, processed,
and made available pursuant to an effective transaction reporting plan, or an effective national market
system plan for reporting transactions in listed options.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 185
 Regulation NMS                                                                                09062023
Protected quotation: a quotation in an NMS stock that:
   • is displayed by an automated trading center;
   • is disseminated pursuant to an effective national market system plan; and
   • is an automated quotation that is the best bid or best offer of a national securities exchange
       (including NASDAQ) or the best bid or best offer of a national securities association.

To be protected, the quotation must be immediately and automatically accessible.

Trade-through: the execution of a trade as principal or agent during regular trading hours at a price
inferior to protected quotations.

Trading center: a national securities exchange or national securities association that operates an SRO
trading facility, an alternative trading system, an exchange market maker, an OTC market maker, or any
other broker or dealer that executes orders internally by trading as principal or crossing orders as agent.

[FINRA Notice to Members 04-66]

This section describes supervisory procedures to prevent errors when entering orders through order-
routing and execution systems ("trading systems"):
    • Only authorized persons are permitted to access trading systems. Access is password-protected.
    • Individuals authorized to route orders will receive training (i.e., demos), Q&As and User Manual
       (if available) to assure proper operation of the system.
    • Orders will be validated for accuracy and identification of duplicate or re-transmitted orders.
    • Limits on order entry include order size and/or credit limits.
    • Errors due to system failure, order entry errors, or other system-related errors will be corrected
       internally. The system will be tested daily. Testing connectivity to execution broker’s' systems will
       be conducted.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 186
 Trading Systems and Electronic Transmission of Orders                                          09062023
9.5.1 Supervision of Orders
The access system provides for oversight of orders to assure compliance with FINRA rules. The
customer/member access system provides for the following:

     •    Customer/members are provided password-protected access to ensure only authorized persons
          enter orders on behalf of the customer/member.
     •    Sell orders require an indication of "long" or "short."
     •    For long sales, the system will confirm the security is held in the customer/member's account at
          T3TG prior to entry.
     •    Short sales require that the security be available through the Easy-to-Borrow List or, if difficult to
          borrow, may be borrowed through a “locate” prior to order entry.
     •    Patterns of orders that are potentially manipulative are subject to surveillance and supervisory
          action.

                                            9.6 Order Records
['34 Act Rules 17a-3 and 17a-4; FINRA Rule 4510]

Certain information must be recorded for orders accepted by T3TG through its clearing agent(s).
Information to be recorded includes:
    • Identification of the account
    • Buy or sell
    • If sell, long or short
    • If a short sale, an indication the security can be borrowed
    • If sell long, an indication that the seller will deliver the security
    • If an option, put or call and open or close
    • Name of security
    • Quantity
    • Price (if a limit order)
    • Day or GTC (if not a market order)
    • Other terms of the order (fill or kill, stop limit, etc.)
    • If a discretionary account, notation whether discretion is exercised or not exercised (DE or DNE)
    • Identity of RR responsible for the account, if any
    • Identity of other person(s) who entered or accepted the order
    • Date and time order is received and entered

Other information to be recorded for the order includes:
   • If the customer/member's order is granted a stop (i.e., price protection on an order as negotiated
       by T3TG and the customer/member), the stop is to be noted on the order
   • Any modification to/cancellation of order or instructions
   • Execution price
   • Date and time of execution or cancellation
   • If the customer/member enters the order on an electronic system, a notation regarding electronic
       entry

                                         9.8 Sellouts
 Responsibility                •    Operations Manager or Designee
 Resources                     •    Notices from Operations regarding sellouts
 Frequency                     •    As required
 Action                        •    Sell out customer/member securities
 Record                        •    Sellouts are included in the day's order records

Customer/members who fail to pay for transactions will be subject to sellouts to close out the unpaid
security position. RRs will be charged for any unpaid balance remaining after the sellout.

A pattern of sellouts may indicate problems with customer/members or potential unauthorized
transactions. The designated supervisor is responsible for directly contacting customer/members where
there is a pattern of sellouts to determine the cause of the sellout and whether the customer/member
authorized the transaction. If unauthorized transactions or other wrongdoing are identified, it is the
designated supervisor's responsibility to take corrective action, including disciplinary action against the
RR. Compliance should be contacted for guidance regarding corrective action.

All time clocks (computer system or mechanical) used for recording date and time of orders must be
synchronized to ensure the accuracy of recorded time. The following procedures are to be followed to
synchronize time clocks used to record order entry and execution times:
    • All clocks must reflect Eastern Time (Daylight or Standard, depending on time of year).

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                                Page 189
 Sellouts                                                                                            09062023
   •   Clocks that have not been checked according to these procedures must not be used for recording
       order information.
   •   All clocks will be synchronized with the National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST)
       system clock each business day:
           o prior to the opening; and
           o at least once during the trading day (target time: 12:00 p.m. Eastern Time).
   •   Traders or other order desk personnel must record clock synchronization on the Clock
       Synchronization Log and provide the Log to the trading or order desk manager weekly
   •   Failure to conduct required synchronization may result in disciplinary action.
   •   The designated trading or order desk supervisor is responsible for reviewing and retaining the
       Logs.

Clock Synchronization is not done by T3TG, but through the various OMS utilized.

                             taken, if any
