                                 12.5 Option Operations Procedures
This section describes some of the operations procedures relating to options. The Operations Department
is responsible for these areas.

12.5.1 Reporting Positions
The designated operations supervisor is responsible for reporting option positions in accordance with SRO
requirements.

When T3TG is not a member of the options exchange upon which the standardized options are listed and
traded, T3TG is required to file a report with FINRA regarding the following accounts which have
established an aggregate position of 200 or more option contracts (whether long or short) of the put class
and the call class on the same side of the market covering the same underlying security or index:
    • each account in which the Firm has an interest
    • each account of a partner, officer, director or Employee/Member of the Firm
    • each customer/member, non-member broker, or non-member dealer account

Reporting combines long positions in put options with short positions in call options and short positions
in put options with long positions in call options. Position reporting is currently conducted by the Firm’s
clearing agent.

12.5.2 Position Limits
Customer/Members are subject to limits on how many contracts may be accumulated in a particular
option at any one time. Total positions include all accounts under "common control" by one party. An
example of common control would be a registered investment adviser who manages multiple accounts
and establishes option positions in accounts under the adviser's management. Position limits also include
accounts "acting in concert" to accumulate a position. T3TG (or its clearing firms or other firm executing
transactions on its behalf) has systems to prevent entry of orders that would violate position limits.

Some qualified hedge strategies and positions are exempt from position limits, including a delta hedging
exemption for FINRA members and non-member affiliates. The designated supervisor is responsible for
applying the Permitted Pricing Model to all positions in, or relating to, the security underlying the relevant
options position that are owned or controlled by such member or non-member affiliate and providing

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 232
 Option Operations Procedures                                                                   09062023
FINRA with written certification that T3TG or its affiliate are using the Permitted Pricing Model and that
the affiliate will provide immediate notice to T3TG if it ceases to hedge stock positions; T3TG is responsible
for carrying all option positions relying on the exemption.. T3TG is responsible for reporting any positions.
[FINRA Rule 2360(b) (A) (vii)]

The designated operations supervisor is responsible for identifying positions that exceed allowable limits
under SRO rules. When a position limit violation is identified, the SRO will be notified as required by rule
and the customer/member will be notified and asked to reduce the position to within the permitted
levels.

12.5.3 Liquidation of Positions and Restrictions on Access
When FINRA determines that a person or group of persons acting in concert holds or controls, or is
obligated in respect of, an aggregate position in option contracts covering any underlying security or index
in excess of position limits, it may direct a member or all members carrying a position in option contracts
covering such underlying security or index for such person or persons to liquidate such position or
positions, or portions thereof, as expeditiously as possible and consistent with the maintenance of an
orderly market, to bring such person or persons into compliance with the position limits. When directed
by FINRA, T3TG will liquidate positions and will not permit such person or persons to execute an opening
transaction and will not accept and/or execute for any person or persons named in such directive, any
order for an opening transaction in any option contract, unless in each instance express approval is given
by FINRA, the directive is rescinded, or the directive specifies another restriction appropriate under the
circumstances.
FINRA will notify the subject person or persons who have the right to appeal the decision.

12.5.4 Exercise of Options
The exercise of options is subject to regulatory rules; the method used for exercising options is disclosed
in the option agreement and all exercises are conducted by the Firm’s clearing agent.

The designated supervisor is responsible for assuring the exercise of options does not exceed limitations
specified in options rules. If T3TG has reason to believe someone acting alone or in concert with others
has exceeded or is attempting to exceed position or exercise limits, T3TG will promptly contact FINRA.

The exercise cut-off is established by regulators, or the Firm’s clearing agent may establish an earlier time
which is communicated to customer/members trading options. Exercise instructions will not be accepted
after the cut-off time. Submission of exercise instructions may be submitted electronically, in which case
the instructions will include an electronic record of the time it is submitted to comply with the cut-off
time. If not submitted electronically, a manual record will be maintained of the time when the exercise
instructions are submitted. Mistakes or errors in submitted exercise instructions will be reviewed and
resolved by Operations at T3TG or its clearing firms.

12.5.5 Branch Copies of Account Information and Statements
[FINRA Rule 2360(b) (17) (B)]




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 233

for approved options accounts and customer/member statements for the prior 6 months. These records
may be in electronic format if readily accessible and promptly retrievable.

[FINRA Rules 2210 and 2220]

Requirements for options communications include the following, which are explained in more detail
below:

       to the chapter titled COMMUNICATIONS WITH THE PUBLIC for more information about these
       policies.
   • Worksheet templates are considered sales literature.
   • Distribution of independently prepared reprints requires pre-approval by a ROP.
   • The exception for institutional sales material applies to options communications.
   • Widely-disseminated communications must be filed with FINRA.
   • All options communications (other than institutional sales material) must include a statement that
       supporting documentation for any claims (including claims on behalf of options programs or the
       options expertise of sales persons); comparisons; recommendations; statistics; or other technical
       data, will be supplied upon request.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                            Page 234
 Option Complaints                                                                               09062023
12.7.1 Definitions
Institutional investor:
    • bank, savings and loan association, insurance company, or registered investment company
    • registered investment adviser
    • entity (natural person, corporation partnership, trust, or other) with total assets of at least $50
        million
    • government entity
    • Employee/Member benefit plan under Internal Revenue Code Section 403(b) or 457 with at least
        100 participants
    • qualified plan under Section 3(a)(12)(C) of the '34 act with at least 100 participants
    • FINRA member or someone registered with a FINRA member firm acting solely on behalf of an
        institutional investor

Institutional sales material: Any communication that is distributed or made available only to institutional
customer/members (investors).

Options [FINRA Rule 2360(a) (20)]: Any put, call, straddle or other option or privilege, which is a "security"
as defined in Section 2(1) of the Securities Act of 1933 but not including any tender offer, registered
warrant, right, convertible security or any other option in respect to which the writer is the issuer of the
security which may be purchased or sold upon the exercise of the option.

Options Disclosure Document (ODD) [FINRA Rule 2360(a) (12)]: The options-market document
containing explanatory information relating to the mechanics of buying, writing and exercising options;
the risks involved; and other required information about options. The ODD must be provided to all options
customer/members and provided with certain options communications.

Public appearance: Any participation in a seminar, forum (including an interactive electronic forum),
radio, television or print media interview, or other public speaking activity, or the writing of a print media
article, concerning options.

Standardized Option: Any options contract issued, or subject to issuance, by The Options Clearing
Corporation, that has standardized terms for the strike price, expiration date, and amount of the
underlying security, and is traded on a national securities exchange registered pursuant to section 6(a) of
the Securities Act.

12.7.2 Content Standards
   •   All options communications (other than institutional sales material, defined below) must include
       a statement that supporting documentation for any claims (including claims on behalf of options
       programs or the options expertise of sales persons); comparisons; recommendations; statistics;
       or other technical data, will be supplied upon request.
   •   All options communications (other than institutional sales material) must include a statement that
       options are not suitable for all investors.

Communications including historical and/or performance figures or projections must be preceded or
accompanied by the Options Disclosure Document (ODD). For historical performance projections, specific
guidelines apply [see FINRA Rule 2220(d) (3) and (4)].

12.7.4 Institutional Sales Material
[FINRA Rule 2210]

Sales material prepared for institutional investors is not subject to the same requirements as material
prepared for non-institutional investors. This does not include material that the Firm has reason to believe
will be distributed to anyone who is not an institutional investor. For example, if sales material is provided
to an institution for distribution to associates participating in a 401K plan, the sales material would be
treated as distributed to retail investors.

Options institutional sales material must be approved by a ROP but does not require pre-use approval
and filing with FINRA.

   • Post-approval by designated supervisor: correspondence distributed to one or more existing
       retail customer/members and to fewer than 25 prospective retail customer/members within any

   • Pre-approval by designated supervisor: Correspondence distributed to 25 or more existing retail

   • Considered sales literature requiring pre-approval by a ROP: Correspondence distributed to 25
       or more prospective retail customer/members within any 30 calendar-day period

Written communications should avoid hedge clauses which disclaim responsibility for the content of such

suggesting the certain availability of a secondary market for options may not be made.

Written communications regarding options should include the following:
   • special risks of options and the complexities of certain strategies

12.7.7 Independently Prepared Reprints
Articles and other reprints are subject to outgoing correspondence reviews and copies should accompany
correspondence submitted for review and approval. If the article or reprint was provided by T3TG to RRs,
the letter or other correspondence may reference what was enclosed without including a copy of the
reprint.

Articles and reprints that are issued by an independent publisher and not materially altered are not
subject to FINRA filing requirements and most content standards. This exception does not apply to
reprints published by an affiliate of T3TG.

Distribution of copyrighted material is subject to the copyright holder's approval. T3TG will obtain
approval for independent reprints it distributes to RRs for sending to customer/members. For other
reprints, Compliance should be consulted for assistance in obtaining the publisher's approval.

12.7.8 Option Communications Prior to Delivery of the ODD
Prior to providing the ODD, communications are limited to a brief description of options including a
statement that identifies the registered clearing agency for options and a brief description of the general
attributes and method of operation of the option exchanges, including a discussion of how an option is
priced. Such pre-ODD communications must include contact information for obtaining a copy of the ODD
and must not contain recommendations or past or projected performance figures including annualized
rates of return or the names of specific securities. They may include statements required by state law and
administrative authority and may include advertising designs and devices, providing such material is not
misleading.

T3TG offers online trading services to its customer/members through T3TG’s Internet-based order entry

13.1.1 Security
T3TG uses encryption technology to maintain a secure website. Access is limited to users with valid
passwords and user names. In addition, internal access is limited to authorized personnel with designated
passwords. The OMS trading platforms used also provide encryption and other security measures
independently.

13.1.2 Capacity
T3TG will periodically review the system's capacity in view of customer/member/Member and market
activity to ensure adequate capacity is maintained to handle orders under different market conditions.

13.1.3 System Outages
When the online trading system is unavailable or capacity is diminished and limits
customer/members'/Members ability to enter orders, instructions will be communicated notifying
customer/members/Members of alternative methods for entering or revising orders. At all times,
customer/members/Members can contact T3TG’s trade desk for information and placing trades.

T3TG has established a contingency plan for dealing with system outages and failures.

 Orders                                                                               09062023
14 RISK MANAGEMENT CONTROLS
                                 14.1 Requirements
In order to reduce and eliminate the risk exposure of routing T3TG’s orders to various market venues
directly or through a sponsoring executing broker and/or third-party provider’s risk systems, T3TG has
implemented, through various approved arrangements with OMSs and its execution broker(s), certain
procedures and controls to establish pre-set limits on order flow, and to limit or eliminate financial risk
and the opportunity for erroneous orders being routed to market centers. Such controls are designed to
protect the firm, its Members, its introduced customer/members and clearing agent(s) - and adhere
and/or complement applicable regulatory requirements, such as SEC Rule 15c3-5 relating to risk
management controls for broker-dealers with market access, and will be used in conjunction with the
Firm’s licensed and/or third-party order entry system(s).

14.1.1 Order Routing Mechanisms / Ownership & Control
T3TG‘s customer/members utilize various order management systems (“OMS”) for the routing of orders
to market centers. The various OMSs are owned and operated through independent third parties and one
OMS is an affiliate with common ownership with T3TG. These OMS arrangements have been reviewed
and approved by the Firm’s execution broker, which has assured SEC Rule 15C3-5 compliance. With
respect to these various approved OMSs, the sponsoring execution broker and T3TG are notified of any
material systems issues directly through email or telephonically.

14.1.2 Direct Access Agreements
As T3TG is a not an active member of any exchange, it does not utilize its membership privileges currently
with any other exchange or ECN, and consequently does not route its orders directly unless through
routing/execution services agreements. To the extent that an execution broker agrees to provide T3TG
with routing/execution services directly to a market venue(s) to which it is a member, the Firm will assure
that all appropriate agreements are executed in accordance with the execution broker wishes and the
regulatory requirements of the market center.

14.1.3 Certification

T3TG will assure, if within its power, that T3TG will test the OMSs risk management and order flow
controls through the third-party vendors, to certify that such systems controls are appropriately designed
and operationally adequate to limit or reduce risk consistent with regulatory requirements imposed by
SEC Rule 15c3-5. T3TG’s CEO will assure that T3TG, its affiliates or any third-party vendors associated are
utilized and cooperate with the execution broker to assure prompt and accurate testing of the system to
assure compliance with the requirements imposed by SEC Rule 15c3-5.

14.1.4 Monitoring

T3TG will also assure that T3TG has the ability to monitor the systems order-flow on a real-time or near
real-time (via drop copies or systems access) basis to assure T3TG’s operational efficacy and risk
management controls, and to otherwise monitor or gauge order flow and buying power limitations or


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                        Page 243
 Requirements                                                                                09062023
other restrictions T3TG has pursuant the requirements of SEC Rule 15C3-5 as well as all other regulatory
requirements.

                                 14.3 Order Management Systems

14.3.1 Identification Requirement

T3TG does not permit any individual to enter orders through any OMS or through any other mechanism
or access the markets without that individual providing proper identification (and, as required, other
information), and receiving approval and authorization to route orders to T3TG’s execution broker(s).

14.3.2 System Security
To assure the integrity and security of the order routing process, each OMS requires that each trader is
issued a unique “trader identification code” and “password.” Such devices are designed to properly
identify from whom orders originate, prevent unauthorized access, and use and assure that all orders are
subject to the Firm’s risk management controls. After the issuance of the initial password, each trader
may be prompted to create their own personalized password prior to accessing the system to assure a
high level of security pursuant to the password protection policy of the OMS. Traders may then
periodically change their passwords at the user’s own election, depending on the specific security
procedures at each OMS. The issuance of these security devices assures that the OMS will only recognize
and permit order entry and other defined access to an individual who has been duly authorized by the
Firm and has a password which links to his/her unique trader identification code.

14.3.3 Risk Management Pre-Trade Controls
T3TG utilizes multiple pre-trade risk control metrics to assure that its order flow directed, either through
the pre-trade risks systems of its execution broker(s) or routed directly to market venues pursuant to a
sponsored access agreement with its execution broker, are reasonably designed to systematically limit
the financial exposure of the firm, assure compliance with regulatory requirements, particularly SEC Rule
15c3-5, which governs risk management controls for brokers or dealers with market access, and limit or
reduce other risks. These risk controls are effectuated through the use of the various OMSs and back-end
systems and also applies to any API-based application. These controls operate when routing orders
through an executing broker (and their independent risk controls) or directly to a market center through
a sponsored agreement.

14.3.4 Pre-trade Risk Controls
The OMSs utilized by T3TG clients have implemented a number of risk controls that limit or eliminate the
opportunity for the routing of erroneous orders, limit risk exposure and adhere to regulatory
requirements governing order flow and executions. The risk controls generally preclude traders from
entering orders if the order(s) would breach any number of predefined limits. Illustrations of pre-trade
risk controls that are utilized by one or OMS include:
    a. *Maximum Exposure – predefined limit of the amount of buying power that may be utilized
        during a trading day.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 244
 Order Management Systems                                                                     09062023
   b. Maximum OCC Requirement – predefined limit of the OCC haircut that may be utilized during a
      trading day.
   c. *Maximum Order Size – predefined limit of the maximum order size that may be routed to a
      market.
   d. * Maximum Position Size – predefined limit of the amount shares of exposure a trader may have
      in any one security position.
   e. *Position Multiplier – predefined limit of the dollar amount of exposure a trader may have in any
      one security position.
   f. *Maximum Gross Loss – predefined maximum gross loss (realized and unrealized) that a trader
      may reach before they are no longer permitted to route new opening orders.
   g. *Maximum Open Orders – predefined number of open orders a trader may route during a day. If
      the limit is breached, the trader is not permitted to route additional orders.
   h. *Maximum Number of Positions - predefined number of open positions a trader may have during
      a day. If the limit is breached, the trader is not permitted to route orders in a new security.
   i. *Maximum Stock Price - predefined maximum limit price of any stock that may be entered by a
      trader may have during a day. The trader may not enter a price for a stock that is higher than the
      predefined limit.
   j. *Minimum Stock Price - predefined minimum limit price of any stock that may be entered by a
      trader may have during a day. The trader may not enter a price for a stock that is lower than the
      predefined limit.
   k. *Auto Closeout Gross Loss - predefined maximum gross loss that may be taken before all position
      are liquidated or closed out.
   l. *Auto Closeout Equity - predefined equity limit below which all position are liquidated or closed
      out.
   m. Dynamic Buying Power – This buying power changes with changes in the account equity during
      the day.
   n. *Stock Filter - predefined symbols in which a trader may enter orders. Symbols not included would
      be restricted from order entry.
   o. Auto Trim – adjusts a trader’s exposure to keep within buying power parameters. When an account
      is dropping in value and has dynamic buying power enabled, as the dynamic buying power is
      reduced, there is a concomitant reduction is open positions, so that exposure does not exceed the
      dynamic buying power.
   p. Master Control – this assures that the aggregate buying power for the firm cannot be exceeded.
       *Share basic risk metrics on each OMS.


14.3.5 Compliance Controls
In addition to assuring that certain pre-trade risk metrics are incorporated into the various OMSs, there
are also certain compliance controls or restrictions to assure that orders are subject to, and compliant
with, certain material regulatory requirements governing trading. These compliance controls include:


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                      Page 245
 Order Management Systems                                                                  09062023
    a. Order Marking – pursuant to the order marking requirements of Rule 200 of Regulation, T3TG
       assures that OMSs mark orders in accordance with their tenor for each trader (i.e., long sale or
       short sale.).
    b. Locate Requirements – pursuant to Rule 203(b) of Regulation, T3TG assures that the OMSs block
       any attempted short sale of a “hard-to-borrow” security without a bona fide locate. In addition,
       the OMS will not permit the shorting of any security beyond the quantity of locates received by
       the trader.
    c. Clearly Erroneous – the provision will not permit a buy or sell limit order to be sent to the market
       if it was to result in an unfavorable execution at a price exceeding the NBBO by a certain
       percentage (e.g., 10%).
    d. Wash Sales – the OMSs may not permit a trader to effectuate a wash sale with themselves by
       routing buy and sell orders with the same limit price to the same venue. To the extent blocks are
       not active, an exception report will identify any potential exceptions within certain parameters.
    e. Time Synching – each OMS will calibrate its internal clock used for logging order flow with the
       Atomic Clock periodically throughout each day to assure the OMS’ time is accurate.
    f. Time-in-Force – each OMS assures that orders are marked with the appropriate time-in-force
       supported by the system (i.e., day, IOC etc.).

                                    14.4 Administrative Controls (entitlements and security)
T3TG has assured that only individuals duly authorized receive the ability to route orders, receive market
data or other financial data (i.e., charting etc.) or have administrative powers to monitor trading of
specific groups of traders within the Firm. As noted earlier, trader identification codes and passwords
prevent unauthorized access to the OMS for traders. These devices also prevent individuals from
accessing information, such as charting or trading or market data that has not been paid for or authorized
by senior management.

                                    14.5 Daily Testing
Each OMS undergoes daily testing prior to the use of the platform for that trading day in order to assure
that the risk control and other metrics are fully functional and operational. Any issues relating to the
systems’ risk management functionality are reported to T3TG’s Technology Operations Officer
immediately via email.

                                    14.6 Risk Monitoring
T3TG has developed certain risk management redundancies to assure that there are different reviewers
of trading activity throughout each trading day in order to detect significant trader losses or other unusual
activity and take appropriate remedial actions. Risk Management is conducted through an administrative
feature on the various OMSs which permit duly authorized individuals to monitor orders, executions and
performance of customer/members and traders.




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 246
 Administrative Controls (entitlements and security)                                           09062023
As part of this daily risk management effort, T3TG has designated at least two RRs dedicated to risk
management. The risk manager is responsible for reviewing the profitability and other issues affecting
trading daily. T3TG’s risk managers also work closely with branch managers and other designated risk
management supervisors, if applicable, to assure that any material trading or other risk issue is brought
to the attention of senior management and addressed appropriately.

In addition, Branch Managers and other designated risk managers may also be permitted certain risk
management responsibilities regarding their traders only. These individuals are required to work closely
with the home office and their respective Branch Manager to assure that risk issues are identified,
brought to senior management’s attention and resolved in an expeditious fashion.

T3TG also works closely with its execution broker(s) and clearing agent(s) to assure that order and
execution detail are available, and that any limits or restrictions can be monitored appropriately.

                                 14.7 Annual Audit and CEO Certification
To assure the continuing efficacy of T3TG’s OMS risk management controls, the Firm requires that the
risk controls are audited formally each year by a third-party agent of the execution broker(s), as provided
under SEC Rule 15c3-5. The audit shall assure that the risk management and other controls are functioning
as designed and fully operational. In addition, the audit shall be designed to provide recommendations
to improve the current system or expand its functionality. The CEO shall request a copy of the report from
the execution broker.

The CEO shall review the audit report and conduct any other reviews of the system he may deem
warranted as part of his annual assessment of the Firm’s risk management and supervisory systems
controls with respect to market access. The CEO shall then on an annual basis certify, as part of the annual
certification regarding the adequacy of the Firm’s risk management and supervisory controls. Such
certification shall be in writing and maintained as part of the Firm’s books and records in a manner
consistent with SEC Rule 17a-4(b).

15.6.1 Orders
Orders entered by RRs/Members must conform to applicable FINRA rules. Each RR/Member should
familiarize himself with the rules for the specific type of order before engaging in the order. Orders shall
be entered on the trading system utilized by the RR/Member or through the trade support desk (home
office). No order shall be entered unless it bears all the account numbers for which the order is being
entered. Transactions shall not be booked to another RR/Member after execution without the approval
of the appropriate supervisor, and only where the RR/Member ultimately receiving the trade is in fact the
RR/Member for which the order was initially intended.

15.6.2 Regulation SHO
The following sets forth the policies and procedures of T3TG relating to compliance with Regulation SHO
(“Reg SHO”) under the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 (the “Exchange Act”). The requirements set out in
these policies and procedures only apply to transactions in securities subject to U.S. short sale regulation,
which generally includes all equity securities traded in the United States. (Sales of non-U.S. securities
traded in the United States through American Depository Receipts (“ADRs”), as well as exchange traded
funds (“ETFs”), are covered by these policies and procedures. In addition, securities convertible into
equity securities are considered equity securities and, accordingly, are also covered). Unless specifically
noted, sales of straight debt issues, commodities or other non-securities instruments, and sales of non-
U.S. securities traded abroad, are not subject to this Policy.

15.6.3 Short Sale Definition, Ownership, Net Positions and Aggregation Units
Definition of “Short Sale”. Rule 200 of Regulation SHO defines a “short sale” as any sale of a security
which the seller does not “own” or any sale which is consummated by the delivery of a security borrowed
by, or for the account of, the seller. A “short sale against the box” is an example of a sale that must be
considered short even though the seller owns the security because the sale is settled with borrowed stock.

Ownership. A person “owns” a security for purposes of Reg SHO if:
         • the person or his agent has title to it;
         • the person has purchased, or has entered into a binding and unconditional contract to
             purchase it, but has not yet received it;
         • the person owns a security convertible into or exchangeable for it and has tendered such
             security for conversion or exchange;
         • the person has an option to purchase or acquire it and has exercised such option;
         • the person has rights or warrants to subscribe to it and has exercised such rights or
             warrants; or
         • the person holds a security futures contract to purchase it and has received notice that the
             position will be physically settled and is irrevocably bound to receive the underlying
             security.

Net Position. Under Rule 200(c) of Reg SHO, in order for a person to be deemed to own a security, not
only must the person “own” the security as defined above, but the person also must have a net long

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 249
 Trading                                                                                       09062023
position in the security. For example, if a broker-dealer that does not have Aggregation Units (as discussed
more fully below) has positions in a security in two accounts (e.g., short 1,000 shares in one account and
long 300 shares in another account), those positions must be netted to determine the broker-dealer’s net
position (i.e., 700 short) such that any sales must be marked short even if only selling the 300 share long
position in the separate account.

Aggregation Units. As seen above, ownership of a security is discussed in the context of a “person.” A
“person” may be a natural person or a legal entity such as a corporation, LLC, partnership, etc., including
T3. Under certain circumstances, however, a broker-dealer that would normally be viewed as a single
“person” may be divided into separate units (“Aggregation Units”) and short sale regulations applied at
the Aggregation Unit level. T3 has adopted an Aggregation Unit Plan, so each T3 Aggregation Unit is
separate for purposes of Reg SHO.
By establishing Aggregation Units, each Aggregation Unit is able to execute a sale of a security based upon
that Aggregation Unit’s own positions without considering T3’s overall position or the positions of other
T3 Aggregation Units. Rule 200(f) of Reg SHO sets forth 4 requirements for maintenance of Aggregation
Units:
           • maintain a written plan of organization that identifies each Aggregation Unit, specifies its
               trading objective(s), and supports its independent identity;
           • each Aggregation Unit determines, at the time of each sale, its net position for every
               security that it trades;
           • all traders in an Aggregation Unit must pursue only the trading objective(s) or strategy(s)
               of that Aggregation Unit and MUST NOT coordinate that strategy with any other
               Aggregation Unit; and
           • a trader must be assigned to only one Aggregation Unit at any time.

15.6.4 Plan for Independent Aggregation Units
This section of T3’s short sale policies and procedures constitutes T3’s Aggregation Unit Plan. T3 considers
each of its proprietary traders, or under certain limited circumstances, a group of traders, to be an
independent Aggregation Unit of T3. T3 views the treatment of each Aggregation Unit, as a separate unit
as appropriate because the trading activity in each Aggregation Unit’s accounts is directed only by that
Aggregation Unit’s Members based upon its trading decisions. Furthermore, each Aggregation Unit’s
profit and loss is determined solely based upon the activity in its Aggregation Unit to assure that each
Aggregation Unit’s sole monetary motivation for its trading decisions is the performance of its
Aggregation Unit and not that of any other Aggregation Unit.

15.6.5 Supervision of Aggregation Unit Requirements
The Chief Compliance Officer is responsible for maintaining/updating T3’s Aggregation Unit Plan as
necessary. Upon acceptance as a member of the T3, and annually thereafter, each proprietary trader
must sign an attestation affirming their agreement and understanding of T3’s procedures to ensure
compliance with Regulation SHO.

In general, Class B Members do not have the ability to execute transactions, or direct that others execute
transactions, in the accounts of any Class C Member under any circumstances, unless the Class B Member
is concurrently also a Class C Member, in which case he or she shall be able to execute transactions in the

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                         Page 250
 Trading                                                                                      09062023
trading account in his or her name. Class B Members can, however, make recommendations regarding
associated Class C Members’ trading with respect to risk limits or other risk mitigation strategies to
appropriate T3 compliance or risk management personnel. The implementation of any such
recommendations are subject to the sole discretion of such compliance or risk management personnel.
Risk limits can only be changed if approved by the Chief Executive Officer, Chief Operating Officer or
delegated supervisor.

Class B Members do not have access to view individual trade activity or individual securities positions held
by associated Class C Members on an intraday basis unless they are also a Supervisor. Otherwise, Class B
Members only have access on a T+1 basis to their associated Class C Members.

The Compliance department reviews electronic communications for inappropriate communications
between Class B & C Members to help ensure that Class B Members only discuss general trading
strategies, as opposed to specific trades or trading activity, with associated Class C Members.

In certain circumstances, groups of traders who wish to coordinate trading strategies may request
designation as a single Aggregation Unit, sometimes referred to as a “multi-account aggregation unit”, in
which case the restrictions on coordination of trading described above shall not apply to communication
between the members within the designated Aggregation Unit. In all cases, designation of groups of
traders as a single Aggregation Unit shall be subject to the sole discretion of the Chief Compliance Officer.

In order to be sure that the trading platforms are in synch with Clarity (the software utilized by the firm
to ensure that all orders are marked correctly before going to the markets), a daily exception report has
been created to show any breaks between the systems. This report is reviewed by the Trade Desk on T+1
and any corrections are made before the opening of the market to ensure all positions are matched
correctly.

15.6.6 Marking of Sale Orders
Marking Requirement. Rule 200(g) of Reg SHO requires all sale orders, with respect to both equity and
debt securities, to be marked either “long” or “short.” The SEC eliminated the “short exempt” order
designation in July 2007 when the SEC eliminated the tick and bid tests. There is no special order marking
designation for short sales that are exempt from the “locate” requirement discussed below. An order to
sell may be marked “long” only if: (a) the seller “owns” the security and (b) the security is either:
           • in the physical possession or control of the broker-dealer, or
           • is reasonably expected to be in the physical possession or control of the broker-dealer by
               settlement date. All sales ineligible to be marked “long,” must be marked as “short.”

Supervision. T3’s third party trading platforms and other tools calculate net long/short positions on a
real-time basis to prevent an Aggregation Unit to marking an order inconsistent with such position. To
detect instances where a trader has violated order marking rules, the Company generates daily exception
reports that it reviews.

Written Supervisory Procedures Acknowledgement Form. As part of the onboarding process each new
RR/Member will be required to fill out the Written Supervisory Procedures Acknowledgement form. As

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 251
 Trading                                                                                       09062023
part of this form and as an addition to the above stated aggregation unit plan, each RR/Member will be
required to identify if they are a Proprietary Trader or not. If they identify themselves as a Proprietary
Trader they will be required to provide a detailed written description of their individual strategy or be
designated as part of a multi-account aggregation unit.

15.6.7 Clearance & Settlement
Clearance and settlement is provided by the Clearing Companies. Each RR/Member is responsible for

account due to activity, which occurred the prior day, should be addressed before the market opens the
following day.

15.6.8 Account Trading Parameters
Upon opening a new account the Managing Member Sean Hendelman, or a delegate in his absence, will
set the trading parameters of the account. The compliance staff and the Supervisors will monitor the
trading in each account against the trading parameters of the account. Trading activity that deviates from
the trading parameters of the account without due cause or approval by the Managing Member, CCO or
delegate, may result in the suspension of said account. Violating these procedures may result in fines,
suspensions or termination of the RR/Member in question. For further information please look under Risk
Management.

15.6.9 Short Sales & Affirmative Determination
T3TG follows the Blanket Assurance Policy in regards to listed securities and securities trading on NASDAQ
in compliance with Reg. SHO, FINRA Rule 4320 by obtaining EASY TO BORROW lists from its Clearing
Companies and locates from stock locate vendors on a daily basis. If an RR/Member enters a short sale
order that is not on the Easy to Borrow list and does not have a locate for that trading day, the trading
platform will reject the order with a prompt directing the RR/Member to get a locate by contacting the
T3TG Trade Desk.

15.6.10 Locate and Delivery Requirements
[SEC Regulation SHO, Rule 203]

Prior to affecting a short sale in an equity security, there is an obligation to "locate" securities available
for borrowing and delivery by settlement date. The "locate" must be determined prior to order entry.
Exceptions to this requirement are listed below.

It is the Associated Person's responsibility to contact Operations (or consult a "borrow" list if available) to
confirm whether the security being sold short may be borrowed. The borrow list relied upon must be less
than 24 hours old. The following locate information must be recorded on the order:
             • If obtained from a person, the name of the person and the number of shares to be
                 borrowed and the date; or
             • If obtained from an authorized "borrow list," notation that the list was consulted and the
                 date.
             • If affirmation is obtained from someone outside T3, the order record must also include the
                 name of the person's employer.

 Trading                                                                                        09062023
15.6.11 Stock Locate Vendor
If an RR/Member is using a trade system operated by or through the Clearing Companies, they must
coordinate with T3TG to obtain a locate through approved locate vendors. Once a valid locate is received,
T3TG will provide the Clearing Companies’ with evidence of the locates by the end of the day. Once the
pertinent information is communicated to the authorized personnel, they shall enable access to the
located stock using an override function.

The locate information required to process the orders stated in this section must include the present
location of the securities in question, whether they are in good deliverable form and the locate vendor’s
ability to deliver them to T3TG’s Clearing Companies within two (2) business days.

Some trading platforms have a Locate Injector, which allows a RR/Member to enter the symbol in the
Locate Field and request a locate directly from the locate vendor. If a locate is obtained, the locate injector
will automatically enable access to the located stock using an override function.

To ensure that T3TG exercises diligence in its supervision of short sale violations, T3TG will generate
exception reports on a daily basis to be reviewed by a designee of the Compliance Department. As there
are various ways in which short sale violations can occur that cannot be addressed through programming,
T3TG shall review this report to strengthen its review for short sale violations. If a violation is detected,
the designee shall contact the RR/Member and take appropriate action as deemed necessary.

15.6.12 Odd Lot Trading
NYSE, ARCA and the Amex have modified order entry rules concerning Odd Lot Trading. No longer will it
be advantageous to trade odd lots as the new rules eliminate automatic execution requirements on the
odd lot portion. It should be noted that despite the rule change that the regulators and T3 will still be
monitoring odd lot trading for the appearance of any still in place restrictions and generally, odd lot
trading is not considered a professional trading strategy and is discouraged. The following restrictions are
still in effect:

   •   Unbundling round-lots for the purpose of entering odd-lot orders in comparable amounts;
   •   Failing to aggregate odd-lot orders into round lots when such orders are for the same account or
       for various accounts in which there is a common monetary interest;
   •   Entering both buy and sell odd-lot orders in the same stock before one of the orders is executed,
       for the purpose of capturing the “spread” in the stock; and
   •   Other types of trading activity in odd-lots that is not consistent with traditional odd-lot investment
       activity.

As abusive odd-lot practices violate such requirements and may also constitute a failure to adhere to
principles of good business practice, fraud or fraudulent acts, conduct inconsistent with just and equitable
principles of trade, T3 has implemented a policy to facilitate RR/Member compliance with such rules. T3
has elected to implement a policy as follows for odd-lot trading violations:




 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 253
 Trading                                                                                        09062023
1st Violation: Initial Written Warning
2nd Violation: A Monetary Fine of $25
Subsequent Violations: A Minimum Monetary Fine of $25 per violation, but will be increased if
determined appropriate by the CCO.

All profits derived from improper odd lot transactions are subject to disgorgement. RRs/Members who
are repeat violators risk suspension or termination of their trading privileges.

15.6.13 Partial Round Lot Trading (“PRL”)
NYSE, ARCA and the Amex have modified order entry rules concerning partial round lot trading (“PRL”).
No longer will it be advantageous to trade PRL’s as the new rules eliminate automatic execution
requirements on the odd lot portion. It should be noted that despite the rule change, regulators and T3
will still be monitoring PRL’s for the appearance of any still in place restrictions, but otherwise PRL trading
will be allowed. The following restrictions are still in effect:

a) Effecting prearranged or wash sales in the round-lot portion of a PRL for the purpose of effecting an
execution of the odd-lot portion of the PRL with no market risk and at a more favorable price than market
conditions would have otherwise permitted. For example, when the quote in XYZ is .05 - .15, an
RR/Member simultaneously bids .06 for 199 shares and offers 100 shares at .06. This would cause 100
shares to execute at .06 (this would be a prohibited wash sale), which would in turn force the specialist
to sell to the RR/Member the remaining 99 shares at .06, thereby generating a riskless odd-lot trade at a
better price for the odd-lot than the market at the time would have provided.
b) Other types of trading activity in PRLs that is not consistent with traditional odd-lot investment activity,
such as a routing multiple PRL orders in close time proximity to each other, or any pattern of activity that
would suggest day trading or is otherwise clearly suspicious.
c) Any PRL trading that is otherwise subject to the rules governing harassment and manipulative conduct
(e.g., "spraying the market" - a number of PRL orders sent simultaneously and/or contemporaneously) is
also prohibited.

T3 has elected to implement a policy as follows for PRL trading violations:
1st Violation: Initial Written Warning
2nd Violation: A Monetary Fine of $25
Subsequent Violations: A Minimum Monetary Fine of $25 per violation, but will be increased if
determined appropriate by the CCO.

All profits derived from improper PRL transactions are subject to disgorgement. RRs/Members who are
repeat violators risk suspension or termination of their trading privileges.

15.6.14 Wash Sales
T3TG prohibits RR/Members from conducting wash sales or matched orders. Wash sales are trades of
securities without a real change in ownership of the securities traded. Matched orders are orders to buy
or sell securities that are entered with knowledge that a matching order on the opposite side has been or
will be entered. As the SEC, NYSE, ARCA and FINRA may view such activity as a potentially manipulative

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 254
 Trading                                                                                        09062023
or fraudulent device, it is incumbent on supervisors to investigate the trading, including making a
determination of any pattern, trading strategy and economic benefit. Supervisors should investigate
these exceptions and where appropriate issue a written warning (e.g., deliberate actions). An isolated
crossing trade due to a systems error would not be considered deliberate.

T3TG has elected to implement a policy as follows for Wash Sale trading violations:
1st Violation: Initial Written Warning
2nd Violation: A Monetary Fine of $25
Subsequent Violations: A Minimum Monetary Fine of $25 per violation, but will be increased if
determined appropriate by the CCO.

RR/Members who are repeat violators risk suspension or termination of their trading privileges.

15.6.15 Marking the Close
T3TG strictly forbids marking the close of any publicly traded issue. In particular, near close of the market,
T3TG’s compliance department shall look for and address any activity that may be construed by the
regulators to be "marking the close." The regulators consider this practice to be a manipulation of the
market and it carries heavy fines. Please note that sanctions apply whether such activity was negligent or
intentional. If Compliance identifies activity at or near the close, especially in thinly traded stocks with
large spreads, please alert the compliance department immediately. In addition, such findings should be
noted in each office’s daily trade reviews.

15.6.16 Price Manipulation & Unauthorized Market Making
Stocks that trade in low volume (less than 100,000) may be susceptible to trading that is manipulative in
nature. T3TG will review instances when an individual RR/Member represents ten percent (10%) or more
of the daily trading volume on a particular day for such low volume stocks. In some instances, it may be
important to review prior trading in the issue to determine whether there was a particular trading pattern
or whether the trading on a particular day was isolated in nature. Apart from issues relating to potential
manipulative conduct, any pattern of trading where the RR/Member consistently represents an
inordinate percentage of the daily trading volume may raise the specter of a requirement to register as a
market maker in the stock. In addition, holdings representing a significant percentage of the outstanding
shares in a particular issue may subject T3TG and/or the position holder to submit a regulatory filing.
Supervisors must review all instances and respond to the Compliance Department with respect to their
findings. Any unusual, suspicious or potential manipulative activity should be reported immediately to
the Compliance Department for review and determination of whether disciplinary recommendations are
warranted.

15.6.17 Origin Code Surveillance for Options
T3TG utilizes Dash Financial Technologies via its various OMS systems to route and code option orders.
T3TG has instructed Dash to code all option orders as “Firm”. If T3TG should utilize any other firm for
options execution the instructions will be the same. T3TG will supply these instructions in writing at the
time of the account opening.



 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                           Page 255
 Trading                                                                                        09062023
In order to ensure that all option orders are coded correctly, the compliance department will receive a
daily list of option transactions from all executing firms. This list will include how all orders were tagged.
The compliance department will sign off and save these lists as proof of review. Any order coded without
the “firm” identifier will be researched and corrected.

15.6.18 LLCs as Class B Members
From time-to-time and on a case-by-case basis, T3TG may accept LLC entities as Class B Members, but
only with the express written approval of the Chief Compliance Officer Steven Pikowski.
Generally speaking the LLC and its Members are subject to all the rules and procedures outlined
throughout the T3TG WSP, unless specific special exceptions are detailed herein this Section 4, as well as
all applicable FINRA and SEC Rules and Regulations.

15.6.19 Prerequisites
In order for an LLC to be considered for becoming a Class B Member of T3TG, there are several
prerequisites that must be met and are listed as follows:
            • The LLC must complete the LLC Account Pack in its entirety,
            • All Members of the LLC should also be Registered RR/Members of T3TG,
            • If available, the Previous 3 month’s bank statements must be submitted with the
                application and if approved, every month thereafter,
            • The LLC must supply their Operating Agreement, which must list all Members of the LLC
                and if approved as a Member, any updated Operating Agreements thereafter, e) The LLC
                must provide a copy of its State Certificate of formation

15.6.20 Profit Splits
The LLC as a Class B Member will be allowed to receive the profit split for those Registered RR/Members
of T3TG that are also Members of the LLC, provided they have filled out the T3TG Journal Request Form
with all applicable approvals.

15.6.21 Other LLC Prohibited Acts:
            •   The LLC will not mark up any commission rates or participate in any transaction-based
                income from its T3TG RR/Members.
            •   LLC Members cannot be Class C Members.
            •   LLCs will not be proprietary traders in and of themselves.
            •   T3TG will not accept capital contributions from the LLC and the LLC may not accept capital
                contributions from its own Members that are also Registered Proprietary Traders of T3TG.
                The LLC may only accept capital contributions from its Class A Member(s)/Managing
                Member, so long as he is also a Registered RR/Member of T3TG.

 Trading                                                                                        09062023
Officer. As a reminder, all content for the website must be approved prior to posting and the email
domains must be monitored through the T3TG’s approved surveillance systems, currently SMARSH.

15.6.23 LLC Supervision
As Registered RR/Members of T3TG, all LLC Members will automatically be assigned appropriate
Supervisors in the normal course of business. Review of monthly bank statements, communications with
the public and general business activities of the LLC will be supervised by the Chief Compliance Officer or
delegate.

15.6.24 Capital Contributions
General. Class B & C Members may make a capital contribution to T3TG. All such funds cannot be third
party funds, but must be from the Class B or C Member. To ensure that all contributions are from Class B
or C Members, T3TG does not except any checks or wires from an account that states a third-party name.
In addition,
T3TG does not accept physical cash or physical cash equivalents such as money orders, and requires all of
its Class B & C Members to sign and acknowledge that they are aware of T3TG’s policies against the receipt
of third-party funds and will abide by such terms during their registration with T3TG.

One Year Lockup on All Capital Contributions. All capital contributions by Class B & C Members shall be
subject to SEC Rule 15c3-1 Under the Rule, all capital contributions will be held for a minimum of one (1)
year from the date of contribution, and can only be withdrawn after the one-year anniversary date of the
contribution. Such conditions and terms of the Rule are set forth in the Operating Agreement. Such
treatment of capital contributions under the Rule is also set forth in the Signature Page to the T3TG
Operating / Trading Agreement, which is signed by the associated individual. A copy of the Signature Page
is retained in each RR/Member’s file maintained by T3TG.

15.6.25 Risk Management
General. Every Proprietary Trader is issued pre-determined risk management controls as part of the initial
account opening process. These limits are specifically tailored to the individual trader’s trading profile
based on a wide array of characteristics including but not limited to years of trading experience,
performance track record (specifically consistency and Profit and Loss peak to trough) and the specific
trading strategy to be implemented. Each trader has a number of pre-determined risk settings that are
put in place during the account opening process; these include:

            •   Maximum Position Size – maximum # of shares per position
            •   Maximum # of positions
            •   Maximum order size
            •   Maximum Loss – if a trader’s gross loss exceeds this limit, the trader can place closing
                orders only.
            •   Reset – this is a pre-determined limit that is less than the maximum loss setting. This
                setting allows opening orders to resume being placed if the trader’s gross loss recovers to
                less negative. Example: If the maximum loss is ($500) and reset is ($490); the trader will be
                stopped from placing opening orders if his/her gross loss exceeds ($500) and he/she would


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 257
 Trading                                                                                       09062023
              be allowed to resume placing opening orders if his gross loss recovered to better than
              ($490)
           • Buying Power
Supervisors and Risk Setting Approval Process. Supervisors confer with Class B Members, when
applicable, to make determinations of when adjustments to the pre-determined risk settings are
appropriate. These adjustment requests are emailed by the Supervisor to the Managing Member, Sean
Hendelman, and/or the Chief Operations Officer Garret Marquis, (or an approved Delegate) for approval.

Once approval has been given by Mr. Marquis or Mr. Hendelman, instructions are given to implement the
adjusted risk setting. These instructions are then emailed to the associated Third Party trading platform
for implementation from pre-approved RR/Members of T3TG. Risk Settings can also be adjusted on an
ongoing basis for that specific trading day or permanently after the request has been approved.

Risk Parameter Breaches. If a trader breaches their gross loss risk setting in a manner that falls outside of
an acceptable margin for error, this event may be flagged by Compliance or Mr. Hendelman as a failure
to act. This can result in a reduction in the trader’s risk settings, fines or termination from the Company.

T3TG reserves the right to take action at any time without notice, including, but not limited to:
          • Liquidating positions;
          • Reducing risk parameters;
          • Restricting access
          • Closing an account

Risk Management Tools:
          • Each trading platform that T3TG uses is equipped with a risk management suite of tools.
            This includes:
          • A “risk monitor” that displays both individual and grouped positions and realized and
          • unrealized profit and loss, quickly and efficiently
          • Risk settings adjustment tools to allow risk setting adjustments in real-time
          • Order Execution Interface that allows the user to place orders against any account that
            may warrant intervention
          • In addition, T3TG has built a proprietary risk management tool that allows the user to view
            traders across multiple platforms in one interface.
          • PropReports is our online account reconciliation website that is password protected and
            access is granted on authorized only user specific basis. The site allows approved
            individuals access T+1 to access previous trading day and historical activity as well as end
            balances.

Other Tools. Risk events and update are reported via email to a risk management distribution list:
Compliance@T3trading.com.

15.6.26 Rule 15c3-5 & T3TG’s Market Access
The Market Access Rule under the Exchange Act generally requires a broker-dealer with market access,
or that provides a customer/member or any other person with access to an exchange or ATS through use

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 258
 Trading                                                                                       09062023
of its MPID or otherwise, to establish, document, and maintain a system of risk management controls and
supervisory procedures reasonably designed to manage the financial, regulatory, and other risks of this
business activity.
The rule applies broadly to all forms of market access by broker-dealers that are exchange members or
ATS operators or subscribers, including sponsored access, direct market access, and more traditional
agency brokerage arrangements with customer/members, as well as proprietary trading (including
market making activities). Accordingly, the term “market access” includes all such activities. By virtue of
its requirements, the rule also prohibits broker-dealers from providing “unfiltered” or “naked” access to
an exchange or ATS. In addition, the definition of market access encompasses trading in all securities on
an exchange or ATS, including equities, options, exchange-traded funds, debt securities, security futures
and security-based swaps.

15.6.27 T3TG’s Market Access
T3TG does not have direct market access or provide any direct market access to any other party. T3TG is
not a member of any exchange. We access the market through routing and execution agreements with
Clearpool Execution Services, LLC (“Clearpool”), Instinet, LLC, Velocity and others as added from time-to-
time. Clearpool, Instinet and Velocity route our orders to the exchanges with instructions that they clear
either Velocity or ICBC, respectively.

15.6.28 Regulation M
Regulation M addresses certain activities that could be viewed as artificially impacting the price of an
offered security, It is intended to protect the integrity of the securities offering process by preventing
persons with a financial interest in a securities offering from taking particular actions that might
manipulate the market for the securities being offered.

Rule 101
Rule 101 restricts distribution participants and their affiliated purchasers from bidding for, purchasing, or
attempting to induce others to bid for or purchase, covered securities during the applicable restricted
period.

Rule 102
Rule 102 prohibits issuers and selling security holders, and their respective affiliated purchasers, from
bidding for, purchasing, or attempting to induce others to bid for or purchase, any covered security during
the applicable restricted period.

Rule 103
Rule 103 provides an exemption for the continuance of certain passive market making activities in a
security by a distribution participant during the Rule 101 restricted period if the distribution participant
is a registered market maker.

Rule 104
Rule 104 provides that it is “unlawful for any person, directly or indirectly, to stabilize, to effect any
syndicate covering transaction, or to impose a penalty bid, in connection with an offering of an y security”
except in accordance with the provisions of Rule 104.

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                          Page 259
 Trading                                                                                       09062023
Rule 105
Rule 105 prohibits any person that has sold short a security that is the subject of a registered offering
from purchasing securities in the offering from an underwriter, broker or dealer participating in the
offering if the short sale took place during a specified period prior to the pricing of the offered securities
(the pre-pricing period).

15.6.29 Regulation M and T3TG

T3TG is in the business of proprietary trading in the secondary market only and does not participate in
the offering of any security, either as a distribution participant or as an investor. Therefore, T3TG does
not currently conduct any business that would require special attention in regards to Regulation M. If the
business model of T3TG were to change, the Chief Compliance Officer would alter T3TG’s procedures to
account for those modifications.

FOREX transactions are conducted in the OTC market between customer/members and dealers. Members
who intend to trade FOREX must comply with the requirements of this policy. Questions should be
referred to Compliance.


 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                       Page 261
 Trading                                                                                    09062023
16.1.1 Qualifying Members

Member’s who engage in FOREX transactions must:

            •   Acknowledge they may lose all of their investment
            •   be provided the NFA brochure “Trading in the Off-Exchange Foreign Currency Market.

16.1.2 Risks

Trading in foreign currencies includes the following risks which the member must understand:

            •   The currency market is extremely volatile; some currencies are more volatile than others
                and members are exposed to substantial currency risk.
            •   Leverage may be at ratios of 400:1 or higher which magnifies even minor fluctuations in
                currency rates resulting in increased gains or losses.
            •   There is no central clearing organization for FOREX transactions and funds deposited for
                currency transactions are no covered by SIPC.
            •   Members may lose their entire investment.

16.1.3 FOREX Related Communications

As for all communications, FOREX communications must be fair and balanced. Advertising or sales
material must be submitted to Compliance for approval prior to use.

Communications must not be misleading regarding aspects of FOREX trading, including:

            •   The likelihood of profits or the risks of FOREX trading, including leveraged trading;
            •   The Firm’s role in or compensation from trades;
            •   The Firm’s access to the Inter-bank currency market; or
            •   The performance or accuracy of electronic trading platforms or software sold or licensed
                by or through the Firm to members in connection with FOREX trading, including falsely
                advertising claims regarding slippage rates.

FOREX communications cannot include references to SIPC since SIPC does not cover currency
transactions.

16.1.4 FINRA / NFA Requirements

FINRA has stated that currency exchange activities must be conducted consistent with NFA requirements,
some of which duplicate rules applicable to FINRA members. The following are the standards including
FINRA Regulatory Notice 08-66:

            •   Misappropriating or mishandling member funds;

 Compliance and Supervisory Procedures                                                     Page 262
 Trading                                                                                  09062023
           •   Failing to disclose the Firm is acting as a counterparty to a transaction;
           •   Failing to adequately disclose the risks associated with FOREX trading;
           •   Using, selling or leasing electronic trading platforms that allow “slippage” of trade
               executions in a manner that disproportionately or unfairly affects the member;
           •   Making post-execution price adjustments that are inappropriate and unfavorable to the
               member;
           •   Creating false books and records;
           •   Failing to adequately disclose to members the risk and terms of leveraged trading.
